# The Ganapati of Auspicious Times
<note>original: "Muhūrtagaṇapati"</note>

->Composed by Ganapati, a master of the established principles of astronomy and astrology, a treasury of all sciences, and the son of the illustrious scholar-astrologer Ravala Harishankar Suri<-

->In which<-

->Auspicious times <term>Muhurta: specific time intervals calculated for performing rituals or actions to ensure success</term> are described in easy and elegant verses for the accomplishment of worldly affairs<-

->Second Edition<-

->**Location: Lucknow**<-

->Printed at the Press of Munshi Naval Kishore<-
<note>Munshi Naval Kishore (1836–1895) was a legendary book publisher in 19th-century India, credited with preserving much of South Asia's literary heritage.</note>

->August, Year 1885 AD<-

<summary>This is the title page of the "Muhūrtagaṇapati," a treatise on electional astrology composed by Ganapati, son of Harishankar Suri. It was printed in 1885 by the famous Naval Kishore Press in Lucknow for the purpose of guiding daily life through auspicious timing.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, Ganapati, Harishankar Suri, Naval Kishore Press, Lucknow, Hindu Astrology, 19th-century printing, Jyotish</keywords>

--- Page 2 ---
<meta>This page marks the beginning of the Muhurta Ganapati, a treatise on electional astrology. It follows the traditional opening invocations and establishes the author's lineage and the mathematical formulas for calculating the Jovian year cycle.</meta>

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati 2</margin>

->**Salutations to the Holy Ganesha**<-

The eternal Lord Shiva, embraced by the daughter of the Himalayas <note>Parvati</note> as if by a golden desire-granting vine, reigns supreme; He is the very Tree of Plenty that bestows all fruits of desire. || 1 ||

I bow to the Lord who wears a garland of skulls <note>Shiva</note>, who impels the world toward the performance of sacrifices and sacred rites, and who created the *Muhurta* <note>a specific auspicious unit of time, roughly 48 minutes</note> to guide the people. || 2 ||

Having bowed to the feet of Ganesha—who is the form of the entire universe—to the feet of my Guru, to the embodiment of Sacrifice, to my parents, and to all the great sages who authored the *Samhitas* <note>traditional collections of Vedic hymns or astrological laws</note>; and having understood the foundational texts composed by Garga, Atri, Vashistha, and Angiras, and having reflected upon the *Ratnamala* <note>The "Jewel Garland," a famous astrological text</note> with its elegant verses and the various astrological compendiums; || 3 ||

And setting aside complex technical codes to provide ease of practical use for the quick enlightenment of students, I compose this work using simple and concise words based on the meanings declared in the astronomical texts to ensure the success of all undertakings. I, who am a knower of the established astronomical truths <note>Siddhanta</note>, whose intellect is proficient in the Vedas and various arts and sciences, who is honored by the lords of the Gauda region <note>modern-day Bengal/Bihar area</note>, and who is repeatedly worshipped by the foremost of kings; || 4 ||

Born from the lineage of Shri Ramadasa, the son of Shri Ravala Harishankara, endowed with humility, I, Vidyanidhi Ganapati, a knower of the traditional mathematical sciences, compose this book titled **Muhurta Ganapati**. || 5 ||

### Now, the Calculation of the Year

Take the *Shaka* era year <note>The traditional Indian calendar starting in 78 CE</note> in two places. Multiply one by 22. Then, divide it by 4281 <note>represented by the cryptic words "Sunanda-Abdhi," where Sunanda=9, Abdhi=4, etc., though the text provides the numerals 4281</note>. Add the result to the product of the Shaka year and 1875 <note>represented by "Arrows-Mountains-Elephants-Moon"</note>. Divide the sum by 60. The remainder gives the number of elapsed years in the cycle. According to the Jovian measurement <note>the 60-year cycle of Jupiter</note>, these years follow the sequence starting with *Prabhava*. || 7 ||

### Now, the Names of the Sixty Years

1. Prabhava, 2. Vibhava, 3. Shukla, 4. Pramoda, 5. Prajapati, 6. Angiras, 7. Shrimukha, 8. Bhava, 9. Yuva, 10. Dhata, 11. Ishvara... || 8 ||

...Bahudhanya (12), Pramathi (13), Vikrama (14), Vrisha (15), Chitrabhanu (16), Subhanu (17), Tarana (18), Parthiva (19), and Vyaya (20). || 8 || Sarvajit (21), Sarvadhari (22), Virodhi (23), Vikrita (24), Khara (25), Nandana (26), Vijaya (27), Jaya (28), Manmatha (29), and Durmukha (30). || 10 || Hemalambi (31), Vilambi (32), Vikari (33), Sharvari (34), Plava (35), Shubhakrit (36), Shobhana (37), Krodhi (38), Vishvavasu (39), and Parabhava (40). || 11 || Plavanga (41), Kilaka (42), Saumya (43), Sadharana (44), Virodhakrit (45), Paridhavi (46), Pramadi (47), Ananda (48), Rakshasa (49), and Anala (50). || 12 || Pingala (51), Kalayukta (52), Siddharthi (53), Raudra (54), Durmati (55), Dundubhi (56), Rudhirodgari (57), Raktaksha (58), Krodhana (59), and Kshaya (60). || 13 ||

These sixty years <term>Samvatsara; the sixty-year Jovian cycle</term> have been proclaimed in their proper order. The wise should understand that the results or omens of these years correspond to their specific names. || 14 ||

### Calculation of the Year South of the Reva River
Add twelve to the Shaka era year <note>The Shaka era is a historical Hindu calendar epoch starting in 78 CE</note> and divide by sixty (60); the remainder is the current year. This is the method remembered by the learned for those living in the region south of the Reva <note>The Narmada River</note>. || 15 ||

### Calculation North of the Narmada
Add eighty-nine (89) to the Shaka year <insert>and divide by sixty</insert> for the region north of the Narmada. This is called the "Jovian" year <note>original: "Jaiva"</note>, determined by the transit of Jupiter <note>original: "Vachaspati"</note> through the signs of the zodiac. || 16 ||

### The Beginning of the Prabhava Year
When Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> rises in the first quarter of the lunar mansion Dhanishtha in the month of Magha <note>roughly January/February</note>, then the foremost and most excellent year, Prabhava, begins. || 17 ||

### The Designations of the Eras within a Five-Year Cycle
In the beginning of a cycle <term>Yuga; here referring to a 5-year sub-cycle</term>, the first year is known as "Samvatsara," and its presiding deity is Fire <note>original: "Anala"</note>. The second year is called "Parivatsara," and the Sun <note>original: "Bhanu"</note> is declared its deity. || 18 || Ida...

--- Page 4 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the Hindu calendar (Panchanga), specifically detailing the five-year cycle of the Yuga and the divisions of the solar year into solstices, seasons, and months.</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>

<margin>Su. Ga. 4</margin>The third year is named Vatsara, and its presiding deity is the Moon. The fourth is called Anuvatsara, and its deity is said to be Prajapati <note>The Creator-God</note>. || 18 || Likewise, the fifth year is named Vatsara <note>specifically Idavatsara in other traditions</note>, and its deity is Gauri <note>The Goddess</note>. A Yuga <note>a small cycle of time</note> is completed by these five years, and sixty years are completed by twelve such Yugas. || 20 ||

The lords of these twelve Yugas are remembered in order as: Agni <note>Fire</note>, Jiva <note>Jupiter</note>, Indra, Pavaka <note>Purifier/Fire</note>, Tvashtr <note>The Celestial Architect</note>, Ahirbudhnya <note>The Serpent of the Deep</note>, the Pitris <note>Ancestors</note>, the Vishvedevas <note>Universal Gods</note>, the Moon, Puruhuta <note>Indra</note> and Anala <note>Fire</note>, the Dasras <note>The Ashvini twins</note>, and Bhaga <note>The Dispenser</note>. || 21 ||

### The Definition of the Solstices (Ayana)

The three signs beginning with Capricorn <note>original: Makara</note> are known as the Northern Transit <note>original: Uttarayana</note>. That period is the "Day of the Gods," and auspicious work is highly praised during this time. || 22 || That which begins with Cancer <note>original: Karkata</note> is known as the Southern Transit <note>original: Dakshinayana</note> of the Sun. That is the "Night of the Gods," and it is said that work initiated here does not reach fulfillment. || 23 || The Northern Hemisphere begins with Aries <note>original: Mesha</note>, while the Southern is named from Libra <note>original: Ghata</note> onwards. || 24 ||

### The Definition of the Seasons (Ritu)

Spring, Summer, the Rainy Season, Autumn, Early Winter, and Late Winter—these are remembered as the six seasons. || 25 || When the Sun is in Pisces and Aries, it is declared to be Spring <note>original: Vasanta</note>. In Taurus and Gemini, it is Summer <note>original: Grishma</note>. In Cancer and Leo, it is the Rainy Season <note>original: Varsha</note>. || 26 || In Virgo and Libra, the season is called Autumn <note>original: Sharad</note>. Early Winter <note>original: Hemanta</note> is in Scorpio and Sagittarius, and Late Winter <note>original: Shishira</note> is in Capricorn and Aquarius. || 27 ||

### The Definition of the Months (Masa)

The months are Chaitra, then Vaishakha, Jyeshtha, and the one named Ashadha. Then Shravana, Bhadrapada, and the one named Ashvina. || 28 || Kartika, Margashirsha, Pausha, Magha, and Phalguna. || 29 ||

### The Archaic Names of the Months from Chaitra onwards

1. Madhu, 2. Madhava, 3. Shukra, 4. Shuchi, 5. Nabhas, 6. Nabhasya, 7. Isha, 8. Urja, <margin>4</margin> 9. Sahas, 10. Sahasya, || 30 || 11. Tapas, and 12. Tapasya. These are the names of the months in order. A month ends with the New Moon <note>original: Amanta system</note>...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of timekeeping systems from the previous section, transitioning from the definitions of months to the specific properties of lunar days (tithis).</meta>

<page-num>5</page-num>
<header>Mu. Ga. 5</header>

...the Lunar, the Solar, and the month determined by the sun's entry into a zodiac sign <note>Sankranti</note>. || 31 || The Civil month <term>Savana</term> consists of thirty days; the Sidereal month <term>Nakshatra</term> is measured by the moon's passage through the constellations. These months are established for specific purposes. || The learned should always recognize these months: || 32 || The Solar month is used for poetic works, marriages, and determining the planetary days. The Civil month is used for calculating the term of pregnancy and medical treatments. The Sidereal month is used for observing the formation of rain clouds. || 33 || Vows, sacrifices, and similar rituals are calculated using the Lunar month. The Lunar month is of two types: ending with the new moon <term>Amanta</term> or ending with the full moon <term>Purnimanta</term>. || 34 || The month ending with the full moon is preferred for rites concerning the gods, while the month ending with the new moon is for rites concerning the ancestors. || 35 || **Now, the definition of the Fortnights:** There are two fortnights, the Bright <term>Shukla</term> and the Dark <term>Krishna</term>, used for divine and ancestral rites respectively. All auspicious activities are praised when performed in the Bright fortnight. || 36 || Thus ends the chapter on the Year and related divisions in the *Muhurta Ganapati*, composed by Ganapati, the son of the illustrious astrologer Ravala Harishankara Suri. || 1 ||

### Now, the Chapter on Lunar Days (Tithis)

The first day <note>Pratipat</note>, the second, the third, and then the fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh, and eighth; || 1 || the ninth, tenth, eleventh, twelfth, thirteenth, and then the fourteenth is known. || 2 || The full moon day <note>Purnima</note> is the end of the Bright fortnight, while the new moon day <note>Amavasya</note> is recorded in the Dark fortnight. The presiding deities of these days are: Fire, the Creator <note>Brahma</note>, the Serpent <note>Gauri/Shiva</note>, Ganesha, the Serpent-King, Yama, and the Sun; || 3 || Shiva, the Mother Goddesses, the Sun, Cupid, the All-gods, Vishnu, the God of Desire, and the Moon. For the new moon day, the Ancestors are proclaimed as the presiding lords. || 4 || The lunar days, starting from the first, are categorized as Joyous <term>Nanda</term>, Prosperous <term>Bhadra</term>, Victorious <term>Jaya</term>, Empty <term>Rikta</term>, and Full <term>Purna</term>. This sequence repeats every five days, starting again from the sixth and the eleventh. || 5 || In the Bright fortnight, the three groups of five lunar days are considered respectively: mediocre, auspicious, and most auspicious. In the Dark fortnight, they are considered auspicious, mediocre, and undesirable for good works. || 6 || **Now, the Nanda and other categories...**

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Muhurta (electional astrology), specifically detailing the activities suitable for the five categories of lunar days and identifying which dates are generally auspicious or inauspicious for human endeavors.</meta>

<page-num>6</page-num>

<margin>Muhurta-Grantha 6</margin> **Prescribed Actions**

Singing, dancing, as well as matters concerning fields <note>agriculture or land acquisition</note>, painting, festivals, and the construction of houses; the wearing of new clothes and ornaments, and various crafts—these are considered auspicious when performed on **Nanda** <note>the "Joyous"</note> lunar days. || 7 ||

Marriage, the thread ceremony <note>Upanayana, a rite of passage for students</note>, travel, jewelry making, and artistic works; activities involving elephants, horses, and chariots—these bring success when performed on **Bhadra** <note>the "Lucky"</note> lunar days. || 8 ||

Military affairs, battle, weaponry, travel, festivals, and house-building; medicine and commerce—these are successfully accomplished on **Jaya** <note>the "Victorious"</note> lunar days. || 9 ||

The imprisonment of enemies, the use of poisons, weapons, and fire should be carried out on **Rikta** <note>the "Empty"</note> lunar days; however, one should never perform any auspicious or benevolent ceremony on these days. || 10 ||

Vows, marriage, travel, royal coronations, and rituals for peace or nourishment—these are indeed fulfilled on **Purna** <note>the "Full"</note> lunar days. || 11 ||

**Now, the generally auspicious and inauspicious lunar days are described:**

The second, fifth, third, and seventh; the tenth, eleventh, <unclear>prescribed</unclear> first, and the thirteenth— || 12 ||

And the Full Moon—these lunar days are conducive to success in all undertakings. Other days are generally not desired, though some authorities consider them auspicious in specific ritual contexts. || 13 ||

The fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, the first day of the bright fortnight, and the New Moon day—one should make an effort to avoid these for auspicious works. || 14 ||

**Now, the <term>Paksha-randhra</term> <note>literally "clefts of the fortnight," these are specific lunar days considered volatile or "pierced" and thus generally avoided for major beginnings</note> lunar days:**

The fourth, sixth, eighth, ninth, twelfth, and fourteenth—these lunar days are known as **Paksha-randhra** and should be abandoned in all ceremonies. || 15 ||

In these specific dates—the 8th, 9th, 14th, 25th <note>likely referring to the 10th of the dark fortnight</note>, 10th, and 5th—certain portions of the day are particularly harmful. One should avoid the beginning portions as prescribed, while the remaining parts are considered good. || 16 ||

**Now, the lunar days known as Inauspicious:**

For the four signs starting with Aries... <margin>6</margin>

Then, starting from the first day of the lunar fortnight... the fifth day is declared as the completion of that group of four. // 17 // For Leo, Virgo, Libra, and Scorpio, the lunar days begin from the sixth in sequence. The tenth day is the completion for that group of four. Similarly, starting from the eleventh day // 18 // for the four signs beginning with Sagittarius, the Full Moon <note>original: "Pūrṇā"</note> is the median. A lunar day associated with a zodiac sign occupied by a malefic planet is not auspicious. // 19 //

### Now, the specific activities for each lunar day:

Marriage ceremonies, festivals, journeys, consecrations <note>term: Pratiṣṭhā; the ritual of "breathing life" into a temple deity</note>, and architectural work <note>term: Vāstu; the science of sacred architecture</note>—one should perform the tonsure ceremony <note>term: Caula; the first ritual haircut of a child</note> and such on the first lunar day of the dark fortnight, but not on the first day of the bright fortnight. // 20 //

State affairs, marriage, and other auspicious ceremonies, architecture, and the thread ceremony <note>term: Vratabandha; the initiation ritual for students</note> and consecrations are recommended on the second lunar day. // 21 //

The first feeding of solid food to a child <note>term: Annaprāśana</note>, music, education, the parting of the hair ceremony <note>term: Sīmānta; a ritual for pregnant women</note>, and craftsmanship—everything prescribed for the second day is also praised on the third day. // 22 //

The killing or binding of enemies, and the use of poison, weapons, or fire should be performed on the fourth day; however, auspicious ceremonies should never be done then. // 23 //

All auspicious deeds, whether stable or moving, reach great success on the fifth day, with the exception of taking out loans. // 24 //

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the systematic listing of auspicious activities for each lunar day (tithi), following the previous page's discussion of the first through sixth days. The text maintains a prescriptive tone for daily Vedic life.</meta>

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati 8</margin>
->**The Seventh Day**<-
Activities involving elephants, marriage ceremonies, music, and the acquisition of fine clothing; travel, entering a new home, and battles—all these shall meet with success on the **seventh lunar day** <note>Saptami</note>. (26)

<margin>8</margin>
->**The Eighth Day**<-
Dancing, matters concerning women, gems, and ornaments; battles and the bearing of weapons; and works of architecture or craftsmanship—these tasks shall attain perfection on the **eighth day** <note>Ashtami</note>. (27)

->**The Ninth Day**<-
Confrontation, quarrels, gambling, and the use of intoxicants; hunting, and actions involving poisons, fire, or weapons—these shall find success on the **ninth day** <note>Navami; this day is traditionally considered "fierce" or "cruel" and thus suited for such activities</note>. (28)

->**The Tenth Day**<-
Auspicious works such as marriage, the wearing of ornaments, travel, and entering a home; and matters concerning elephants, horses, or royalty—these are accomplished on the **tenth day** <note>Dashami</note>. (29)

->**The Eleventh Day**<-
The thread ceremony <note>Vratabandha; the initiation into Vedic study</note>, marriage, dealing with groups, craftsmanship, and festivals for the gods; coming and going, and putting on decorations—these are praised on the **eleventh day** <note>Ekadashi</note>. (30)

->**The Twelfth Day**<-
Activities of both a moving and fixed nature, marriage, and the thread ceremony should be performed on the **twelfth day** <note>Dwadashi</note>; however, one should avoid oil massages and travel. (31)

->**The Thirteenth Day**<-
Travel, entering a home, battle, and the donning of clothes and ornaments are auspicious; with the exception of the thread ceremony, other auspicious acts are suited to the **thirteenth day** <note>Trayodashi</note>. (32)

->**The Fourteenth Day**<-
One should perform "cruel" deeds involving poison, binding, fire, and weapons on the **fourteenth day** <note>Chaturdashi</note>; avoid auspicious works, shaving <note>Kshauram</note>, and travel. (33)

->**The Full Moon**<-
Marriage and auspicious rites, ornaments, craftsmanship, the consecration of idols <note>Pratishtha</note>, and sacrificial rituals; battle and household tasks—all are successfully achieved on the **Full Moon day** <note>Purnima</note>. (34)

->**The New Moon**<-
The establishment of the sacred fire, great acts of charity, and rituals for the ancestors <note>Pitri-yaga</note>—these stated deeds should be performed; other auspicious actions are not recommended on the **New Moon** <note>Darsha/Amavasya</note>. (35)

### On the Forbidden Days for Cleaning Teeth
<margin>8</margin>
On the New Moon, the sixth day, the eleventh day, and on the day of the Sun <note>Sunday</note>; and on the first day of the lunar fortnight after the first watch—on these days, one should not perform **dental hygiene** <note>Danta-dhavana; traditionally using a fresh twig</note>. (36)

### On the Forbidden Days for Bathing with Amla
On the tenth, seventh, new moon, second, and ninth days <insert>one should avoid bathing with Amla fruit</insert>.

On the thirteenth lunar day, a man should likewise never bathe with myrobalan fruits <note>original: "āmalakaiḥ"; the Indian gooseberry, often used in ritual bathing but forbidden on certain days</note>. || 37 || **Now, activities prohibited on specific days:** <margin>1</margin> One should avoid shaving on the fourteenth day, oil massage on the sixth, meat consumption on the eighth, and sexual intercourse on the New Moon day, if one desires a long life. || 38 || **Now, items to be avoided during the sixteen lunar days from the first to the New Moon:** One should avoid ash gourd on the first day, and similarly the Indian nightshade fruit <note>original: "vṛhatī phalam"</note>, salt, radish, and again the use of oil <note>on the respective following days</note>. || 39 || <term>Myrobalan</term> <note>the fruit of the Emblic Myrobalan tree</note>, coconut, bottle gourd, pointed gourd, neem leaves, lentils, eggplant, and honey should be avoided in that order <note>referring to the sequence of lunar days</note>. || 40 || On the New Moon, avoid sexual intercourse, and on the Full Moon, avoid gambling. || 41 || **Now, in this context, the lunar days marking the start of the World Ages (Yugadi) and the Eras of Manu (Manvadi):** The bright ninth of the month of Kartika, the bright third of Vaishakha, the dark thirteenth of Ashvina, and the New Moon of Phalguna. || 42 || The <term>Krita Yuga</term> <note>the Golden Age</note> began in Kartika; the <term>Treta Yuga</term> began in Madhava <note>the month of Vaishakha</note>; the <term>Dvapara Yuga</term> began in Phalguna; and the <term>Kali Yuga</term> <note>the current Iron Age</note> began in Ashvina. || 43 || The presiding deities of these ages are, in order: Vishnu, Hari, Brahma, and the Ancestors. On these Age-anniversaries, one should give gifts of sesame, salt, gold, and cows. || 44 || These Age-anniversaries should be avoided for auspicious ceremonies by the wise; however, the <term>Akshaya Tritiya</term> <note>the "Inexhaustible Third" in the month of Vaishakha</note> is excellent for all undertakings. || 45 || The bright ninth of Ashvina, the seventh of Magha, the third of Bhadrapada and Chaitra, and the twelfth of Kartika; || 46 || <margin>H</margin> the tenth of Ashadha, and the Full Moon of Jyeshtha; the Full Moon days of Ashadha, Phalguna, Chaitra, and Kartika; || 47 || the dark eighth of Bhadrapada, and the bright eleventh of Pausha, and the New Moon of Bhadrapada—these are the lunar days marking the start of the <term>Manvantaras</term> <note>the administrative periods of the various Manus, the progenitors of humanity</note>.

<summary>This page outlines dietary and behavioral prohibitions for specific lunar days and lists the significant dates marking the beginnings of the four World Ages (Yugas) and the various Eras of Manu (Manvantaras).</summary>

<page-num>10</page-num>
<header>M. G. 10</header>

...these are the ones. || 48 || **Now, the months are understood by the best of astronomers to be like wine and a beloved woman.** <note>This metaphorical comparison likely refers to the intoxicating or captivating nature of seasonal changes or specific lunar months.</note> || 49 || **Now, the Alignment of the Half-Rise (Ardhodya):** In the month of Magha <note>January/February</note>, during the new moon of the bright fortnight, if the Yoga is Vyatipata and the moon is in the Shravana constellation, it is called the Ardhodya alignment. It is considered a hundred times more merit-yielding than a solar eclipse. **Now, the Great Rise (Mahodaya) Alignment:** If the conditions are slightly less than those of the Ardhodya, it is called Mahodaya. || 50 || **Now, the Elephant's Shadow (Gajacchaya):** During the fortnight of the ancestors <note>Pitru Paksha</note>, on the thirteenth lunar day, when the sun is in the Hasta constellation and the moon is in Magha, the alignment known as the Elephant's Shadow occurs. Performing ancestral rites (Shraddha) during this time grants inexhaustible rewards. || 51 || **Now, the Kapila Sixth:** In the month of Ashvina <note>September/October</note>, during the dark fortnight on the sixth lunar day, if it falls on a Tuesday with the Rohini constellation and the Vyatipata Yoga, that Sixth is called Kapila, which bestows infinite merit. || 52 || **Now, in this context, the Rising of Agastya:** Multiply the equinoctial shadow <term>Palabha: the shadow of a gnomon at noon on the equinox</term> by eight, and add the result to the number of degrees represented by the "Vasus" (8) and the "Planets" (9) <note>original: "Vasugrahah", meaning 98 degrees</note>. At that degree of the sun, the rising of the Sage born from a pitcher <note>Agastya/Canopus</note> is known to occur. || 53 ||

Thus ends the Chapter on Lunar Days (Tithis) in the *Muhurta Ganapati*, composed by Ganapati, the son of the astrologer Ravala Hari Shankara Hari. || 2 ||

**Now begins the Chapter on Weekdays (Varas):**
The Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn—these are proclaimed to be the weekdays. || 1 || Shiva, Durga, Guha <note>Kartikeya</note>, Vishnu, Brahma, Indra, and Kaala <note>Time/Yama</note> are proclaimed as the respective presiding deities of the Sun and the other planets in order. || 2 || Jupiter, Moon, Mercury, and Venus are considered auspicious days for auspicious works. The cruel planets—Mars, Sun, and Saturn—should be chosen for harsh or aggressive deeds. || 3 || The Sun is steady, the Moon and Mars are fierce, Mercury is neutral, Jupiter is light/swift, Venus is soft/gentle, and Saturn is sharp/intense; thus it is said. || 4 || **Now, the specific activities for each weekday:**

An action is auspicious when performed on the day of a planet situated in an "Upachaya" <note>term: Upachaya (houses of increase/growth in a horoscope)</note> position. Now, for the purpose of determining the specific duties of the moment, the **Hora** <note>term: Hora (an hour-long duration ruled by a specific planet)</note> is explained:
Take the distance in "Yojanas" <note>term: Yojana (a traditional unit of distance, approx. 8 miles)</note> between one's own location and the central meridian <note>original: "madhya rekha"</note>, subtract one quarter, and with that many "Palas" <note>term: Pala (a unit of time, 1/60th of a Ghatika)</note>, add or subtract from the 15 "Tithis" <note>term: Tithi (lunar day)</note> depending on whether one is to the West or East. || 13 ||
By the "Palas" representing the difference from midday, whether more or less than midday (21), the entry of the day's ruler is calculated sequentially from sunrise. || 14 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for time-keeping and daily rituals from the Muhurta Ganapati. It concludes the section on planetary hours and the effects of oil massages before transitioning into the catalog of lunar mansions.</meta>

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati Page 12</margin> The hours <note>Sanskrit: *hora*, a unit of time roughly equal to one hour</note> pass in sequence starting from the lord of the day. || 15 || The Sun, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, Saturn, Jupiter, and then Mars: in this specific order, the planetary lords of the **Time-Hours** are to be understood. || 16 || Whatever task has been declared by the ancient teachers as suitable for a specific day, that task will surely find success if performed during the corresponding hour of that planet. || 17 || Those defects <note>Sanskrit: *dosha*, or astrological flaws</note> which are said to exist during the day do not have power at night; however, some authorities say this rule specifically applies to the days of Saturn, Mars, and the Sun. || 18 ||

->**The Results of Oil Massage on Specific Days**<-

On the Sun's day <note>Sunday</note>, oil massage causes heat; on the Moon's day <note>Monday</note>, beauty; on Mars's day <note>Tuesday</note>, death; on Mercury's day <note>Wednesday</note>, prosperity. || 19 || On Jupiter's day <note>Thursday</note>, loss of wealth; on Venus's day <note>Friday</note>, misfortune; and on Saturn's day <note>Saturday</note>, happiness. || 20 || This is the effect of oil massage on men; of this, there is no doubt. One should not perform an oil massage on the days of the Sun or Mars, nor during the *Vyatipata* or *Vaidhriti* <note>specific inauspicious astronomical yogas</note>, nor during a solar transit <note>Sankranti</note>. || 21 || Nor should it be done on the sixth or eighth lunar days, during the *Vishti* period, or on festival days.

->**Exceptions to the Defects of Oil Massage**<-

An oil massage causes no defect for one who performs it every single day, or during a festival, or for the treatment of "wind" diseases <note>Sanskrit: *vata-roga*, such as arthritis or nerve pain</note>, or when specifically prescribed. || 22 || By placing cow dung in the oil on Friday, earth on Tuesday, *Durva* grass on Thursday, and a flower on Sunday, the oil massage becomes free of defect. || 23 || Oil that has been consecrated with mantras, or is scented with flowers, or mixed with other substances, never causes any astrological defect. || 24 ||

**Thus ends the Chapter on Days in the *Muhurta Ganapati* composed by Ganapati, son of the illustrious astrologer Ravala Harishankara Suri.**

### Now, the Lords of the Lunar Mansions:
Ashwini has the <term>Dasras</term><note>The Ashwini Kumaras, divine twin physicians</note> as its deity; Bharani has Yama as its deity. Krittika is said to belong to Agni <note>the Fire god</note>; the Creator <note>Brahma</note> is the lord of Rohini. || 5 || The lord of Mrigashirsha is the Moon; likewise, the lord of Ardra is Shiva. Aditi is the protector of the two Punarvasus; the Lord of Speech <note>Brihaspati/Jupiter</note> is the lord of Pushya. || 6 || The lords of Ashlesha are the Serpents; the <term>Pitris</term><note>Ancestral spirits</note> are remembered as the lords of Magha. Bhaga is the lord of Purvaphalguni; Aryaman is the lord of the latter one <note>Uttaraphalguni</note>. || 7 || The lord of Hasta is the Sun, and Tvashtar is the lord of the one named Chitra. The deity of Swati is Vayu <note>the Wind</note>; the deities of Vishakha are Indra and Agni. || 8 || The lord of Anuradha is Mitra; Indra is said to be the lord of Jyeshtha. The deity of Mula is the <term>Rakshasa</term><note>Nirriti, the deity of destruction</note>; the lord of Purvashadha is Water <note>Apah</note>. || 9 || The deity of the latter one <note>Uttarashadha</note> is the Vishvedevas; Vidhi <note>Brahma</note> is the lord of Abhijit. The lord of Shravana is Vishnu; the Vasus are the deities of Dhanishtha. || 10 || Varuna is the lord of Shatatara; Ajaikapada is said to be the lord of the first <note>Purvabhadrapada</note>. Ahirbudhnya is the lord of the second <note>Uttarabhadrapada</note>, and Pushan is stated to be the lord of Revati. || 11 ||

### Now, the specific actions for each lunar mansion:

In **Ashvini**, one should perform journeys, administer medicine, put on ornaments, pursue education, crafts, and arts. Work involving elephants, marriage rites, and auspicious ceremonies should also be conducted. (16)

In **Bharani**, one should perform daring or violent acts, the destruction of enemies, the use of poisons, and binding. The digging of wells and activities involving fire should also be carried out. (17)

In **Krittika**, one should always perform daring acts and the maintenance of sacred fires <note>Agni-parigraha</note>. The slaying of enemies, marriage, and works involving iron or fire-drills <note>Arani</note> are also done. (18)

In **Rohini**, one should perform nourishing rites <term>Paushtika: rituals intended to increase wealth, health, or prosperity</term>, engage in debates, and the collection of wealth. Building palaces, divine service, auspicious ceremonies, and wearing ornaments are favored. (19)

Marriage, the thread ceremony <note>Upanayana</note>, and journeys are auspicious in **Mrigashirsha**. The installation of deities, architecture, and work in the fields or with water will meet with success. (20)

In **Ardra**, one should engage in conflict, killing, cutting, and slaughter. The use of poison, treaties, fire-rituals, and fierce acts of exorcism <note>Uchhatana</note> are likewise performed. (21)

Pacification rites <term>Shantika: rituals to avert evil or misfortune</term>, nourishing rites, journeys, and matters concerning land, architecture, or vows should be done. Work with vehicles, agriculture, and education are prescribed in **Punarvasu**. (22)

Actions suited for both moving and fixed signs, pacification rites, nourishing rituals, and festivals—except for marriage—all other tasks should be performed in the mansion of **Pushya**. (23)

In **Ashlesha**, one should carry out the destruction of enemies, commerce, and daring acts. Deeds involving poison or snakes, theft, and deceitful conduct should be practiced. (24) <margin>14</margin>

In **Magha**, one should perform ancestral rites <note>Paitruka</note>, as well as the planting of crops. Building reservoirs, marriage, and daring acts like war should be undertaken. (25)

### In Praise of Pushya

Even if afflicted by malefic planets, or joined with them, or lacking strength, if the Moon and the stars are favorable, all auspicious works succeed when performed under **Pushya**. || 43 ||

### On the Special Classifications of Stars and Their Activities

The three "Uttara" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> and **Rohini** are known as **Fixed** or **Steady** <term>Dhruva/Sthira: stars used for long-term stability</term>. In these, one should perform lasting actions such as building temples or villages and consecration ceremonies. || 44 || **Mrigashirsha**, **Chitra**, **Anuradha**, and **Revati** are **Soft** and **Friendly** <term>Mridu/Maitra: stars used for gentle or social activities</term>. There, one should engage in music, intimacy, ornamentation, auspicious rites, and wearing new clothes. || 45 || **Pushya**, **Ashwini**, **Abhijit**,

### Now, the Downward, Upward, and Sideways-facing Nakshatras and their respective tasks.

Mula, Ashlesha, Magha, the Purvas <note>Purvaphalguni, Purvashadha, and Purvabhadrapada</note>, Vishakha, and Bharani—these two <note>referring to the grouping</note> are the Downward-facing <term>Adhomukha: constellations whose influence is directed toward the earth</term> stars. Success in work is achieved here through downward actions. || 51 || The construction of step-wells, wells, ponds, and excavations is ordained there. So too are the burying of treasures, the entering of holes or underground passages, and mathematical calculations. || 52 ||

Punarvasu, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, the three beginning with Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, Swati</note>, and Revati—these two <note>referring to the remaining set</note> are Sideways-facing <term>Tiryanmukha: constellations whose influence is horizontal</term>. In these, the following actions are performed: || 53 || The movement and training of elephants, horses, chariots, and machines, as well as the sailing of boats. The use of palanquins, plows, and mechanical tools is auspicious in the Sideways-facing stars. || 54 ||

The Uttaras <note>Uttaraphalguni, Uttarashadha, Uttarabhadrapada</note>, Rohini, Pushya, Ardra, and the three beginning with Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha</note>—these are the Upward-facing <term>Urdhvamukha: constellations whose influence is directed toward the sky</term> stars. Wise men state that actions of a similar nature should be done here. || 55 || The building of palaces, hoisting flags and umbrellas, constructing ramparts, houses, and arched gateways; the consecration of kings, planting gardens, and festivals—these are for the Upward-facing stars. || 56 ||

...resembling the vulva <note>original: "bhagasannibha"; likely referring to the previous description of the constellation Purva Phalguni</note>. The Krittikas <note>the Pleiades</note> are four-sided in form, while Rohini <note>Aldebaran</note> has the appearance of a cart. || 59 || Mrigashira <note>Orion's head</note> is shaped like the head of a deer, and Ardra <note>Betelgeuse</note> resembles a shining gem. Punarvasu <note>Castor and Pollux</note> is shaped like a house, and Pushya <note>in Cancer</note> has the form of an arrow. || 60 || Ashlesha is shaped like a wheel, and Magha is remembered as being like a palatial hall. Svati <note>Arcturus</note> is like a piece of coral, and Vishakha is shaped like a triumphal archway. || 62 || Anuradha is like a sacrificial offering-garland <note>original: "vali"; can also mean a fold of skin or a circular pattern</note>, and Jyeshtha resembles an earring. Mula is like the tail of a lion, and Purvashadha is like a couch. || 63 || Uttarashadha appears like the tusk of an elephant, and Abhijit <note>Vega</note> is remembered as a triangle. Shravana has the form of three footsteps, and Dhanishtha is shaped like a drum. || 64 || Shatabhisha is circular in form, and Purvabhadrapada appears like a twin-bed <note>original: "yamabha"</note>. Uttarabhadrapada is shaped like a bed, and Revati is like a drum. || 65 || Now, the <term>celestial latitude of the stars</term> <note>original: "Nakshatra-shara"; refers to the angular distance of a star north or south of the ecliptic</note>. Purvashadha (both parts), Mula, Anuradha, Ardra, Mrigashira, and Hasta; || 66 || Jyeshtha, and Punarvasu—the stars of these are always situated to the South. Rohini, Krittika, Ashlesha, Revati, the three stars of Shravana, || 67 || Pushya, and Chitra—these nine are the stars that move in the middle <note>near the ecliptic</note>. Ashvini (both), Svati, Vishakha, the two Phalgunis, || 68 || Magha, and the two Bhadrapadas—these are situated to the North. Now, the knowledge of the rising sign when a star is at the mid-heaven: || 69 ||
<margin>18</margin> When the three stars beginning with Vishakha, or Purvashadha, Ashlesha, or Ardra, or Rohini are in the mid-heaven, then the eighth sign from them is rising. || 69 || When the ninth star <note>from a given point</note>, or Mula, or Mrigashira are in the middle of the sky, the seventh sign from them will be rising; for the remaining stars in the mid-heaven, the seventh sign rises. || 70 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical astronomical and astrological instructions from the Muhurta Ganapati, focusing on calculating the time elapsed in the night and identifying auspicious/inauspicious periods.</meta>

# Muhurta Ganapati - Page 26

### Now, the knowledge of the night's duration based on the Sun's position, the Meridian, and the Rising Sign.

Subtract one from the Sun’s sign <note>The zodiac sign where the sun is currently positioned</note>, subtract eight from the sign at the meridian <note>The sign currently crossing the observer's celestial meridian</note>, and subtract fifteen from the rising sign. Alternatively, divide by twenty <note>original: "vanarva"</note> according to the treatises. || 71 || The result gives the ghatis <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> passed in the night, and the remainder gives the fractions of time. || 72 ||

**Or alternatively:**
Subtract fifteen from the Sun’s sign relative to the setting sign, subtract eight from the meridian sign, and subtract one from the rising sign. Divide these by twenty. || 73 || The result should be known in terms of planets and signs, and the remainder provides the precise calculation of the night. Subtract seven, eight, or nine according to the standards of setting and rising. This specific calculation for the meridian should be understood and applied by the wise. || 74 ||

### Now, the Five-fold Prohibition (Panchaka)

A wise person should avoid the five constellations <term>Nakshatras: lunar mansions used in Vedic astrology</term> starting from Dhanishta <note>Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, and Revati</note> for the following activities: traveling toward the south, preparing a bed or canopy, roofing a house, || 75 || erecting a pillar, cremating a corpse, and collecting grass or wood. In these five, the effects of birth, gain, death, or departure are multiplied fivefold. || 76 ||

### Now, the Classifications of Constellations: Blind and Others

The constellations are categorized as Blind, Dull-eyed, Middle-eyed, and Clear-eyed. These groups repeat in cycles of seven, starting from the constellation Rohini. || 77 ||
In a "Blind" constellation, there is swift gain; in a "Dull-eyed" one, the gain is fleeting. In a "Middle-eyed" constellation, the gain is like grain <note>modest or average</note>, but in a "Clear-eyed" constellation, there is no gain at all. || 78 ||
If an object is lost during a "Blind" constellation, it has gone East; if "Dull-eyed," it is in the South. If "Middle-eyed," it is in the West; and if "Clear-eyed," it is in the North. || 79 ||

### Now, the Fifteen Muhurtas of Day and Night

The fifteen Muhurtas <term>Muhurta: a period of 48 minutes</term> are presided over by: Shiva, Ahirbudhnya, Mitra, the Ancestors, the Vasus, the Waters, the Universal Gods, Brahma, || 80 || Vidhi, Indra, Indra-Agni, the Rakshasa, Varuna, Aryaman, and Bhaga.

<margin>15</margin>
<margin>26</margin>

...thus are declared the fifteen divisions of the day in sequence. The night-time <term>Muhurtas</term><note>A Muhurta is a unit of time lasting approximately 48 minutes.</note> begin with Shiva, Jaya, and Ahirbudhnya. || 81 ||

<note>The following verses list the presiding deities of these time segments.</note>
Dhatri, Swati, Aditi, Jiva, Chitra, Tvashtri, and Maruta. A Muhurta is defined as one-fifteenth part of the duration of the day, and similarly for the night. || 82 ||
One should not perform any desired action during a time governed by a lord <note>deity</note> that is inimical to the purpose. However, during the midday period known as <term>Abhijit</term>, one may perform all auspicious works even if other faults are present, || 83 || except for traveling toward the southern direction. || 84 ||

**Now, the Muhurtas to be avoided on Sunday and other days:**
On Sunday, avoid Aryaman and Bhaga. On Monday, avoid Vidhi and Rakshasa. On Tuesday, avoid Pitrya and Agni. On Wednesday, avoid Abhijit. || 85 ||
On Thursday, avoid Rakshasa and Sarpa. On Friday, avoid Pitrya and Brahmi. On Saturday, avoid Raudra and Sarpa. These specific Muhurtas must be abandoned for auspicious works. || 86 ||

Thus ends the Chapter on <term>Nakshatras</term><note>Lunar Mansions</note> in the *Muhurta Ganapati*, composed by Ganapati, the son of Hari and grandson of the illustrious astrologer Ravala Harishankara. || 4 ||

->...Brahma, Indra, and Vaidhriti. The fruits of these Yogas <note>Yoga: one of the five elements of the Hindu calendar, representing the relationship between the sun and moon</note> should be understood as corresponding to their names. (3)<-

**Now, the portions to be avoided in the inauspicious Yogas:**
One should avoid the entirety of the Yogas named Vaidhriti and Vyatipada for any auspicious activity. In Vajra and Vishkambha, avoid the first three *ghatikas* <note>A ghatika is a unit of time equal to 24 minutes; three ghatikas equal 72 minutes</note>. (4) Avoid the first half of Parigha; nine ghatikas in Shula and Vyaghata; and six ghatikas in Ganda and Atiganda. These are to be avoided in all rituals. (5) This is the general rule remembered for these Yogas. Some authorities, however, simply discard the first quarter of any inauspicious Yoga. (6)

Thus ends the Chapter on Yogas in the *Muhurta Gananayati*, composed by Gananayati, the son of the learned scholar Daivajña Ravala Harishankara. (5)

**Now, the Chapter on the Karanas:**
The Karanas <note>Karana: half of a lunar day (tithi). There are 11 types in total</note> are: Bava, Balava, Kaulava, Taitila, Gara, Vanija, and Vishti—these are the seven [mobile Karanas]. (1) Take the elapsed lunar days, multiply by two, and count from the first day of the bright fortnight. The Karana Bava begins the sequence in the first half of the day. (2) In the second half of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, the Karana is Shakuni. On the New Moon day, the two halves are Chatushpada and Naga respectively. (3) In the first half of the first day of the bright fortnight, the Karana is named Kimstughna. These four [Shakuni, Chatushpada, Naga, and Kimstughna] are known as the "Fixed" Karanas. (4)

**Now, the Lords of the Karanas:**
Indra, Brahma, Mitra, Aryaman, Bhumi (Earth), Shri (Lakshmi), Yama, Kali, the Sun, the Serpent (Naga), and Vayu (Wind) are the presiding deities of the Karanas. (5) In Bava, perform acts for nourishment and growth; in Balava, perform rituals related to Brahmins. In Kaulava, activities related to women and friends are favored; in Taitila, acts that are auspicious and dear. (6) In Gara, one should perform tasks related to seeds and agriculture; in Vanija, commerce...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing, specifically focusing on the Karanas (half-lunar days) and the rules surrounding the volatile period known as Bhadra or Vishti.</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>
->Su-Ga<-

One should conduct trade <note>original: "Vāṇija," the name of a specific Karana or lunar half-day</note> during its period. During the Vishti <note>another name for the Bhadra period</note> period, burning, falling, or similar calamities occur; nothing auspicious should be done then. || 7 || During the Shakuni period, one should engage in omens, medicine, chanting mantras, and rites for prosperity. During the Chatushpada period, one should attend to matters of the state, cattle, priests, and ancestral rites. || 8 || During the Naga period, one should perform acts related to beauty or those of a harsh nature. During the Kimstughna period, auspicious ceremonies should be performed. || 9 ||

### Now, the Specifics of Bhadra

In the bright fortnight <note>the waxing moon</note>, Bhadra resides in the latter half of the eleventh and fourth lunar days. It is said to reside in the first half of the eighth and full moon days. || 10 || In the dark fortnight <note>the waning moon</note>, it resides in the latter half of the third and tenth days, and in the first half of the seventh and fourteenth days. || 11 ||

### Now, the Division of Bhadra’s Limbs and Their Results

Five units of time <note>original: "nāḍī," a measure of 24 minutes</note> are in the mouth, one in the throat, and ten in the heart. Five are in the navel, six in the waist, and three units of time make up the tail of the Vishti. || 12 || The results arising from these limbs of Vishti are, in order: destruction of work, death, loss of wealth, gain of wisdom, stability, and strife. Success is the result of the tail. || 13 || In urgent matters, one must avoid only the mouth of the Vishti.

### Now, the Direction of Bhadra’s Face

On the lunar days measured by the numbers 14, 8, 7, 3, 4, 10, 11, and 1, during the successive watches of the day starting from the first, the face of Vishti resides in the directions starting from the East. || 14 || One should know the face of Vishti; the back is auspicious, while the front is inauspicious. || 15 ||

### Now, the Tail of Bhadra

On the eighth day, it is in the first and eighth watches; on the eleventh day, in the first and eighth; on the full moon day, in the sixth; and on the third day, in the third. || 16 || These three units of time are called the "tail." Now, for the dark fortnight: on the third day, it is in the seventh watch; on the seventh day, in the second. || 17 || On the tenth day, in the fifth watch; and on the fourteenth day, in the fourth watch. In the tail of Vishti, there is victory in battle and other works are highly auspicious. || 18 ||

### Now, the Nature of Bhadra

Ga. 23 <note>Likely an abbreviation for the title of the work and the folio number.</note></margin>In ancient times, during the war between the gods and the demons, she emerged from the body of Lord Shiva. She is the destroyer of demons, mounted upon a donkey, with trembling fingers and three legs. || 19 || She has the neck of a lion, is mounted on a breath <note>original: "shvasa-rudha," possibly implying a swift or ethereal movement</note>, possesses seven hands, and a slender belly. By the command of Shiva, she was appointed by the immortals during the confusion of the *Shravana* <note>the month or the lunar mansion</note>. || 20 || She is fiercely terrible, with a gaping mouth and large, frightening fangs. She is the destroyer of undertakings for the world, being filled with the flames of fire. || 21 ||

Now, the exceptions to the faults of *Bhadra* <note>Bhadra, also known as Vishti, is an inauspicious astrological period</note> are stated: If *Bhadra* occurs in the first half of a lunar day during the night, or in the latter half of a lunar day during the daytime, the fault of *Bhadra* does not apply there when a task is necessary. || 22 || When the Moon is in Aries, Taurus, Gemini, or Scorpio, *Bhadra* resides in the world of the gods (Heaven). When the Moon is in Virgo, Libra, Sagittarius, or Gemini <note>original: "dhanuryugme," referring to the pairs of signs</note>, *Bhadra* resides in the netherworld (*Rasatala*). || 23 || When the Moon is well-situated in Aquarius, Pisces, Cancer, or Leo, *Bhadra* resides in the world of mortals (Earth). One should always avoid her when she is in the world of mortals; she is auspicious when residing in Heaven or the netherworld. || 24 || The astrological yogas *Vaidhriti* and *Vyatipata*, as well as *Vishti* (*Bhadra*) and Tuesdays—likewise, even inauspicious stars—become auspicious after midday. || 25 ||

Now, a specific opinion regarding the distinctions of *Bhadra*: In the bright fortnight, *Bhadra* is a Scorpion; in the dark fortnight, she is a Serpent. Some say she is a Serpent by day and a Scorpion by night. || 26 || One should avoid the mouth of the Serpent and the tail of the Scorpion. In the birth of donkeys or horses, in difficult forts, in worship, and in acts of charity, or in burning and striking, *Bhadra* is considered praised (auspicious); she is not beautiful (auspicious) in other matters. || 27 ||

For Ashwini, the syllables are *Chu, Che, Cho, La*. For Bharani, they are *Li, Lu, Le, Lo*. For Krittika, they are *A, I, U, E*. For Rohini, they are known as *O, Va, Vi, Vu*. || 9 ||

For Hasta, they are separately stated as *Pu, Sha, Na, Tha*. For Chitra, <margin>*</margin> they are *Pe, Po, Ra, Ri*. For Swati, they are *Ru, Re, Ro, Ta*. For Vishakha, they are *Ti, Tu, Te, To*. || 12 ||
For Anuradha, they are *Na, Ni, Nu, Ne*. For Jyeshtha, <margin>24</margin> they are considered *No, Ya, Yi, Yu*. For Mula, they are *Ye, Yo, Bha, Bhi*. For Purva Ashadha, they are *Bhu, Dha, Pha, Dha*. || 13 ||
For Uttara Ashadha, they are *Bhe, Bho, Ja, Ji*...

Ju, Je, Jo, Kha, as well as Abhijit <note>Abhijit is the "intercalary" 28th lunar mansion, often used in specific astrological calculations</note>. Khi, Khu, Khe, Kho are to be known here; Ga, Gi, Gu, Ge belong to Dhanishta <note>The 23rd lunar mansion, associated with wealth and music</note>. || 14 || Go, Sa, Si, Su are called Shatataraka <note>Also known as Shatabhisha, the 24th lunar mansion</note>; Se, So, Da, Di are considered Purva Bhadrapada. || 15 || Utha, Du, Tha, Jha, Jña are to be known as Uttara Bhadrapada; De, Do, Cha, Chi belong to Revati. <note>These syllables represent the "Padas" or quarters of the lunar mansions used for naming children and calculating compatibility</note> || 15 ||

### Now, the Auspicious and Inauspicious Results of the Moon

The results are determined by the position of the Moon relative to one's birth sign <term>Janma Rashi: the zodiac sign the moon occupied at the moment of birth</term>. When the Moon is in the first house (the birth sign), there is physical nourishment; in the second, there is happiness. || 16 || In the third, there is gain of wealth; in the fourth, the possibility of disease. In the fifth, there is success in undertakings; in the sixth, a great arrival of money. || 17 || In the seventh, there is great honor; in the eighth, death is certain <note>This is a traditional exaggeration indicating extreme difficulty or "death-like" suffering</note>. In the ninth, fear is to be known; in the tenth, wealth from work. || 18 || In the eleventh, gain of profit; and in the twelfth, various calamities. || 19 ||

### Now, the Specifics for the Bright Fortnight

The Moon in the second, fifth, and ninth houses is particularly auspicious. This is the specific rule for the Bright Fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha: the waxing phase of the moon</term>; in other respects, the two fortnights are equal. || 20 || In the Bright Fortnight, the strength of the Moon itself is paramount, not the strength of the stars <term>Tara: the lunar constellation</term>. Just as when a husband is present and powerful at night, a woman does not act independently. || 21 || However, in the Dark Fortnight <term>Krishna Paksha: the waning phase</term>, the strength of the stars must always be taken. When the Moon is weak or like a husband who has gone abroad, the mistress of the house must manage the household herself. || 22 ||

<margin>
25
</margin>

### Now, the Strength of the Fortnightly Moon in Urgent Matters

Even if the lunar position is unfavorable, the fortnight is auspicious at the beginning of the Dark Fortnight. In the Bright Fortnight, if the Moon is auspicious, the fortnight is highly beneficial even in times of crisis. || 23 ||

### Now, the Strength of the Stars

Count from the birth star to the star of the day and divide by nine. The remainders represent: Birth, Wealth, Calamity, Prosperity, Obstacle, Realization, Destruction, Friend, and Great Friend, respectively. || 24 || The results are consistent with these names. The 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, and 9th positions are auspicious. 2 | 4 | 6 | 8 <note>The text lists the favorable counts for the 'Tara Bala' or stellar strength calculation</note>

The eighth stars are considered the most excellent. || 25 || The star known as "Birth" (Janma) is known to be middling, while the others—specifically the 3rd, 5th, and 7th—are proclaimed to be avoided. || 26 || In cases of extreme necessity for a task, one must avoid the specific harmful portions of the "bad" stars. The stars are full at their end; for the 5th, 3rd, and 7th stars <note>These refer to Vipat (danger), Pratyari (enemy), and Vadha (death) stars in the Navatara system</note>, one should avoid the first quarter of the 5th, the second quarter of the 3rd, and the third quarter of the 7th. In the third cycle, however, all are considered auspicious when a task is absolutely mandatory. || 27 || **Now, regarding charity for the "bad" stars** || On the seventh star, one should give gold and sesame seeds; on the third star, jaggery; and on the fifth, salt. To remove the faults of these stars, one should give vegetables during the three "Birth" stars. || 28 || **Now, the States of the Moon (Chandra-avastha)** || 1. Journeying, 2. Destruction, 3. Death, 4. Victory, 5. Laughter, 6. Pleasure, 7. Play, 8. Sleep, 9. Eating, 10. Sitting, 11. Trembling, and 12. Stability—these twelve states of the Moon are remembered. || 29 || Their duration is known to be eleven <term>Nadis</term> <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> and a quarter (11.25 Nadis). || 30 || Starting from Aries, these states like "Journeying" provide results corresponding to their names. || 31 || **Now, an example of their sequence** || When the Moon is in Aries, the sequence starts with "Journeying"; in Taurus, it starts with "Destruction"; in Gemini, it starts with "Death," and so on through the twelve signs. <margin>26</margin> || 32 || **Now, the strength of planets for specific tasks and the universal strength of the Moon** || Jupiter is strong for weddings and festivals; the Sun for meeting kings or authorities; Mars for collections or battles; Mercury for the commencement of education. || 33 || Venus is proclaimed for journeys, and Saturn for initiation (<term>Diksha</term>). However, the Moon is accepted by the wise as auspicious for all undertakings. || 34 || **Now, a specific detail on the Moon's strength** || Even a beneficial Moon, if affected by a fall or confusion...

### Now, the giving of gifts for the removal of defects of the Lunar Day, Weekday, Constellation, Moon, and Star-strength during essential tasks:

One should give rice when the Lunar Day <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> is unfavorable; when the Weekday is unfavorable, a gem with gold; when the Constellation <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 sectors of the ecliptic</term> is unfavorable, a cow; during an unfavorable Yoga, gold; and during an unfavorable Karana <note>half of a Tithi</note>, grain. || 37 ||

When the Moon is weak, one should give a conch shell with silver; when the Star-strength <term>Tara: the strength of the star relative to one's birth star</term> is poor, rock salt; and when the Pulse <note>Nadi: a specific astrological point</note> is afflicted, a king or leader should give gold, provided the work to be done is absolutely necessary. || 38 ||

### Thus ends the chapter on the Strength of the Moon and Stars by the illustrious astrologer Ravala Harishankara. || 7 ||

### || Now begins the Chapter on Auspicious and Inauspicious Timings ||

Regarding the auspicious combinations arising from the Lunar Day and the Weekday:
Nanda on Friday, Bhadra on Wednesday, Rikta on Saturday, Jaya on Tuesday, and Purna on Thursday—these are the "Success-Granting Combinations" <note>Karya Siddhi Yogas</note> which bring auspicious results. || 1 || According to the opinion of sages like Vashistha, the following are also auspicious: Nanda, Bhadra, Nanda, Jaya, Rikta, Bhadra, and Purna on the days starting from Sunday <note>Sunday through Saturday respectively</note>. || 2 ||

### Now, the combinations arising from the Constellation and the Weekday:

First, the "Attainment of Immortality" combinations <term>Amrita Siddhi Yoga: a highly auspicious time for starting new ventures</term>:
Hand <note>original: "Hasta"</note> on Sunday, Deer-Head <note>original: "Mrigashira"</note> on Monday, Nourishing <note>original: "Pushya"</note> on Thursday, Horse-Men <note>original: "Ashwini"</note> on Tuesday, Small-Sparkler <note>original: "Anuradha"</note> on Wednesday, Wealthy <note>original: "Revati"</note> on Friday, and Red-One <note>original: "Rohini"</note> on Saturday. || 3 || This is the "Internal Success Combination" which grants success in all matters. Just as the Sun destroys darkness, this combination destroys a multitude of astrological defects. || 4 ||

In certain cases, even this "Attainment of Immortality" combination must be avoided:
When the combination of Sunday and the Hand constellation falls on the fifth lunar day, or Tuesday with the Horse-Men constellation <insert>and so on</insert>, these specific "Attainment of Immortality" combinations should be avoided; all others are effective for all purposes. || 5 || For marriage, the combination of Thursday and the Nourishing constellation <note>Guru-Pushya</note> is avoided, and for entering a new home...

### The Sun-Yoga which Destroys Groups of Defects
When the Moon's constellation is the 10th, 6th, 4th, 20th, 13th, or 9th from the Sun's constellation, it is called *Ravi-yoga* <note>Sun-combination</note>, which bestows auspiciousness. (7) This combination is declared to be the destroyer of numerous groups of astrological defects. (8)

### The Ananda and Other Combinations
The twenty-eight combinations are: Ananda <note>Joy</note>, Kaladanda <note>Staff of Death</note>, Dhumraksha <note>Smoke-eyed</note>, Prajapati <note>Lord of Creatures</note>, Saumya <note>Gentle</note>, Dhvanksha <note>Crow</note>, Dhvaja <note>Banner</note>, Shrivatsa <note>Mark of Vishnu</note>, Vajra <note>Thunderbolt</note>, Mudgara <note>Hammer</note>, (9) Kshatra <note>Dominion</note>, Mitra <note>Friend</note>, Kanera <note>meaning uncertain, likely a specific omen</note>, Manasa <note>Mental</note>, Padma <note>Lotus</note>, Lumbaka <note>Pendant</note>, Utpata <note>Calamity</note>, Mrityu <note>Death</note>, Kana <note>One-eyed</note>, Siddhi <note>Success</note>, Shubha <note>Auspicious</note>, Amrita <note>Nectar</note>, (10) Musala <note>Pestle</note>, Gada <note>Mace</note>, Matanga <note>Elephant</note>, Rakshasa <note>Demon</note>, Chara <note>Mobile</note>, Sthira <note>Fixed</note>, and Pravardhamana <note>Increasing</note>. The fruits of these are equal to their names. (11)

On Sunday, starting from the Ashvini constellation (including Abhijit among all the stars), these twenty-eight combinations occur in sequence. (12) On Tuesday, starting from Mrigashira at night until the end of the bright fortnight. On Wednesday starting from Hasta; on Thursday from Anuradha; (13) on Friday from Uttarashadha; and on Saturday from Shatabhisha. (14)

### Knowledge of the Fourfold Calamitous Combinations
In sequence, for the four constellations starting from Vishakha on Sunday; from Purvashadha on Monday; from Dhanishtha on Tuesday; (15) from Revati on Wednesday; from Rohini on Thursday; from Pushya on Friday; and from [the appropriate star] on Saturday—these "Utpata" and others occur. (16) These four combinations are known as Utpata <note>Calamity</note>, Mrityu <note>Death</note>, Kana <note>One-eyed/Defective</note>, and Siddhi <note>Success</note>. (17)

### Now, the <term>Mrityu Yoga</term> <note>The "Death Combination," considered highly inauspicious for starting new ventures.</note>

Anuradha on a Sunday; Shravana <insert>Uttara Ashadha</insert> on a Monday; Ashwini on a Wednesday; Mrigashira on a Thursday; Ashlesha on a Friday; and Hasta on a Saturday. On a Tuesday, Shatabhisha creates this **Mrityu Yoga**, which causes the destruction of wealth. || 30 ||

### Now, the <term>Dagdha Yoga</term> <note>The "Burnt Combination," where the energy of the day is considered consumed or scorched.</note>

Bharani on a Sunday; Chitra on a Monday; Uttara Phalguni on a Thursday; Uttara Ashadha on a Tuesday; Dhanishta on a Wednesday; || 31 || Jyeshtha on a Friday; and Revati on a Saturday. One should avoid these "burnt" constellations. || 32 ||

### Now, the three types of <term>Gandanta</term> <note>A "spiritual knot" or transitional junction point between zodiac signs and lunar mansions, considered unstable.</note> to be avoided in auspicious acts:

One should avoid the three types of **Gandanta**: those of the Constellation (Nakshatra), the Lunar Day (Tithi), and the Zodiac Sign (Rashi). The duration to be avoided is 4, 5, and 2 <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>A traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes; 2 Ghatikas = 48 minutes.</note> respectively. One should avoid these durations at the end of the previous and the beginning of the following period. || 33 ||

The text further explains:
The junction between Jyeshtha and Mula; between Revati and Ashwini; and between Ashlesha and Magha—the four <term>Nadis</term> <note>Synonymous with Ghatikas</note> at these intervals are avoided. || 34 ||

The junction between the 5th and 6th lunar days; between the Full Moon and the 1st day of the dark fortnight; and between the 10th and 11th lunar days—these are the **Gandanta** junctions lasting two **Ghatikas**. || 35 ||

The junction between Cancer and Leo; between Pisces and Aries; and between Scorpio and Sagittarius—the junction of these Ascendants (Lagnas) lasts for one **Ghatika**. || 36 ||

### Now, the <term>Ardha-Yamas</term> <note>Specific half-segments of the day (roughly 1.5 hours) ruled by different planetary energies.</note>

The **Ardha-Yamas** to be avoided are the 4th, 7th, 2nd, 5th, 8th, 3rd, and 6th segments, counting sequentially from Sunday. <margin>30</margin> || 37 ||

# Page 32

The auspicious moments <note>original: "Muhurtas"</note> are numbered 6, 10, 7, 8, and 14. On the day of the Moon <note>Monday</note>, the moments are measured by the Ashvins <note>2</note>, Vasus <note>8</note>, Agni <note>3</note>, Vishvedevas <note>13</note>, Manu <note>14</note>, and the Planets and Sun <note>9 and 12</note>, totaling 4, 8, 3, 13, 14, 6, <margin>Muhurta Ganapati 32</margin> 22. || 42 || On the day of Mars <note>Tuesday</note>, the moments are remembered in the numbers 2, 3, 4, 6, and 10. || On the day of Mercury <note>Wednesday</note>, the moments are measured by the Vedas <note>4</note>, Ashvins <note>2</note>, Agni <note>3</note>, the Nine <note>9</note>, Manu <note>14</note>, and the Directions <note>10</note>, totaling 4, 2, 8, 6, 14, 10. || 43 || On the day of Jupiter <note>Thursday</note>, they are measured by the Kings <note>16</note>, Ashvins <note>2</note>, the Tithis <note>15</note>, the Limbs <note>6</note>, Manu <note>14</note>, and the Sun <note>12</note>, totaling 16, 2, 15, 6, 14, 12. || On the day of Venus <note>Friday</note>, they are measured by the Five <note>5</note>, the Nine <note>9</note>, the Limbs <note>6</note>, the Vedas <note>4</note>, the Directions <note>10</note>, the Sun <note>12</note>, and Manu <note>14</note>, totaling 5, 9, 6, 4, 10, 12, 14. || 44 || On the day of Saturn <note>Saturday</note>, these moments are the portions of the Sages <note>7</note>, the Eyes <note>2</note>, Rudra <note>11</note>, and the Eight <note>8</note>, totaling 2, 2, 11, 8, and sixteen parts. || 45 ||

### Regarding the Specific Avoidance of Yama-ghanta and Kulika Moments

Now, the specific regions where the moments of Yama-ghanta <note>the "Bell of Death," an inauspicious time</note> and Kulika <note>a specific astrological period</note> must be avoided: The Yama-ghanta is a defect in the regions between the Vindhya and Himalaya mountains and in Magadha. In the regions of Anga, Vanga, or Matsya, this defect is considered influential; elsewhere, it is not. || 46 || In Kashmir, the Kulika period must be avoided, but the "half-watch" <note>Ardha-yama</note> should be avoided everywhere. || 47 ||

### Regarding Prohibited Times During Solar Transits

Now, the time to be avoided during a Solar Transit <note>Sankranti</note>: During the Solstices <note>Ayana</note> and Equinoxes <note>Vishuva</note>, the day before, the day of, and the day after should be avoided. For other transits—specifically the 2nd, 5th, 8th, 11th, 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 12th signs—sixteen ghatikas <note>units of 24 minutes</note> before and after the transit are prohibited. || 48 ||

### Regarding the Defects of Lunar Day Decrease and Increase

Now, the defect of Lunar Day <note>Tithi</note> decrease and increase: When three lunar days touch a single solar weekday, that day is known as an "Omitted Day" <note>Avama</note>; one should abandon it for all auspicious deeds. || 49 || When a single lunar day touches three solar weekdays, it is known as "Vidyutsrikka" <note>Lightning-streaked</note>; it should not be accepted for marriages and other auspicious events. || 50 ||

### Regarding "Empty Months," Lunar Days, and Constellations

Now, the "Empty Months" <note>Masa-shunya</note>, lunar days, and ascendants: Here, the months ending with the New Moon <note>Amanta system</note> are to be taken. In the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, the 8th and 9th lunar days are empty. In the month of Vaishakha <note>April-May</note>, the 12th lunar day is empty. <margin>32</margin>

In the month of Shravana <note>July-August</note>, the second and third lunar days are void (2, 3). In Bhadrapada <note>August-September</note>, it is the twelfth (12). Now, the Void Constellations <term>Shunya Nakshatras: lunar mansions that lose their efficacy during specific months</term> of the months.**

Ashvini and Rohini are void in Chaitra <note>March-April</note>; Chitra and Swati in Madhava <note>Vaishakha, April-May</note>. In Jyeshtha, the primary void is Uttarashadha; in Shuchi <note>Ashadha</note>, it is Dhanishtha. || 56 || In Shravana, it is Shravana; in Ashvina, it is Purvabhadrapada. In Phalguna, Bharani and Jyeshtha; in Magha, Mula and Shravana. These are the constellations declared "void of the month" by the learned. || 58 ||

Aquarius, Pisces, Taurus, Gemini, Aries, Virgo, Scorpio, Libra, Sagittarius, Cancer, Capricorn, and Leo. || 59 || These are stated as the void signs in order, starting from the month of Chaitra. || 60 || **Now, the Void Ascendants <term>Shunya Lagnas: the rising signs considered inauspicious on specific lunar days</term> during odd-numbered lunar days.**

Cancer and Sagittarius. || 61 || On the eleventh lunar day, Sagittarius and Pisces; on the ninth, Leo and Cancer; on the thirteenth, Pisces. One should avoid these "Death Ascendants" <note>mṛtyu-lagna: specific zodiac signs considered fatal if they rise during certain lunar days</note>. || 62 || Now, regarding the regional exceptions for avoiding these "Death Dates" and other factors: The specific constellations, lunar days, ascendants, and "Month-Lords of Death" should be avoided for auspicious acts in the *Middle Country* <note>Madhya-desha: the central heartland of ancient India, roughly between the Himalayas and the Vindhya mountains</note>, but not necessarily elsewhere. || 63 || Now, the exceptions for these combinations when a task is mandatory: Combinations arising from the lunar day and weekday, those known as "Evil Names," and those born of the constellations—one should avoid these in the regions of *Vanga* <note>Bengal</note> and *Arvasha* <note>possibly a regional variant for the Northwest or a specific local kingdom</note>. || 64 || They are not prohibited in other regions, as the merits of the lunar day and constellation prevail there. Regarding the portents of death, the "Prison" yoga, the "Belly of the Earth," and the "Time-Spirit" <note>Kālaka</note>— || 65 || As well as the "Saw" <note>Krakacha</note> and the "Bell of Death" <note>Yamaghaṇṭā</note>: authorities declare these become auspicious if the Moon is well-placed. Some say this applies after midday; others say after one watch <note>yāma: a period of about 3 hours</note> has passed. || 66 || For activities other than travel, others declare these timings auspicious if the Ascendant is pure. If an inauspicious combination and a "Success Combination" <note>Siddhi Yoga</note> occur together, both are considered auspicious. || 67 || The good combination destroys the evil combination, bringing about the successful completion of the task. || 68 || Now, the "Triple-Lotus" <note>Tripuṣkara</note> and "Twin" <note>Yamala</note> combinations for auspicious and inauspicious acts: If on a *Bhadrā* lunar day <note>the 2nd, 7th, or 12th</note> falling on a Saturday, Thursday, or Sunday, there is a constellation with three-quarters of its feet <note>pāda: a quarter division of a constellation</note> remaining, it is the *Tripuṣkara* Yoga. If it occurs on a *Nandā* day <note>1st, 6th, or 11th</note> with a two-footed constellation, it is the *Yamala* Yoga. || 69 || Now, for easy understanding, the auspicious and inauspicious combinations on Sundays and other days: On Sunday, the constellations Hasta, Ashvini, Mula, Dhanishtha, the three Uttaras, and Pushya, as well as the eighth lunar day—these are known as "Success Combinations" <note>Siddhi Yogas</note>. || 70 || Vishakha, Bharani, Shravana, Magha, Jyeshtha, Anuradha—

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati Page 36</margin>...[The constellations of] Ashadha, Uttarashadha, Chitra, Hasta, and Revati. The sixth lunar day <note>tithi</note> and the seventh lunar day are forbidden on a Saturday. || 83 || 

->Thus ends the chapter on Auspicious and Inauspicious Matters <note>Shubhashubha Prakarana</note> by the astrologer Ravala Harishankara, son of Hari. This is the 8th chapter.<-

### Now begins the Chapter on Forbidden Times <note>Tyajya Prakarana</note>

Understand the harmful combinations <note>yogas</note> arising from the mutual relationships between lunar days <note>tithis</note>, constellations <note>nakshatras</note>, and days of the week. Know also the ill-omened periods such as *Vyatipata* <note>a specific astronomical alignment</note>, the New Moon <note>Darsha</note>, and the Sun's entry into a new sign <note>Sankranti</note>. || 1 ||

[Avoid] the half-tithi, the half-month, the half-constellation of Pushya, and the day of a decreased lunar day <note>Avama</note>. Also avoid constellations joined with or pierced by malefic planets, and those affected by "Lata" <note>an astrological 'kick' or planetary influence</note>. || 2 ||

Avoid constellations affected by portents <note>utpata</note> or split by planets, and the specific degrees of an eclipse within one's own constellation. If a solar or lunar eclipse is half-visible, the prohibition lasts for three months. || 3 ||

If a quarter of the Moon or Sun is eclipsed, the prohibition lasts for one month. One should avoid three days before an eclipse and seven days after an eclipse. || 4 ||

If the Sun or Moon sets while eclipsed, avoid three days; if they rise while eclipsed, avoid the following three days. This applies to a total eclipse; in a partial eclipse, the prohibition applies only to the specific time of the fall <note>pata</note>. || 5 ||

The three types of *Gandanta* <note>unlucky transition points between signs/constellations</note> are wicked, as are the waning moon, and the "Malefic Scissors" <note>Papa Kartari - when a house is hemmed in by malefic planets</note>. Avoid the hour <note>hora</note> of a malefic planet, the day of a cruel planet, and the half-watch <note>yamardha</note> of *Kulika* and similar periods. || 6 ||

Avoid a Moon joined with malefic planets, or a malefic planet in the first or seventh house, or a bad ninth-division <note>Navamsha</note>. Also avoid the eighth house from the birth sign or the birth ascendant. || 7 ||

Avoid one day at the end of a month, two ghatis <note>approx. 48 minutes</note> at the end of a constellation, one ghati at the end of a lunar day, and half a ghati at the end of an ascendant <note>Lagna</note>. || 8 ||

The "poison-named" pulses <note>Visha-nadika</note> of the constellations, the *Pata* and *Ekargala* <note>specific astronomical hazards</note>, the "Burnt Day" <note>Dagdhaha</note>, and the equality of declination <note>Kranti Samya</note>. <margin>36</margin> Also avoid when the Lord of the Ascendant is in the 6th or 8th house. || 9 ||

Avoid the constellation of one's birth at midday, and twenty moments <note>palas</note> at the junctions <note>sandhi</note>. Avoid the intercalary month <note>Malamasa</note>, the setting of Venus or Jupiter, and their periods of "infancy" or "old age" <note>referring to the days immediately after or before a planet's heliacal rising/setting</note>...

It establishes the technical rules for identifying periods of spiritual or physical risk in the Hindu calendar.</summary>

...in the same manner. || 10 || One should avoid the following for auspicious ceremonies: the birth constellation <note>Janma Nakshatra</note>, mental distress, the period of impurity following a birth or death <note>Sutaka</note>, a mother’s menstrual cycle, illnesses, and natural portents or calamities. || 11 || Thus ends the Chapter on Avoidance <note>Tyajya Prakarana</note> in the *Muhurta Ganapati* composed by Ganapati, the son of the illustrious astrologer Ravala Harishankara Suri. || 8 ||

# || Now, the Chapter on the Ascendant ||

Aries, Taurus, then Gemini, Cancer, Leo, and Virgo; Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces. || 1 || These signs, in this order, are known respectively as male and female, and as cruel and gentle. Furthermore, they are understood to be movable, fixed, and dual-natured in successive order. || 2 || Sagittarius, Aries, Capricorn, Taurus, and Cancer are "rear-rising" <note>Prishthodaya: signs that rise with their back first</note>. Pisces rises "both ways" <note>Ubhayodaya</note>. The remaining signs are "head-rising" <note>Shirshodaya</note>. || 3 || Aries, Taurus, Sagittarius, Gemini, Cancer, and Capricorn are strong at night. The others are strong during the day. All are most powerful during their own specific times. || 4 ||

### || Now, the Lords of the Signs ||

Mars is the lord of Aries and Scorpio; Mercury of Gemini and Virgo. Venus rules Libra and Taurus; the Moon rules Cancer. || 5 || The Sun is the lord of Leo; Saturn of Capricorn and Aquarius. Jupiter is the lord of both Sagittarius and Pisces. These are remembered as the Lords of the Signs. || 6 ||

### || Now, the Exaltations of the Planets ||

Aries, Taurus, Capricorn, Virgo, Cancer, and Libra—these are declared to be the signs of exaltation for the Sun and the other planets in their respective order. || 7 || The degrees of highest exaltation for the Sun and the others are, in order: 10, 3, <margin>37</margin> 28, 15, 5, 27, and 20. <note>These represent the specific degrees within the signs where the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn reach their peak strength.</note> || 8 ||
