-> **Now, the Debilitations** <-
The sages have declared that the debilitation <term>Neecha; the point where a planet is weakest</term> of the Sun and the other planets is the seventh sign from their respective exaltation signs. For Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>, the sign of Virgo is its home, and Gemini is remembered as its exaltation sign. || 9 ||

-> **Now, the Root-Trines** <-
Leo, Taurus, Aries, Virgo, Sagittarius, Libra, and Aquarius are remembered as the Root-Trine <term>Mula-Trikona; a sign position where a planet is exceptionally strong, second only to exaltation</term> signs for the Sun and the other planets, respectively. || 10 ||

-> **Now, Activities for Aries and the Following Ascendants** <-
Building walls or fortifications, working with metals, the coronation of kings, confrontations, and courageous or daring deeds—these are successfully accomplished when **Aries** is the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>. || 11 ||

Gifts of women's clothing and ornaments, the formal selection of a bride, entering a new home, and marriage ceremonies are successfully achieved during the rise of **Taurus**. || 12 ||

Arts, decorations, and scientific or technical knowledge—whatever work is prescribed for the rise of Taurus is also declared for **Gemini** by those expert in the science of time <term>Hora Shastra; astrology</term>. || 13 ||

The damming or releasing of water, nourishing rituals, and activities involving movement, as well as the construction of large ponds, wells, and tanks, are prescribed for **Cancer** by the wise. || 14 ||

Service to kings, agriculture, trade, joining with others, and the paths of merchants—all these are successful in **Leo**, as well as whatever was mentioned for the Aries Ascendant. || 15 ||

Ornaments, craftsmanship, science, medicine, and nourishing acts, whether of a moving or fixed nature—all these are successfully accomplished in the **Virgo** Ascendant. || 16 ||

Commerce, plowing, service, journeys, and the management of vessels and expenses—these have been described for the **Libra** Ascendant by the sages who know the underlying principles. || 17 ||

Service to kings, coronations, daring deeds, harsh or formidable actions, fierce activities, theft <note>likely referring to strategic raids or recovery of stolen goods</note>, and stable works should be performed in **Scorpio** <note>original: "Sarisripa," meaning the creeping one</note>. <margin>38</margin> || 18 ||

Marriage, nourishing rituals, journeys, vehicles, and the management of fires—these and other moving activities were declared for the **Sagittarius** Ascendant by the ancient scholars. || 19 ||

Purchasing female servants, four-footed animals, or camels; the binding or releasing of water; matters concerning fields and lands; and making requests or journeys should be undertaken when Capricorn is rising. || 20 || The collection of seeds, matters involving boats, and the movement of water; as well as fixed or moving works, should always be performed by the wise when Aquarius is rising. || 21 || Marriage, consecration ceremonies, the pursuit of knowledge, and the wearing of ornaments; as well as activities involving cattle and water, are declared auspicious when Pisces is the rising sign. || 22 || When these signs are pure <note>meaning free from the influence of malefic planets</note>, the prescribed actions will meet with success. However, if they are aspected by or joined with malefic planets, only cruel or harsh deeds will succeed, and nothing else. || 23 ||

**Now, the Six-fold Divisions (Shadvarga):**

The six divisions are known as the Sign (Griha), the Half-sign (Hora), the Third (Drekkana), the Ninth (Navamshaka), the Twelfth (Dvadasamsha), and the <term>Trimshamsha</term> <note>the 30th part or degree-division</note>; these are the divisions of the signs. || 24 || A full sign consists of thirty degrees (30°). Half of a sign (15°) is called a <term>Hora</term>. The <term>Drekkana</term> is the third part (10°), and the <term>Navamshaka</term> is the ninth part (3° 20'). || 25 || The <term>Dvadasamsha</term> is the twelfth part (2° 30'), and the <term>Trimshamsha</term> is the division of single degrees (1°). The planet that rules the rising sign is declared the Lord of the Horoscope. || 26 ||

In even signs, the Horas belong to the Moon and the Sun respectively; in odd signs, they belong to the Sun and the Moon. The lords of the Drekkanas are the lords of the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses from that sign. || 27 || For Sagittarius, Leo, and Aries, the Ninth-parts (Navamshakas) begin with Aries. For Capricorn, Taurus, and Virgo, they are declared to begin with Capricorn. || 28 || For Libra, Gemini, and Aquarius, the wise say they begin with Libra. For Pisces, Scorpio, and Cancer, they are known to begin with Cancer. || 29 || The Twelfth-parts (Dvadasamshas) are to be understood numerically starting from the sign itself. <note>The text concludes with numerical codes for the Trimshamsha degrees: 5, 5, 8, 7, 5.</note>

|| 30 || The *Trimsamsas* <note>The 30th division of a zodiac sign, used to determine specific planetary strengths and character traits.</note> are proclaimed for Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus. In even signs, the degrees are assigned in portions of five (5), seven (7), eight (8), five (5), and five (5) respectively. || 31 || These belong to Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and Mars in that specific order. || 32 ||

### Now, the Names of the Twelve Houses starting from the Body

The houses starting from the Body (1st) are sequentially: Body (1st), Wealth (2nd), Siblings (3rd), Friend (4th), Son (5th), Enemy (6th), Wife (7th), Death (8th), Dharma/Virtue (9th), Action (10th), Income (11th), and Loss (12th). || 33 || The *Kendra* <note>Angular houses</note> are 1, 4, 7, and 10. The *Panaphara* <note>Succedent houses</note> are 2, 5, 8, and 11. The *Apoklima* <note>Cadent houses</note> are 3, 6, 9, and 12, counted repeatedly from the Ascendant. The ninth and fifth places (9, 5) are proclaimed as the *Trikona* <note>Trine houses, considered highly auspicious.</note>. || 34 ||
The third, tenth, and eleventh houses are called the *Upachaya* <note>Houses of growth or improvement.</note>. The seventh house is called *Jamitra*, *Duna*, or *Sadana*. || 35 ||
The twelfth (12) should be known as *Rishpha*. The third is *Duschikya*. The fourth and eighth are called *Chaturasra*, and the eighth is also termed *Randhra*. || 36 ||

### Now, the Best Division in the Ninth Part (Vargottama Navamsha)

In a Moveable sign, the first *Navamsha* <note>The 1/9th division of a sign.</note> is the best; in a Fixed sign, it is the fifth; and in a Dual-natured sign, it is the ninth. This is called *Vargottama* <note>Literally "best division"; a planet in the same sign in both the main chart and the 9th division chart gains great strength.</note> and is very auspicious. || 37 ||

### Now, the Strength of Planets in the Ascendant for General Auspicious Activities

In all auspicious activities, planets situated in the 12th house are not desired. Malefics in the Ascendant and benefics in the 6th are avoided; however, malefics in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses are good. || 38 ||
Benefics situated in the *Kendra* (1, 4, 7, 10) or *Trikona* (1, 5, 9), and malefics in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th—or indeed all planets in the 11th (Gain) house—are excellent for auspicious deeds. || 39 || <margin>39</margin>
A house that is aspected by or joined with its own Lord or by benefic planets possesses great strength. It yields its full results, whereas the opposite occurs if it is joined or aspected by malefic planets. || 40 || <margin>40</margin>

<margin>Su. Ga. 49</margin> When the <term>Ascendant</term><note>original: "lagna"; the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</note> and its divisional charts are purified, and the planets are situated in their prescribed positions, all auspicious activities should be performed during favorable lunar signs, lunar days, and weekdays. || 41 ||

This has been stated in general regarding the strength of the planets and the Ascendant. || 42 || However, specific details should be understood according to the <term>scriptures</term><note>original: "shastra"</note> for each particular <term>auspicious moment</term><note>original: "muhurta"</note>.

Thus ends the Chapter on the Ascendant by the illustrious <term>Astrologer</term><note>original: "daivajña"</note> Ravala Harishankara. || 10 ||

**Now begins the Chapter on New Clothing and Ornaments.**

Under the five stars beginning with Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, Svati, Vishakha, and Anuradha</note>, in Pushya, Dhanishtha, and both Revatis <note>likely referring to the transition or specific quarters</note>, in the three "Uttara" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, in Punarvasu, and in Rohini, on an auspicious lunar day, || 1 ||

And on the days of Mercury, Venus, and Jupiter <note>Wednesday, Friday, and Thursday</note>, the wearing of new clothing is advised. The wearing of ornaments made of gold, gems, silver, and so on, is also auspicious. || 2 ||

On the previously mentioned <term>lunar mansions</term><note>original: "dhishnya"; stars/nakshatras</note> and weekdays, one may wear gold and other ornaments. Red garments may be worn on Wednesday, and also on the days of Mars and the Sun <note>Tuesday and Sunday</note>. || 3 ||

**Now, the Results of the Weekdays:**

Wearing new clothes on a Sunday leads to the garment becoming old quickly; on Monday, it may become wet or soiled; on Tuesday, it brings sorrow; on Wednesday, it brings wealth; ||
On Thursday, it brings knowledge; on Friday, it leads to the attainment of a journey; and on Saturday, it makes the garment dirty. These are the effects of the weekdays when wearing new clothing. || 4 ||

**Now, the Results of the Lunar Mansions:**

One obtains more clothes under Ashvini; under Bharani, they are destroyed. Krittika causes fear of fire, while Rohini brings all forms of prosperity. || 5 ||
Under Mrigashira, there is fear of mice <note>who might chew the cloth</note>; under Ardra, there is death. Under Punarvasu and Pushya, there is wealth, righteousness, and great festivals. || 6 ||
Under Ashlesha, there is sorrow; under Magha, death is certain. Under Purva Phalguni, there is fear of the king <note>or government</note>; <margin>49</margin> under Uttara Phalguni, there is the arrival of wealth. || 7 ||
Under Hasta, there is success in one's work; under Chitra, there is desired prosperity. Svati grants the enjoyment of delicious food.

...bringing the gain of friends. In the constellation of *Anuradha* <note>The 17th lunar mansion, associated with friendship</note> and *Jyeshtha*, wearing new clothes leads to the destruction of the garment. In the constellation of *Mula*, there is a risk of leakage or water damage. In *Purvashadha*, it brings severe disease. || 9 ||

In *Uttarashadha*, it brings sweet foods. In *Shravana*, it removes eye diseases. In *Dhanistha*, there is the arrival of grain. In *Shatabhisha*, there is a fear of poison. || 10 ||

In *Purvabhadrapada*, there is fear from water. In *Uttarabhadrapada*, there is the arrival of wealth. In *Revati*, the wearing of new clothes results in the acquisition of gems. || 11 ||

**Now, the specific rules for women regarding the wearing of clothes and ornaments:**
In *Ashwini*, *Dhanistha*, *Revati*, and the five "Hand" constellations <note>The "Hand" constellations (Kara-panchaka) usually refer to Hasta and the four surrounding it: Pushya, Punarvasu, Ashlesha, and Magha, though interpretations vary by school</note>, women should wear gold, gems, ivory, and new clothes. || 12 ||

**Now, the ritual for collyrium and mirrors:**
In the four constellations starting with *Chitra* <note>Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, and Anuradha</note>, and in *Ashwini*, *Dhanistha*, *Revati*, and *Mrigashira*, on Fridays, Sundays, and Saturdays, it is beneficial for women to use mirrors and eye-salve <term>Collyrium: a traditional eye cosmetic/medicine</term>. || 13 ||

**Now, prohibitions for a married woman:**
In *Rohini*, the three "Uttara" constellations <note>Uttaraphalguni, Uttarashadha, and Uttarabhadrapada</note>, *Pushya*, and *Punarvasu*, a fortunate married woman <term>Saubhagyavati: a woman whose husband is alive</term> should never put on new ornaments or clothes, nor should she do so on Tuesdays. || 14 ||

**Now, the results of the division of a new garment:**
If a new garment is divided into nine parts, the four corners belong to the Gods. The three middle parts belong to the Demons (*Rakshasas*), and the two side parts belong to Men. || 15 ||

If a new garment is burnt, worn out, or smeared with mud and the like, it is considered unfavorable if it occurs in the Demon-portion, but auspicious if in the God or Man portions. || 16 ||

The same rules apply to beds, seats, and sandals. || 17 ||

**Now, instances where clothes may be worn even without an auspicious moment:**
Clothes gifted out of affection by a King, or during a marriage, or during festivals, or by the command of a Priest <term>Brahmin</term>, may be worn even on an otherwise forbidden or inauspicious day. || 18 || <margin>42</margin>

<summary>This page details the astrological consequences of wearing new clothes and jewelry based on the lunar mansions, including specific guidelines for married women and exceptions for gifts from authority figures.</summary>

### || Now, Specific Rules for the Purification of Clothing ||

<margin>Mu. Ga. 43</margin> || **Now, on the wearing of white, yellow, variegated, and red clothing** || One should wear white clothing, which is the most excellent, on Sundays, Fridays, Thursdays, and Wednesdays. On Mondays, one should wear variegated <note>original: "karburam" - multi-colored or spotted</note> or yellow clothing. On Tuesdays, red garments are auspicious. || 19 ||

### || Now, on Black and Blue Clothing ||

Under the lunar mansions <note>term: Nakshatra - the 27 divisions of the sky used in Vedic astrology</note> of Punarvasu, Dhanistha, Ashvini, and the four starting from Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, Swati, and Vishakha</note>, as well as the three "Purva" and "Uttara" mansions, it is auspicious to wear blue or black garments on Saturdays and Sundays. || 20 ||

### || Now, on Fine Silk and Woven Cloth ||

On Thursdays, Sundays, Wednesdays, and Fridays, when the moon is in the auspicious mansions previously mentioned for clothing, and during a "fixed" zodiac sign <note>term: Sthira Lagna - signs like Taurus or Leo that suggest stability</note> associated with beneficial planets, one should put on fine silk <note>original: "patta-dukula"</note> garments. || 21 ||

### || Now, on Silk ||

Under the mansions of Ashvini, Revati, Hasta, Rohini, the three Shravanas <note>Shravana, Dhanistha, and Shatabhisha</note>, the "Purva" and "Uttara" sets, Punarvasu, Swati, and Pushya—which is always named as auspicious— || 22 || People should wear silk <note>original: "kausheyam" - specifically silk derived from cocoons</note> garments on Sundays, Mondays, and Thursdays. || 23 ||

### || Now, on Woolen Clothing ||

Under the mansions mentioned for blue cloth, and also in Revati and Pushya, one should wear woolen garments <note>original: "romajam-ambaram" - literally 'hair-born' cloth</note> on Fridays, Saturdays, and Sundays. || 24 ||

### || Now, on the Wearing of Jackets and Bodices ||

Under Pushya, the three "Uttara" mansions, Anuradha, Revati, Dhanistha, Ashvini, and the three "Hasta" mansions <note>Hasta, Chitra, Swati</note>, and Rohini, it is auspicious to don a "body-fortress" <note>note: "deha-durga" refers here to a protective jacket or bodice</note> on Thursdays, Fridays, and Wednesdays. || 25 || <margin>43</margin>

### || Now, on Wearing Cloth Woven with Gold and Silver Threads ||

Garments mixed with gold threads should be worn on Sundays and Tuesdays. Those with silver threads should be worn on Mondays and Wednesdays; such wearing of clothes is auspicious. || 26 ||

### || Now, on the Manufacture of Clothing ||

Under Revati, Rohini, Chitra, Anuradha, Mrigashira, and the three "Uttara" mansions, excluding Saturdays, it is auspicious for weavers to prepare the silk cloth with their threads. || 27 ||

### || Now, on the Dyeing of Cloth with Safflower and Other Pigments ||

Under the two Punarvasus, the five mansions starting from Hasta, the two Shravanas, and Ashvini, it is auspicious to dye garments on Sundays, Fridays, Thursdays, and Tuesdays. || 28 ||

### ## Regarding Needlework
The constellations of Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, Ashwini, Pushya, Rohini, and Hasta, as well as Jyeshtha and Shravana, along with the day of the Sun <note>Sunday</note>, are considered favorable for **needlework** <note>original: "suchikarma"; refers to sewing, embroidery, or tailoring</note>. || 29 ||

### ## Regarding the Washing of New Clothes
In the two Punarvasus <note>Punarvasu and Vishakha</note>, Ashwini, Dhanishtha, and the five Hastas <note>Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, and Anuradha</note>; excluding the sign of Cancer, Wednesdays, the "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note>, the sixth lunar day, and the day of the Sun; as well as avoiding days of religious vows or festivals, one should have clothes **washed** by a washerman and others. || 30–31 ||

### ## Regarding Wearing Shoes and Leatherwork
In Chitra, the Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, Magha, Mrigashira, Vishakha, Krittika, Mula, and Revati; on Wednesdays, Thursdays, and Saturdays; the **wearing of shoes** and **leatherwork** <note>original: "charma karma"</note> is praised. || 32 ||

### ## Regarding the Making of Canopies, Quilts, and Pillows, and the Hanging of Canopies
One should create **quilts** and **pillows** during constellations designated as "small" or "upward-facing." One should hang **canopies** and similar hangings specifically during "upward-facing" constellations <note>Urdhva-mukha nakshatras, believed to favor things that rise or hang high</note>. || 33 ||

### ## Regarding the Construction of Tents and Cloth Houses
In the three Shravanas <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha</note>, the two Ashwinis <note>Ashwini and Bharani</note>, Anuradha, Rohini, Mrigashira, the three Hastas <note>Hasta, Chitra, Swati</note>, and the Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, the construction of a **cloth-house** <note>original: "pata-veshma"; a tent or pavilion</note> is favored. || 34 ||

### ## Regarding the Enjoyment of Fragrances
In the three Shravanas, Ashwini, Pushya, Purva Ashadha, Anuradha, the three Hastas, Punarvasu, and Mrigashira, on an auspicious day; || 35 || the application and wearing of **sandalwood**, **maruga** <note>sweet marjoram</note>, **musk**, and other primary **fragrances** is auspicious. || 36 ||

<margin>44</margin>
### ## Regarding the Use of Beds and Seats
In Shravana, Anuradha, the Uttaras, Rohini, Mrigashira, the two Punarvasus, Hasta, Chitra, and the three Revatis <note>Revati, Ashwini, Bharani</note>; || 37 || the **enjoyment of beds, seats, and furniture** is recommended on an auspicious day of the week and an auspicious lunar day. || 4 ||

### On the Fashioning of Gem-Set Jewelry
In the three stars beginning with Krittika <note>Krittika, Rohini, and Mrigashirsha</note>, in the five stars of Hasta <note>Hasta, Citra, Svati, Vishakha, and Anuradha</note>, in the two stars of Revati <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>, in the three stars of Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, in Punarvasu, in Pushya, and in the three "Uttara" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>—on a Tuesday or Sunday, the fashioning of jewelry set with gemstones is auspicious. || 39 ||

### On the Making of Silver, Pearl, and Diamond Jewelry
In the same constellations mentioned for gem-set jewelry, but excluding Vishakha and Krittika, and on a Friday, the making of jewelry composed of silver, diamonds, and pearls is beneficial. || 40 ||

### On the Rite of Tonsure (Shaving)
In the three stars of Shravana, the three stars of Hasta, the two stars of Revati, in Jyeshtha, Mrigashirsha, Shravana <note>referred to here as 'Vaishnava'</note>, and the two stars of Punarvasu <note>Punarvasu and Pushya</note> || 41 || the wise have declared the rite of tonsure <term>Kshura-karma: the ritual cutting of hair or shaving</term> to be performed, avoiding Tuesday, Saturday, and Sunday. One should also avoid Anuradha, the latter half of Vishakha, Krittika, Rohini, and Magha. || 42 ||

### On the Effects of the Days of the Week for Tonsure
Shaving on a Sunday destroys one month of life; on Saturday, seven months; and on Tuesday, it destroys the family lineage within eight months. Mercury <note>Wednesday</note> increases life by five months, and the King of the Vaishyas <note>referring to Monday</note> increases it by seven. || 43 || Venus <note>Friday</note> grants eleven months of well-being, and Jupiter <note>Thursday</note> grants ten. || 44 ||

### On the Lunar Days to be Avoided for Tonsure
One should abandon the eighth, the first, and the sixth lunar days, as well as the "Empty" days <note>Rikta-tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th</note> and the transition days <note>Parvan: Full and New Moon</note>. The fourteenth and the New Moon day must be avoided for tonsure at all costs. || 45 ||

### On the Evil Effects of Forbidden Lunar Days and Constellations
If one shaves five times under Magha, six times under Krittika, three times under Anuradha, or seven times under Rohini || 46 || or a fourth time under the constellation named Uttara Phalguni—even if that person is equal to the Creator <note>Brahma</note> in power, they shall not live to see the end of the year. || 47 || <margin>45</margin>

</margin> Regarding the prohibition of shaving, there is a debate concerning the father <note>referring to whether a son may shave while his father is alive</note>. ### On the Shaving of Kings
For a King, the trimming of the beard is prescribed on the fifth day after the rising of the sun, provided that the stars <term>Nakshatras</term> are not in an inauspicious position. || 49 ||

### On the Care of Nails and Teeth
In those lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> where shaving has been declared permissible by the wise, in those same mansions one should also perform the clipping of nails and the cleaning of teeth. || 50 ||

### On the Prohibitions of Shaving
One who has released his hair, one who has been anointed with oil, one observing a vow, one who has set out on a journey, a practitioner of Yoga, or one who has already performed his daily morning rituals; likewise, one wearing a torch or ornaments, or during the night, or during both the morning and evening twilights || 51 ||
One who desires auspiciousness should not have shaving performed on the ninth lunar day. Those who are in the early stages of life, a student who has completed his studies <term>Snataka</term>, a King, a great Yogi, the husband of a pregnant woman, and one whose father is still living—these should not undergo tonsure, even if previously mentioned as permissible under other rules. || 52 ||

### On Auspicious Recitations during Shaving
"The Treasury, the city of Anarta, Pataliputra, or the city of the Kings; Diti and Aditi"—for those who remember these names during the rite of shaving, welfare and prosperity shall follow. || 53 ||

### On the Beginning of Education
In the five stars beginning with Mrigashira, in Mula, in the three beginning with Hasta, in Ardra <note>original: "Shiva"</note>, and in the three beginning with Shravana—the commencement of education is praised. || 54 || On Sunday, Friday, Wednesday, or Thursday, when the rising sign <term>Lagna</term> is strong and auspicious; on the tenth, eleventh, twelfth, sixth, third, or fifth lunar days || 55 || For the beginning of education, Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury grant the fulfillment of all goals. The Sun is middling, the Moon causes dullness <margin>46</margin> <margin>T</margin> of intellect, and Mars and Saturn are remembered as bringers of death. || 56 ||

In the two Shatabhishas, Anuradha, Ardra, Rohini...

One should commence the study of **Mathematics and related sciences** during the constellations of Pushya, under the influence of Jupiter or Mercury. || 57 || **Now, the commencement of Grammar:** A wise person should study the **Science of Words** <note>original: "Shabda-shastra," referring to Sanskrit grammar</note> under Rohini, the five constellations starting from Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, Svati, Vishakha, Anuradha</note>, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Ashwini, and Pushya, on the days of Venus or Jupiter. || 58 ||
**Now, the commencement of Logic and other sciences:** One should study **Logic** <note>original: "Nyaya-shastra"</note> and similar sciences under the three "Uttara" constellations <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Rohini, Pushya, Punarvasu, Shravana, Hasta, Ashwini, Shatabhisha, and Svati. || 59 || **Now, the commencement of the Codes of Law and Ancient Chronicles:** The auspicious beginning of the **Codes of Law** <note>original: "Dharma-shastra"</note> and **Ancient Chronicles** <note>original: "Purana"</note> occurs under the five constellations starting from Hasta, Pushya, the two constellations ending with Revati <note>Revati and Ashwini</note>, Mrigashira, and the three "Uttara" constellations. || 60 || **Now, the commencement of Medical Science and the Science of Toxicology:** Under the three constellations starting from Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, Svati</note>, Anuradha, Punarvasu, the three starting from Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha</note>, Mula, Revati, Ashwini, Pushya, Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, Ardra, and Mrigashira, one may begin **Medical Science** <note>original: "Vaidya-vidya"</note> on Tuesdays or Sundays. Excluding Jyeshtha, these same timings apply to the **Science of Toxicology** <note>original: "Garudi-vidya," specifically charms and medicines against snake-bite</note>. || 61 || **Now, the commencement of Jaina Thought:** One should study the **Jaina Scriptures** <note>original: "Jaina-agama"</note> under the three "Uttara" constellations, Magha, Purva Phalguni, Anuradha, the three starting from Revati <note>Revati, Ashwini, Bharani</note>, Punarvasu, and Svati, on the days of the Sun or Venus. || 62 || **Now, the commencement of Persian and Turkish Sciences:** Under Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, Magha, Purva Phalguni, Revati, the two starting from Bharani <note>Bharani and Krittika</note>, Vishakha, Ardra, Uttara Ashadha, and Shatabhisha, on the days of malefic planets <note>such as Saturday or Tuesday</note>, || 63 || and when the Ascendant is a fixed sign and contains the Moon, one should study **Persian and Arabic** <note>original: "Farasi" and "Marabi" (likely a corruption of Arabi)</note>. || 64 || **Now, the commencement of Writing:** On an auspicious lunar day and an auspicious weekday, under the two constellations of Revati <note>Revati and Ashwini</note>, Shravana, Anuradha, the three starting from Ardra <note>Ardra, Punarvasu, Pushya</note>, and the three starting from Hasta,

### On the Commencement of the Arts and Crafts

<note>Shilpa-vidya refers to the study of arts, architecture, and manual crafts.</note>
Under the three "Hand" stars <note>Hasta, Citra, Svati</note>, the Ear <note>Shravana</note>, the three "Latter" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, the Stag's Head <note>Mrigashirsha</note>, the Revati, the Horseman <note>Ashvini</note>, the Nourisher <note>Pushya</note>, the Two Restorers <note>Punarvasu</note>, and the Success-bringer <note>Anuradha</note>; on a praised lunar day and an auspicious weekday, one should commence the study of the arts and crafts. || 67 ||

### On Visiting the King

<note>Rajadarshanam: Seeking an audience with a ruler or high official.</note>
Under the three "Latter" stars, the Ear, the Twins <note>Punarvasu</note>, the Stag's Head, the Nourisher, the Success-bringer, the Red One <note>Rohini</note>, the Wealthy <note>Revati</note>, the Chitra, and the Hand; on an auspicious day when the Sun is strong, or even on a Sunday, visiting the King is declared favorable. || 68 ||

### On Royal Service

<note>Raja-seva: Entering the employment of a monarch or the state.</note>
Under the two "Hand" stars <note>Hasta and Citra</note>, the Success-bringer, the Wealthy, the Twins, the Stag's Head, and the Nourisher; on a Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday; on a virtuous lunar day or a Sunday; || 69 || when there is friendship between the zodiac signs of the servant and the master, the master should be served by his followers. || 70 ||

### On the Acquisition of Servants

Under the "Latter" stars and the Red One <note>Rohini</note>, the acquisition of female servants is always recommended. || 71 ||

### On the King’s Use of the Umbrella, Fly-whisk, and Throne

The fly-whisk, the parasol, the palanquin, and likewise the throne and the royal consecration—all these should be performed on an auspicious day. || 72 ||

### On the Making of Seals

<note>Mudrakaranam: The engraving of official signet rings or administrative seals.</note>
Under the three "Latter" stars, the Success-bringer, the three "Ear" stars <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha</note>, the two "Restorers," the three "Hand" stars, the Wealthy, || 73 || the Stag's Head, and the Red One; on the eighth, fifth, third, thirteenth, tenth, or full moon day; || 74 || excluding Saturdays and Mondays, the making of seals is highly praised. || 75 ||

Under the Wealthy, the three "Hand" stars, the three "Ear" stars, the Stag's Head, and the two "Restorers"; on days other than Saturday and Tuesday, one should mount elephants, horses, chariots, and the like—

### Regarding the Mounting of the Palanquin and its Rituals
The mounting of a palanquin <note>A covered litter or sedan chair carried by poles on the shoulders of attendants</note> and its construction should be done during a favorable astrological rising, specifically under the constellations of Uttara-Phalguni, Revati, the pair of Ashvini and Bharani, the three constellations beginning with Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Svati</note>, the three beginning with Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, Punarvasu, Pushya, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, or Mrigashira. || 77 ||

### Regarding <term>Elephant Rituals</term> <note>Gaja Krityam: The care, training, and ceremonial use of royal elephants</note>
The three constellations beginning with Shravana, the three beginning with Hasta, the two beginning with Revati <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>, Mrigashira, Pushya, Anuradha, Rohini, and the three Uttaras <note>Uttara-Phalguni, Uttara-Ashadha, and Uttara-Bhadrapada</note>... || 78 || ...and Punarvasu are favorable. On an auspicious day of the week, or even on a Saturday, activities related to elephants may be performed. || 79 ||

### Regarding the <term>Elephant Goad</term> <note>Gajasyankushah: The metal hook or 'Ankusha' used by a mahout to guide an elephant</note>
The goads for elephants should be fitted or applied during a Saturnine astrological rising <note>Capricorn or Aquarius</note> or on a Saturday. || 80 ||

### Regarding Horse Rituals
On an auspicious day, under the constellations of Hasta, Dhanishta, the pair of Revati and Ashvini, the pair of Punarvasu and Pushya, Svati, Mrigashira, or Shatabhisha—excluding Tuesdays and the "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unlucky for new ventures</note>—work related to horses is highly beneficial. || 81 ||

### Regarding the Manufacture of Saddles and Equipment
Under Shravana, Shatabhisha, Hasta, Pushya, Mula, Mrigashira, Ashvini, or Punarvasu, the making of saddles for elephants, horses, and camels is auspicious. || 82 ||

### Special Duties Regarding Horses
Rituals concerning the voice or neighing are as described in the section on tonsure <note>referring to the 'Chaula' or purification rites</note>; training and education are as stated in the pedagogical texts; and matters of fodder and feeding for the horses of the Sindh region <note>Saindhava: famous breeds from the Indus valley</note> are as described in the sections on diet. || 83 ||

### Regarding <term>Chariot Rituals</term> <note>Ratha Krityam: The construction and consecration of royal war or ceremonial chariots</note>
Under Pushya, Punarvasu, Ashlesha, Anuradha, the pair of Revati and Ashvini, the three beginning with Shravana, the three beginning with Hasta, Rohini, or Mrigashira... || 84 || ...on a Sunday, Wednesday, or Thursday, and during a favorable astrological rising, work on a chariot is excellent. || 85 || <margin>46</margin>

### Regarding the Taking Up of Weapons
Under the constellations of Krittika or Vishakha, on a Tuesday, Sunday, or Saturday, and during a favorable rising, weapons should be forged or taken up by kings...

### On the Wearing of Weapons
Under the two Punarvasus <note>the stars Castor and Pollux</note>, Hasta, Chitra, the two Rohinis, the three beginning with Vishakha, the three Uttaras, and the two Revatis || 87 || on any lunar day except the "Empty" days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered inauspicious for new beginnings</note>, and on the days of the Sun, Venus, or Jupiter: one should perform the first donning of armor, daggers, swords, spears, and other such weapons. || 88 ||

### On Beginning the Science of Archery
Under the stars Anuradha, Magha, Pushya, or Mrigashira, on the eighth or twelfth lunar day, and on an auspicious day of the week, one should commence the study of <term>Archery (Dhanurvidya)</term> <note>the traditional science of the bow and general military training</note>. || 89 ||

### On the Practice of Spears and All Other Weapons
Under the three Hastas, the three Uttaras, Ashvini, Punarvasu, and Pushya, on an auspicious day and under a wise <note>favorable</note> planetary alignment, one should practice with the sword, the <term>Spear (Kunta)</term>, and other arms. || 90 ||

### On the Construction and Wearing of Fire-Weapons
Under Vishakha, Krittika, the Purvas, Magha, Ashlesha, Ashvini, Mrigashira, Mula, Ardra, Bharani, or Jyeshtha, on the day of Jupiter or on a "cruel" day <note>Tuesday or Saturday</note>, the construction and the wearing of <term>Fire-Weapons (Agni Shastra)</term> <note>likely referring to incendiary devices or early pyrotechnics</note> is declared auspicious. || 91 ||

### On the Use of the Fire-Weapon Called the Nalika
Under Ashlesha, Krittika, Mula, Jyeshtha, Ardra, Bharani, Ashvini, the Purvas, the four Hastas, Revati, Punarvasu, and the two Maghas || 92 || on the "Empty" or "Victory" lunar days, and on the days of the malefic planets, the ritual use of the <term>Nalika (Nalikastra)</term> <note>a tube-shaped weapon, often interpreted as an early form of a gun or a blowpipe for incendiaries</note> is auspicious. || 93 ||

### On the Binding and Striking of Enemies and Thieves
Under Jyeshtha, Ardra, Bharani, the Purvas, Mula, Ashlesha, and the two Maghas, when the Ascendant is joined by a malefic planet, and on the days of "cruel" planets <note>Mars or Saturn</note>, one should perform the binding of enemies and the striking of their hair <note>a symbolic or physical act of humiliation and defeat</note>. || 94 ||

### On Peace Treaties with the Enemy
Under Anuradha, Magha, or Pushya, during the half-day known as Taitila, when the Ascendant is under a favorable aspect on the eighth or twelfth lunar day, a <term>Peace Treaty (Sandhi)</term> is desired. || 95 ||

### On the Commencement of Ponds, Wells, and Tanks

### On Planting Trees and Vines and Garden Work
The mansions of Hasta, Chitra, the Uttaras, Mula, Anuradha, the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>, Vishakha, Rohini, and Pushya are auspicious for the <term>Aramopti</term> <note>the planting of a grove or garden</note> and for Mrigashira. // 208 //

The entry and exit of animals should avoid the mansion of one's own birth. // 111 //

### On Camels, Buffaloes, and Related Tasks
In the two Dhanishthas, Purvashadha, and the "sideways-facing" mansions <note>Nakshatras whose influence is directed horizontally</note>, one should perform tasks related to goats, sheep, buffaloes, camels, mules, and dogs. // 112 //

<margin>Mu. Ga. 54</margin> **The Application of Wealth** <note>original: "dravyayoga"; refers to the auspicious timing for financial transactions or investments</note> || In the three constellations starting with Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, the four starting with Chitra <note>Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, and Anuradha</note>, the two of Revati <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>, and especially in Punarvasu and Mrigashira, the application of wealth is auspicious. || 124 ||

During a solar transit <note>Sankranti; when the sun moves into a new zodiac sign</note>, or during the Yoga of Increase <note>Vriddhi Yoga</note>, or when the moon is in the constellation of Hasta on a Sunday or Tuesday, one should never take a loan; for if one does, that debt will remain fixed in one's lineage <note>meaning it will be impossible to pay off and may haunt future generations</note>. Debt should be cleared at such times, but the wise should certainly not give away their wealth then. || 125 ||

**Now, the Sale of Grain and Other Goods** || The sale of grain is recommended in Rohini, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha. || 126 ||

**Now, the Collection of Liquids** <note>original: "rasa sangraha"; refers to gathering juices, oils, or perhaps mercury for medicinal/alchemical use</note> || The best time for collecting liquids is said to be the same as the commencement of water-related works. || 127 ||

**Now, the Commencement of Plowing** || In the two Punarvasus, Mula, the three Uttaras, the two Rohinis <note>Rohini and Mrigashira</note>, the three Hastas <note>Hasta, Chitra, Swati</note>, Anuradha, Revati, and the three Shravanas—on auspicious lunar days and weekdays—the commencement of plowing is auspicious. || 129 ||

**Now, the Plowing-Wheel Chart based on the Sun's Occupied Constellation** || From the constellation occupied by the Sun, count 3, 8, 11, and 9 <note>totaling 27/28</note> in the wheel. The constellations not yet reached by the Sun are considered auspicious in the sequence of the Plowing-Wheel. || 130 ||

**Now, the Constellation for Sowing Seeds based on Rahu's Constellation** <note>Rahu is the North Node of the Moon, considered a "shadow planet"</note> || In the three Hastas, especially Magha, the two Uttaras, the two Rohinis, the pair of Dhanishta and Revati, as well as Mula and Mrigashira || 131 || on auspicious weekdays and lunar days, the sowing of seeds is done. Now, counting from the constellation occupied by Rahu: the 8th, 11th, 12th, <margin>54</margin> <margin>08</margin> 15th, 16th, 18th, 20th, 22nd, and 27th constellations || 132 || are known to be inauspicious and auspicious respectively in the sequence of the day-star of the Serpent <note>Rahu</note>.

### On the Planting of Crops

When the Moon resides in the constellations of Hasta, the three "Uttaras" <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Mula, Dhanishtha, Rohini, or Mrigashirsha; or in Pushya, Ashvini, and Anuradha; on a "Jaya" <note>the 3rd, 8th, or 13th lunar days</note> or any other auspicious day of the week || 134 || excluding the "Rikta" <note>empty/inauspicious</note> lunar days and the days of Saturn and Mars—these are the times for the sowing and planting of crops. || 135 ||

### On the Sowing and Watering of Sugarcane, Creepers, and Others

During the times prescribed for planting crops, but excluding the day of the Sun <note>Sunday</note> and the constellations of Magha and Hasta, the watering of sugarcane, grain crops, and creepers is highly recommended. || 136 ||

### On the Harvesting of Grain

In the constellations of Purva-Phalguni, the three Uttaras, Magha, Jyeshtha, Ashvini, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Bharani, Krittika, Mula, Mrigashirsha, Pushya, and the three "Hasta" constellations <note>Hasta, Chitra, Svati</note>; the harvesting of grain is auspicious, provided one avoids the "Rikta" lunar days and the days of Mars and Saturn. || 137 ||

### On the Threshing of Grain

In the constellations of Anuradha, Ashvini, Mula, Revati, Magha, Purva-Phalguni, Jyeshtha, and Rohini, the threshing of grain <term>Kara-mardanam: the process of beating or treading grain to separate it from the husk</term> shall be auspicious. || 138 ||

### On Bringing Home the Harvest and the Best of Fruits

Under the same constellations mentioned for planting, and specifically on the days of Saturn and Mars, the bringing home of the harvest is considered primary and excellent, especially for fruits and the "Uttara" varieties of produce. || 139 ||

### On the Cooking of Pulses, Grains, and Other Foods

In Mula, Chitra, Anuradha, Vishakha, Krittika, Mrigashirsha, the three Uttaras, Rohini, Jyeshtha, and Revati, the preparation of food is favored. || 140 || One should avoid the Moon in a "movable" sign <note>Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn</note>, the sixth division of a sign, the sixth lunar day, and the day of Saturn. || 141 ||

### On the Ritual of New Grain and the Joy of Eating New Fruits and Roots

<margin>55</margin>
In Hasta, Chitra, Anuradha, Revati, Rohini, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Mrigashirsha, Ashvini, Shravistha <note>Dhanishtha</note>, and the three Uttaras; on a Sunday or any other auspicious day and lunar date; the ritual of the first fruits <term>Navanna: the ceremony of eating the first grains of the harvest</term> should be performed.

### On the Storage of Grain in Granaries
When the Moon is in Punarvasu, the end of Mrigashira, Anuradha, the three stars of Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, the three stars of Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, Ardra, Magha, Rohini, or the three Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> || 143 || and on the days of Jupiter, Venus, the Sun, or the Moon, it is auspicious to store grain in a granary or storehouse. || 144 ||

### On the Collection of Seeds
In the three stars of Hasta, Punarvasu, Rohini, or the three stars of Shravana; during a fixed <term>Lagna</term> <note>an astrological sign that does not move quickly, providing stability</note>, on an auspicious day of the week, and when the Moon is strong, one should collect seeds. || 145 ||

### On Binding Grain with Grass and Rope
In Swati, Mula, the end of Hasta, the two Bhadrapadas, Mrigashira, or Rohini, it is auspicious to protect and bind the grain within the granary. || 146 ||

### On Religious Activities
In the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashwini</note>, the three Hastas, the two Rohinis <note>Rohini and Mrigashira</note>, Shravana, the Uttaras, Punarvasu, or Anuradha || 147 || on the days of Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, the Moon, or the Sun, and when the <term>Lagna</term> is in the division of Mercury or Jupiter, and when Jupiter is present or strong, one should perform religious deeds. || 148 ||

### On Rites for Peace and Prosperity
In the two Punarvasus, Swati, the Uttaras, the three Shravanas, the two Revatis, Hasta, Anuradha, or the two Rohinis; on an auspicious day, the wise declare that <term>Shantika</term> <note>rites to remove obstacles</note> and <term>Paushtika</term> <note>rites to increase prosperity</note> rituals should be performed. || 149 ||

### On the Results of Planetary Influence during Fire Offerings
Counting from the Sun's Nakshatra: 3 for the Sun, 6 for Mercury, 9 for Venus, 12 for Saturn, 15 for the Moon, 18 for Mars, 21 for Rahu, 24 for Jupiter, and 27 for Ketu. These should be known in groups of three Nakshatras respectively. || 150 || <margin>56</margin> <margin>05</margin> A fire offering is unfavorable or yields bad results if the planet is malefic. || 151 ||

### On the Residence of Fire during Rituals
Add the date <note>Tithi</note> and the day of the week together, add one, and divide by four...

...divide [the sum]. If the remainder is one, the fire resides in the heavens; if zero, in the atmosphere; and if two, on the earth. Fire in the heavens is not desired, and a remainder of one or two [in other contexts] may bring misfortune. || 152 ||

**Regarding Initiation into Mantras and Other Rites**
Initiation into a <term>Mantra</term><note>A sacred utterance or chant used in meditation and ritual</note> is auspicious on the days prescribed for the <term>Maunji Bandhana</term><note>The sacred thread ceremony marking the beginning of Vedic study</note>, such as under the lunar mansions of Rohini or the three "Utara" stars. Initiation is also highly recommended during an eclipse as stated in the <term>Agamas</term><note>Traditional doctrinal texts or scriptures</note>. || 153 ||

**The Astrological Chart for Initiation**
According to the *Brihan-Narayana*: When the Sun is in the eleventh house from the birth planet, and the Moon is in the third, eleventh, or sixth house; when Mercury and Mars are in the houses of increase <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses</note>; and when Jupiter is in a center or a trine—these are auspicious. Venus is favorable in the 6th, 3rd, 9th, 12th, 4th, 8th, 5th, or 11th, and Saturn is favorable in the 11th. The influence of Jupiter, Mercury, the Moon, and the Sun in the rising sign is preferred for the rite of <term>Diksha</term><note>Formal initiation by a teacher or Guru</note>. || 154 ||

**The Cycle for Receiving Mantras**
The 4th, 6th (Ashwini), 14th, 17th, 21st, and 27th [lunar days or mansions] are considered excellent in the cycle for receiving a Mantra. || 155 ||

**Regarding Mantras, Yantras, and Vows**
On days when the Sun is in an auspicious position, and under the stars of Hasta, Pushya, Ashwini, Shravana, Vishakha, or Mrigashira, it is best to perform rites involving <term>Mantras</term>, <term>Yantras</term><note>Mystical diagrams used as aids in worship</note>, <term>Vratas</term><note>Religious vows or observances</note>, and fasting. || 156 ||

**The Mastery of Heroic Spirits**
Under the stars of Magha, Ardra, Bharani, or Mula, when the Moon is in Leo, on a Wednesday, and when Venus is in the fourth house and the eighth house is pure—this is the time for the <term>Vira-Vetala Sadhana</term><note>A specialized ritual practice to gain mastery over powerful spirits or 'heroic' entities</note>. || 157 ||

**The Preparation and Consumption of Medicine**
Under the three Hasta stars, Anuradha, Mula, Pushya, the three Shravana stars, Mrigashira, Revati, or Punarvasu, and especially on one's birth star; || 158 ||
<margin>57</margin>
On the days of the Moon (Monday), Venus (Friday), Jupiter (Thursday), or the Sun (Sunday), during an auspicious lunar day and in a "dual-natured" rising sign <note>Signs like Gemini or Virgo</note>, provided the 7th, 8th, and 12th houses are astrologically pure—taking medicine then is said to be beneficial and life-prolonging. || 159 ||

In the mansion of <term>Magha</term> <note>The "Mighty" star, Regulus</note>, the illness lasts seven days; in <term>Bharani</term>, <term>Chitra</term>, and <term>Shravana</term>, it lasts twenty days. In <term>Shatabhisha</term>, it lasts eleven days. || 91 ||

### On the Inauspicious Results of the Onset of Disease
If a disease begins under the three <term>Purvas</term> <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, or under <term>Ashlesha</term>, <term>Jyeshtha</term>, <term>Ardra</term>, or <term>Swati</term>, the death of that person is certain. || 170 ||

### On Inauspicious Combinations at the Onset of Disease
If a fever begins under <term>Ashlesha</term>, <term>Bharani</term>, <term>Swati</term>, or <term>Purva Bhadrapada</term>, and if this coincides with a "malefic" weekday <note>Tuesday or Saturday</note> or on the first, twelfth, fourteenth, seventh, or full moon day of the lunar cycle, that man will meet death, even if protected by his own physician. || 171–172 ||

### Knowledge of Afflictions from the Ascendant at the Time of Inquiry
<note>This section uses "Prashna Lagna," the zodiac sign rising at the moment a patient asks a question, to diagnose the cause of illness.</note>
If the inquiry occurs in **Aries** (<term>Mesha</term>), there is an imbalance of the <term>Pitta</term> <note>The "fire" humor or bile</note> and the body loses its natural color.
In **Taurus** (<term>Vrisha</term>), the affliction is caused by a household deity or spirit; symptoms include bad dreams, eye disease, and fever. || 173 ||
In **Gemini** (<term>Dvandva</term>), the affliction arises from the Great Illusion (<term>Mahamaya</term>), involving erratic winds <note>Vata humor</note> and fever.
In **Cancer** (<term>Karkata</term>), the fault lies with a <term>Shakini</term> <note>A type of female forest spirit or demoness</note>, causing silence, sudden laughter, and weeping. || 174 ||
In **Leo** (<term>Simha</term>), the affliction is caused by a <term>Preta</term> <note>The restless ghost of a deceased person</note>, resulting in depression and cold-fever (chills).
In **Virgo** (<term>Kanya</term>), the fault is caused by the planets (<term>Kheta</term>), resulting in pain, anger, and loss of appetite. || 175 ||
In **Libra** (<term>Tula</term>), the fault lies with the <term>Kshetrapala</term> <note>The guardian deity of the land</note>, causing suffering to one's offspring.
In **Scorpio** (<term>Alibhe</term>), the fault is caused by a <term>Naga</term> <note>Serpent spirit</note>, causing a burning sensation in the body and the destruction of the intellect. || 176 ||

The affliction <note>original: "dosha"; refers here to the spiritual or humoral cause of suffering</note> arising in **Sagittarius** <note>original: "dhanu"</note> results in fever, sorrow, and abdominal pain. In **Capricorn** <note>original: "mriga"</note>, it manifests as a breakdown of the body or a fever born of wind <note>original: "anilajo"; likely referring to Vata imbalance in Ayurvedic terms</note>. || 137 ||

### Knowledge of Afflictions based on Planets in the Query Chart

If the **Sun** is in the 1st, 2nd, or 8th house, the affliction arises from the *Kshetrapala* <note>a protective deity of the land or "Field-Guardian"</note>. If **Mercury** is in the 1st, 6th, 8th, or 12th house, the affliction originates from a Goddess. || 139 ||
If **Mars** is in the 11th or 12th house, the affliction is caused by a *Shakini* <note>a type of female forest demon or spirit</note> or a messenger. If **Mercury** <note>noted as "Saumya"</note> is in the 7th or 12th house, it arises from a Forest Goddess. || 180 ||
If **Jupiter** <note>original: "Chitra Shikhandije"</note> is in the 7th or 12th house, the affliction is from the Gods. If **Venus** is in the 7th or 12th house, the affliction arises from a Brahmin deity <note>original: "bhudeva"; literally an earthly god or priest</note>. || 181 ||
If **Saturn** is in the 7th or 12th house, the affliction is physical and caused by wind. If **Rahu** <note>original: "Simhika-nandane"; the son of Simhika</note> is in the 7th or 12th house, the affliction is caused by a ghost. || 182 ||
If malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, 8th, or 12th houses from the Ascendant, the affliction involves a death in one's own family caused by poison, weapons, or water. || 183 ||
If the **Sun** is involved, the death was by fire; if **Mars**, by weapons; if **Saturn**, by waves of water; if **Rahu**, by disease. These afflictions occur when the planets are in the *Apoklima* <note>the 3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th houses</note> positions. || 184 ||
If **Mercury** is in the 10th house, the affliction is from the Goddess of the Path; if **Jupiter**, from a ghost; if **Venus**, from the Gods; and if a **malefic planet**, from a wicked *Shakini*. || 185 ||
If the planet is in its own <margin>*</margin> sign, the affliction is from one's own lineage; if in an even sign, it is from another family. || 186 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological afflictions and their remedies, following the previous section on planetary influences. The focus here shifts to specific spiritual causes of illness and the prognosis for recovery.</meta>

<margin>Mercury and Ganesha</margin> <note>original: "bu. ga." - likely abbreviations for Budha (Mercury) and Ganesha, invoked for wisdom and the removal of obstacles.</note>
Afflictions arising from an evil eye <note>or "malefic gaze"</note> are caused by the Sun; bile disorders <term>Pitta-dosha; an imbalance of the fire element in the body</term> are caused by the Moon. The influence of female spirits <term>Shakini; a class of fierce female spirits or forest deities</term> is caused by Mars, while the influence of the Sky Goddess arises from Mercury. 187.

The influence of the Clan Goddess <term>Gotra-devi; a tutelary deity protecting a specific family lineage</term> arises from Jupiter, while that of the Water Goddess arises from Venus. Suffering caused by restless spirits <term>Preta-pida; afflictions from the spirits of the deceased who have not moved on</term> is caused by Saturn. All of these are pacified through the performance of peace-making rituals <term>Shanti; rites intended to calm malefic influences</term>. 188.

Afflictions are curable <term>Sadhya</term> when the Moon is strong and positioned in its own zodiac sign or its sign of exaltation. They are incurable <term>Asadhya</term> when the planet Mercury is in its sign of debilitation or situated in the house of an enemy. 189.

Afflictions are curable when gentle planets <note>benefics like Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury</note> are strong and positioned in the center houses <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart</note>. They are incurable when cruel planets <note>malefics like Saturn or Mars</note> are positioned in the center houses and lack strength. 190.

To pacify a specific affliction, one should perform worship dedicated to the deity causing that affliction. For the Water Goddess, an offering should be cast into the water; for the Sky Goddess, an offering should be made in an open space. 191.

In cases of afflictions caused by *Shakini*, *Dakini* <note>classes of female spirits</note>, or ghosts, the offering should be placed at a crossroads. For afflictions of the heart <note>or internal distress</note>, the offering should be carried to a crossroads with that specific intent. 192.

When the affliction arises from the Clan Goddess, one should worship the Family Deity. If the affliction is caused by the ancestors <term>Pitri-dosha</term>, the ritual of the "Offering to Narayana" <note>Narayana Bali; a specific Vedic ritual to appease ancestral spirits and end bad luck</note> must be performed. 193.

One should also perform the ancestral shraddha ceremony known as the "Three-Pinda" rite and offer libations according to the prescribed rules. 194.

**Thus ends the knowledge of afflictions. Now, the unfavorable signs regarding snake bites.**
If a person is bitten by a snake during the constellations <term>Nakshatras</term> of Vishakha, Krittika, Mula, Revati, or Ashlesha, they will not survive. 195.

**Now, the ritual bath after recovery from illness.**
One who has been freed from illness should bathe under the constellations of Magha, Punarvasu, Swati, Rohini, the three "Uttara" signs <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Ashlesha, or Revati, specifically on a Friday or a Monday. 196.

One should not bathe <insert>when recovering</insert> during the "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta Tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, at night, on a Tuesday or Sunday, or when the Ascendant is in a "Movable" sign <note>signs like Aries or Cancer that signify changeability</note>, even if the Moon is otherwise favorable. 197.

Pra. 62 <note>Abbreviation for the source text title and page number.</note></margin>If the Moon is poorly positioned, or during the period of *Vishti* <note>an inauspicious astrological timing or 'Karana'</note>, or on a day corrupted by *Pata* <note>astronomical disturbances</note> and other evils, a person who has just recovered from illness should still bathe, but must perform acts of charity immediately afterward.

200. **Now, the bath for the Holi festival:** Even if a sacrifice was performed previously, when the Moon and the lunar mansion are favorable and the lunar day is auspicious, the people should bathe after the <term>Holi (Holika)</term> <note>the spring festival of colors</note> on a day other than Tuesday or Sunday. 201. **Now, athletic activities and wrestling:** Under the lunar mansions of Jyeshtha, Ardra, Bharani, the three Purvas, Mula, Ashlesha, and Magha; 202. In a dual or movable ascendant, when the first house is strong and the stars are favorable; under the influence of Jupiter and the lunar mansions of Shravana, Jyeshtha, Punarvasu, or Shatabhisha; 205. ### On the Taming of Horses and Animals
In the lunar mansions of Krittika, Revati, Pushya, Swati, Hasta, and the two Shravanas <note>Shravana and Dhanishta</note>; or in Mrigashira, or on a Sunday; and during the solar transit into the sign of Leo, the taming of animals and horses should be performed. // 207 //

### On the Construction of Bridges
Under the three "Uttara" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Rohini, Swati, Mrigashira, or on a Sunday, Tuesday, or Thursday; the binding of bridges is praised when performed during an auspicious Ascendant and a favorable lunar phase. // 208 //

### On Salt Production
The commencement of work related to salt is best on Bharani, Rohini, or Shravana; specifically on a Saturday during the daytime, and when Saturn is strong in relation to one's birth sign. // 209 //

### On the Activities of Jains, Charvakas, and Sectarians
Under the "fierce" mansions <note>Ugra Nakshatras</note>, Ardra, Mrigashira, Swati, Punarvasu, or the three Shravanas; on the "Jaya" or "Purna" lunar days; or on a Friday, Wednesday, or at sunrise; the activities of the Charvakas <note>materialist philosophers</note>, Jains, and sectarian groups are auspiciously performed. // 210 //

### On the Work of Actors and Dancers
In Chitra, Ardra, Rohini, Pushya, the three "Uttaras," or the three Shravanas; on an auspicious day or a Sunday, the work of actors and dancers is said to be favored. // 211 //

### On the Work of Oil-Pressers
In Dhanishta, Ardra, Hasta, Chitra, Anuradha, Pushya, Jyeshtha, Punarvasu, or Revati; these are favorable on an auspicious weekday. // 212 //

### On the Work of Potters
In the two Punarvasus, the three Hastas, Revati, Rohini, Mrigashira, Anuradha, Shravana, or Jyeshtha; on a Sunday or Wednesday, and at sunrise, the activities of the potter are proclaimed by the wise. // 213 //

### On Woodworking and Carpentry
In the six mansions beginning with Hasta, Ardra, Pushya, Revati, the three Shravanas, Punarvasu, or the two Rohinis; the work of the architect or carpenter <term>Sutradhara: literally 'the holder of the string'</term> is most excellent. // 214 //

**On Professional Duties** || When the Moon is in the three constellations of Shravana <note>the Ear</note>, Ashwini, Pushya, Mrigashirsha, the four constellations of Hasta, Krittika, or Punarvasu, and during an auspicious Ascendant <term>Lagna: the rising sign on the eastern horizon</term> or lunar day:

<margin>Mu. Ga. 64</margin> The work of the **Goldsmith** is recommended, provided one avoids Wednesdays and Saturdays. || 215 || **Now, the works concerning Metals, Stones, and Gems** || In the constellations of Swati, the two Jyeshthas <note>Jyeshtha and the one following it, Mula</note>, Mula, Chitra, Ardra, and the three Bharanis <note>the triad of Bharani, Krittika, and Rohini</note>, the working of gems, metals, and stones should be done on a day ruled by a "malefic" planet <note>such as Mars or Saturn</note> during a "fixed" Ascendant. || 216 ||

The auspicious Ascendant has a strength of a thousand points; the Moon is strong with a hundred points; the Birth Star <note>Tara</note> has sixty points; the Yoga <note>astrological union</note> has thirty-two points. <margin>64</margin> || 221 || Half of that <note>sixteen points</note> should be known for the Karana; the Weekday is remembered to have eight points; the Constellation <note>Nakshatra</note> should be known to have four points; and the Lunar Day <note>Tithi</note> is remembered to have only one point. || 222 || Thus ends [this section] in the *Muhurta Ganapati* composed by Ganapati, the son of the illustrious astrologer Raval Harishankar Suri.

The chapter concerning the auspicious timings <note>original: "Muhurtas"</note> for various tasks under the lordship of Lord Ganesha is now concluded. || 21 ||

## Now, the Chapter on the Sun's Transit <note>original: "Sankranti"</note> ##

If the Sun's transit occurs on a Sunday, and falls under the constellations <note>original: "Nakshatras"</note> of Bharani, Mrigashira, or the three "Purvas" <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, it is named *Ghora*. It is known to bring happiness to the Shudra class <note>the laboring class</note>. || 1 ||

On a Monday, if it falls in Abhijit, Pushya, Ashwini, or Hasta, the transit is called *Dhvankshi*. It is said to bestow happiness upon the Vaishya class <note>the merchant and agricultural class</note>. || 2 ||

On a Tuesday, if it occurs in the three constellations starting with Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha</note>, or in Swati or Punarvasu, it is named *Mahodari*. It grants success to thieves and outlaws. || 3 ||

On a Wednesday, if it occurs in Revati, Mrigashira, Chitra, or Anuradha, it is named *Mandakini*. It bestows happiness and prosperity upon kings. || 4 ||

When it occurs on a Thursday in Rohini or the three "Uttaras" <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, it is known as *Manda*. It is beneficial to the Brahmin class <note>the priestly and scholarly class</note>. || 5 ||

On a Friday, if it occurs in Vishakha or Krittika, it is famous as *Mishra*. It brings contentment to animals and livestock. || 6 ||

On a Saturday, if it occurs in Mula, Ardra, Ashlesha, or Jyeshtha, it is named *Rakshasi*. It brings joy to those of demonic or cruel nature. || 7 ||

If the transit occurs in the first third of the day, it harms kings; in the second part, it harms Brahmins; in the third part, it harms Vaishyas; and at the end of the day, it harms the Shudra class. || 8 ||

During the successive watches <note>original: "Yamas"</note> of the night, it harms ghosts, demons, and men respectively. In the final watch, it harms herdsmen, and at dawn, it harms those of all religious marks <note>original: "Linginas," referring to ascetics or practitioners of all sects</note>. || 9 ||

## Now, Other Designations ##

When the Sun enters Scorpio, Taurus, <margin>65</margin> Leo, or Aquarius, the transit is known as *Vishnupadi*. When it enters Pisces, Virgo, Gemini, or Sagittarius, it is known as *Shadashitimukha*. || 10 ||

It also defines specific technical names for transits into fixed and dual zodiac signs.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing from the previous section, focusing specifically on the calculation of auspicious windows for solar transits.</meta>

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati
66</margin>

It is declared that the Southern Path <note>original: "yamyayana," the summer solstice</note> begins in Cancer, and the Northern Path <note>original: "uttarayana," the winter solstice</note> begins in Capricorn. The transits into Libra (7) and Aries (1) are known as the Equinoxes <note>original: "vishuva"</note>. || 21 || **Now, the Auspicious Time:** Sixteen ghatikas <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> before and after a transit are considered auspicious. However, for the transit into Cancer, the twenty ghatikas prior are auspicious; for the transit into Capricorn, the forty ghatikas following it are auspicious. || 22 || If a transit occurs after midday but before midnight, the auspicious time falls on that same day. If it occurs after midnight, the auspicious time falls on the following day before midday. || 23 || If the transit occurs exactly at midnight, the auspicious time is divided between the previous and the following day. || 24 || **Now, the Specific Rule for the Solstices:** If the Sun's transit into Capricorn occurs at night after sunset, then the auspicious time is proclaimed to be on the following day before midday. || 25 || When the Sun transits into Cancer before sunrise, the auspicious time is on the preceding day; if it occurs after sunrise, it is on the following day. || 26 || **Further Specifics:** If the transit into Capricorn occurs within three ghatikas <note>approximately 72 minutes</note> after sunset, the auspicious time may fall on the previous day; otherwise, it is the next day. || 27 || If the transit into Cancer occurs within three ghatikas before sunrise, then the auspicious time is joined with the following day. || 18 <note>Note: The verse numbering in the original manuscript fluctuates here</note> || In the Vishnu-pada <note>fixed signs: Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius</note> transits, the beginning is auspicious; in the Equinoxes, the middle is auspicious; in the Shadasiti-mukha <note>mutable signs: Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces</note> and the Northern Path, the time following the transit is auspicious. || 19 || **Now, the Tropical Transit:** When the transit of the Tropical Sun <note>original: "sayana-arka," the sun's position adjusted for the precession of the equinoxes</note> occurs, there is even greater merit; this is a secret beneficial to the learned. <margin>66</margin> || 20 || **Now, the Transit Muhurtas and Their Fruits:** Punarvasu, Vishakha, Rohini, and the Uttaras <note>referring to the three lunar mansions named Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>...

<summary>This page details the precise timing for "Punya Kala" (auspicious periods) during solar transits, specifically distinguishing between the summer and winter solstices and the equinoxes. It provides complex rules for determining which day holds the religious merit based on whether the transit occurs during the day, at night, or near twilight.</summary>

There is prosperity <term>Subhiksha; literally "good alms," meaning a time of plenty</term> when the Sun's transition occurs within 5 or 4 <term>Muhurtas; a unit of time equal to 48 minutes</term>. || 21 || If the transition happens during the constellations of Bharani, Ardra, Swati, or Jyeshtha—which are considered inferior stars—there will be famine <term>Durbhiksha; literally "difficult alms"</term> if the duration lasts 15 Muhurtas. || 22 || For the remaining constellations, the transition is said to bring moderate prices <note>original: "ardha samyatam," meaning a balance in the cost of goods</note>. Here, the duration is stated as thirty Muhurtas; the same result applies to the rising of the Moon. || 23 ||

**Now, the Twenty-Point Value of the Year**
The annual value <term>Vimshopaka; a 20-point scale used in Vedic astrology to measure the strength or impact of a year</term> is 10 when the Sun enters Cancer on a Sunday. It is 20 when on a Monday, 8 on a Tuesday, 12 on a Wednesday, 5 on a Saturday, 18 on a Friday, and 18 on a Thursday. || 24 ||

**Now, the Postures of the Transition (Standing, Sitting, Sleeping) and Their Results**
The Sun is not auspicious when "sleeping" during the transition in the astrological periods <term>Karanas; half of a lunar day</term> of Naga, Taitila, or Chatushpada. It is auspicious when "standing" in Kimstughna, Kaulava, or Shakuni. || 25 || In the five periods starting with Gara, the Sun is "sitting," which results in moderate prices. || 26 ||

**Now, the Vehicles of the Transition**
The lion, tiger, boar, donkey, elephant, buffalo, dog, goat, cow, and rooster are the vehicles <term>Vahanas; the animal the personified Sankranti deity rides</term> of the Sun's transition in sequence. || 27 ||

**Now, the Garments**
White, yellow, green, red, dark blue, black, variegated, and those resembling the color of marrow or oil are the garments worn by the deity in sequence. || 28 ||

**Now, the Weapons**
The Musundi <note>a type of spiked club or eight-sided mace</note>, mace, sword, staff, bow, Tomara <note>an iron javelin or crowbar</note>, spear, noose, goad, and arrows are the weapons held in sequence. || 29 ||

**Now, the Foods**
Cooked rice, milk-porridge <term>Payasam; a sweet rice pudding</term>, and other eatables...

Cooked food, milk, and yogurt; variegated rice, jaggery <note>unrefined cane sugar</note>, honey, ghee <note>clarified butter</note>, sugar, and various snacks are the items consumed in sequence. **30**

### On Unguents
Musk, saffron, sandalwood, and fragrant earth or yellow pigment <note>original: "rochana," a bright yellow pigment found in cattle</note>; lac-dye, musk-scent, or even turmeric, collyrium <note>eye-salve</note>, and agarwood; camphor and white sandalwood are known as the ointments of the Transit. **31**

### On Classes
Gods, spirits, serpents, birds, beasts, dust-born, and the twice-born <note>the three upper social classes: Brahmins, Kshatriyas, and Vaishyas</note> in order; the Warrior, the Merchant, the Laborer, and the Mixed-caste—these are the classes. **32**

### On Flowers
The Punnaga, Jasmine, Bakula, and Ketaki; the Bilva, Karkaja, Durva grass, Lotus, Mallika flower, Patala, and Jaya in order. **33**

### On Ornaments
Anklets, small bells, pearls, coral, bracelets, and gems; the Gunja berry, cowrie shells, sapphires, and diamond ornaments in their respective order. **34**

### On Stages of Life
The young girl, the maiden, the widow, the middle-aged woman, the mature woman, the bold woman; the old woman, the barren woman, the extremely barren woman, the woman who has not given birth, and the female ascetic—these are the stages of life. **35**

### On the Ill Effects of the Transit’s Vehicle and Other Attributes
For those whose livelihood depends on the specific vehicle, class, weapon, or food associated with the Transit, there will be destruction; furthermore, there will be a great increase in the price of garments and other goods. **36**
Whether the Transit figure is standing, sitting, or sleeping, a corresponding destruction should be known. **37**

### On the Results of the Birth Star in Each Month’s Transit
If the birth star falls within the three stars preceding the day of the Transit, one sees the path; then there is happiness or pain in the next twelve stars. **38**
Then, in the "womb" stars, happiness is known, and likewise, the same applies to the entire remaining sequence. 21

...by those to be known, wealth is obtained in this way; such is the constant monthly result. || 39 || If one’s birth star <note>Nakshatra</note> falls on an auspicious day of the week, it is considered good; but on an inauspicious day, it is said to be bad. This is the result month by month. || 40 ||

### Charity during an Inauspicious Solar Transit
To pacify the defects of a solar transit <note>Sankranti; the moment the sun enters a new zodiac sign</note>, one should draw a triangular diagram with three tridents upon sesame seeds and donate it along with gold. || 41 ||

### Regarding the Intercalary and Vanishing Months
The month in which no solar transit occurs is called an "Additional Month" <note>Adhimasa</note>. It is also known by the name "Impure Month" <note>Malamasa</note> and generally occurs during the seven months starting from Chaitra <note>the first month of the Hindu lunar calendar</note>. || 42 || A month with two solar transits is called a "Vanishing Month" <note>Kshayamasa</note>; this occasionally occurs during the three months starting from Kartika. In such a year, there are two additional months. || 43 || These are called "Impure Months" and are prohibited for all sacred rites. || 44 ||

### The Strength of Other Factors based on Stars
The strength of the Moon is derived from the strength of the stars; the strength of the Sun is derived from the Moon; and the strength of all other planets, whether auspicious or inauspicious, should be known from the Sun. || 45 ||

Thus ends the Chapter on Solar Transits in the <term>Muhurta Ganapatti</term> <note>The Calculation of Auspicious Moments</note> composed by the venerable astrologer Harishankara Suri. || 12 ||

# Muhurta Ganapati

-> Page 72 <-

...it shall be. In other cases, the "piercer" <note>Vedhaka: a planet that obstructs the influence of another</note> and the "pierced" <note>Vedhya: the planet being obstructed</note> should be understood based on their position from the birth sign. || 21 ||

### Results of Planets According to the Eight-Fold Grid
<note>Ashtakavarga: a system of points used to calculate the strength of planetary transits.</note>

When planets in their own eight-fold grid are positioned in signs where they hold high authority or strength, then even if their transit <note>Gochara</note> is technically unfavorable, they will still bring auspicious results, provided they possess sufficient "lines" <note>Rekhas: points of strength in the grid</note>. || 22 ||

### On the Fruitlessness of Planets

A planet that promises good results but is aspected <note>viewed</note> by malefic planets, or a planet that promises bad results but is aspected by benefic planets—both should be known as "fruitless" <note>neutralized</note>, whether in a transit or in a birth chart. || 23 ||

A planet situated in an enemy’s sign, in its sign of debilitation, or one that is "combust" <note>hidden by the sun's glare</note> is also considered fruitless. The same applies to a planet aspected by its enemy. || 24 ||

### The Duration of Planetary Transit in a Sign

Venus, Mercury, and the Sun stay for one month; Mars for one and a half months; Jupiter for one year; and Saturn for two and a half years. The Moon stays for two and a quarter days. A malefic planet <note>Rahu/Ketu</note> is said to have no standard transit period <insert>in this specific context</insert>. || 25 ||

### The Timing of Planetary Results

Mars and the Sun give results at the beginning of a sign; Venus and Jupiter in the middle; the Moon and Saturn at the end; and Mercury is known to give results at all times. || 26 ||

The Sun gives results five days before entering a sign; the Moon three "ghatis" <note>approximately 72 minutes</note> before; Mars from the moment of entry; Venus and Mercury seven days before. || 27 ||

Jupiter gives results two months before; Saturn six months before; and Rahu is remembered to give results three months before entering a sign. || 28 ||

Planets situated at the "junctions" <note>Sandhi: the border between two signs or constellations</note> of signs and constellations certainly give results regarding gain or loss. This applies to planets in signs and constellations, whether moving forward or in retrograde. || 29 ||

### Results of Planets from the Birth Sign

The best positions for planets from the birth sign are the tenth, eleventh, sixth, and third houses.
**10 | 11 | 6 | 3**

-> **The Middle Positions** <-

The middle positions are the fifth, seventh, second, and ninth houses <note>original: "sthana"</note>. | 5 | 7 | 2 | 9 | are thus situated. || 30 || If the planet is in the southwest <note>referring to the direction of the node Rahu</note>, the eighth, or the fourth house | 8 | 4 | and coincides with the birth star <term>Janma-nakshatra: the lunar mansion the moon occupied at the moment of birth</term>, death may occur. ### Actions to be Avoided During Inauspicious Planetary Transits

When a planet is in an unfavorable position <note>literally "uneven" or "difficult" position</note>, the following should be avoided: traveling at improper times, journeying to distant lands, and for a king, riding unruly horses or elephants. One should also avoid entering a new house or temple. || 32 || No courageous or risky undertaking should be performed while the planets are in these difficult positions. || 33 ||

### The Ritual Gifts for the Planets

Those planets which are unfavorable in their current transit <term>Gochara: the movement of planets through the zodiac signs relative to one's birth chart</term>, in their major period <term>Dasha: a planetary period governing a span of a person's life</term>, or in their divisional charts, will become pleased through chanting, charity, and peace-making rituals. || 34 ||

**For the Sun:** To please the Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note>, kings and seekers should give rubies, a cow, wheat, gold, copper, jaggery <note>unrefined cane sugar</note>, a lotus flower, sandalwood, a fly-whisk, and red items. || 35 ||

**For the Moon:** To please the Moon <note>original: "Vidhu"</note>, one should give pearls, silver, white copper, a conch shell, bamboo-seed rice, camphor, a pair of oxen, a gemstone, and a water-pot. || 36 ||

**For Mars:** For the satisfaction of Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, one should give coral, gold, wheat, red cloth, a reddish-brown horse, oleander flowers, jaggery, copper, and red lentils. || 37 ||

**For Mercury:** For the affection of Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note>, one should give bell-metal, blue cloth, gold, elephant tusks <note>ivory</note>, emeralds <note>original: "garutmaka"</note>, mung beans, and yellow flowers. || 38 ||

**For Jupiter:** To please Jupiter <note>original: "Vachaspati," the Lord of Speech</note>, one should give turmeric, a horse, white gold, yellow grain, yellow cloth, salt, and topaz. || 39 ||

**For Venus:** For the affection of Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note>, one should give a bow, multicolored cloth, ghee <note>clarified butter</note>, a white horse, gold, rice, perfumes, silver, and a diamond. || 40 || <margin>71</margin>

**For Saturn:** To please Saturn <note>original: "Shani"</note>, one should give sesame seeds, oil, black lentils <note>urad dal</note>, sapphires, dark cloth, a black cow, a buffalo, and gold.
