For the Sun and Moon, one should wear silver; for Mars <note>the brother of the planets</note>, coral; for Mercury, gold; for Saturn, iron; and for Jupiter, a pearl. || 54 || To please the others <note>Rahu and Ketu</note>, a person should wear Lapis Lazuli <note>original: "Rajavarta"</note> on the body. || 55 ||

**Now, the ring of the nine planets:**
In the center of the ring, place a Ruby for the Sun; in the East, a Diamond for Venus; in the Southeast, a Pearl for the Moon; in the South, Coral for Mars. || 56 || In the Southwest <note>the direction of the demons</note>, a Gomed <note>Hessonite garnet</note> for Rahu; in the West, a Sapphire for Saturn; in the Northwest, a Cat's Eye for Ketu; in the North, a Topaz <note>or Yellow Sapphire</note> for Jupiter. || 57 || Likewise, in the Northeast, an Emerald for the satisfaction of Mercury <note>the son of the Moon</note>. This ring should always be worn by men for the pleasure of the planets. || 58 ||

**Now, the means for calming the affliction of the planets:**
By following the words of the teacher <note>Guru</note>, by bowing to the gods and the learned <note>Brahmins</note>, by listening to the Vedas and other scriptures, or by conversing with holy men; || 59 || by purity of mind, by chanting <note>japa</note>, by giving charity, by fire offerings <note>homa</note>, and by the sight of the Lord—the planets will not cause pain, even if they are situated in harmful positions. || 60 ||

Thus ends the Chapter on Transits <note>original: "Gochara-prakarana"</note> by the great astrologer Raval Harishankar. || 13 ||

Under the three Shravanas <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, Anuradha, the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashwini</note>, Mrigashira, the three Hastas <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, Rohini, Pushya, and the three Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>— || 2 ||

The first appearance of the menses <note>original: "pushpa darshanam," literally "the seeing of the flower"</note> is auspicious for women if they are wearing white clothes. It is considered mediocre in Jyeshtha, Punarvasu, and Mula, and is condemned in all other signs. || 3 ||

On the eighth, twelfth, and sixth lunar days, on "Empty" days <note>Rikta tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note>, on the New Moon, during the Sun's entry into a new sign <note>Sankranti</note>, on Bhadra and Ananda days, during the Vyatipata yoga, during an eclipse, or during the Vaidhriti yoga— || 4 ||

In a stranger's house, at twilight, in a bad region, or in [dirty] clothing, the first menses is inauspicious. Generally, such a sighting is not desired, but it can become auspicious through the performance of peace-making rituals <note>Shanti</note>. || 5 ||

All the faults arising from the timing of the first menses are dissolved if the rising sign <note>Lagna</note> is auspicious and is occupied or aspected by Venus or Jupiter. || 6 ||

### Regarding the Bathing of a Woman after her Period
In Punarvasu, Chitra, Jyeshtha, and Pushya, a woman who has completed her period <note>term: "Ritumati" — a woman after her menses who is ready for conception</note> should bathe on an auspicious weekday and an auspicious lunar day. || 7 ||

### Regarding Quick Conception
Under the two Rohinis <note>Rohini and Mrigashira</note>, Swati, the Hasta constellation, and the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashwini</note>, a young woman who has bathed will quickly achieve pregnancy. || 8 ||

### Regarding the Rite of Conception
One should avoid the birth constellation, Bharani, Mula, a corrupted Moon, Revati, and Magha; also avoid the transition days <note>Parva</note>, Vyatipata, Vaidhriti, and the first half of Parigha yoga. || 9 || One must also avoid the anniversary of a parent's death <note>Shraddha</note>, twilight, daytime, the sixth lunar day, and the four days of the menstrual flow, as well as Vishti <note>Karana</note>, the "Empty" days, and the Sun's transition. || 10 ||

Likewise, one should avoid cruel weekdays for the rite of conception <note>term: "Garbhadhana" — the first of the sixteen traditional sacraments, performed to ensure a healthy child</note>. In Swati, Hasta, Anuradha, Rohini, or the three Shravanas, || 11 || in the Uttaras, or in Mrigashira, on an auspicious day and on even-numbered nights <note>counted from the start of the menses</note>, the rite of conception is praised when the Moon is in Hasta...

**Now, regarding the Rites of Pumsavana and Simantonnayana:**
The <term>Pumsavana</term><note>A ritual performed during pregnancy to ensure the health of the fetus and traditionally to pray for a male child.</note> rite is prescribed in the second or third month. In the sixth or eighth month of the first pregnancy, the <term>Simantonnayana</term><note>The "parting of the hair" ceremony, a baby shower-like ritual to ensure the mental health of the mother and the safety of the unborn child.</note> should be known [to take place]. || 15 || Under the two Punarvasus, Mula, Shravana, Mrigashira, or Hasta; on the days of Jupiter (Thursday), Mars (Tuesday), or the Sun (Sunday), the Pumsavana and related rites are declared [auspicious]. || 16 || In Revati, the three "Utara" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and especially on Friday, Monday, or Wednesday, success is achieved. When the month is purified and the Moon is strong, and the Lagna is in a masculine division, it is auspicious. This is true when benefics are in the Kendras or Konas and malefics are in the 11th, 3rd, or 6th houses. || 17 ||

**Now, the Lords of the Months:**
The lords of the months [of pregnancy] are Jupiter, Mars, Jupiter, the Sun, the Moon, Saturn, Mercury, the Lord of the Lagna, the Moon, and the Sun, in that order. || 18 ||

**Now, Special Rules for Pregnancy Sacraments:**
In marriage and in the <term>Samskaras</term><note>Sacred life-cycle rituals or sacraments.</note> of pregnancy, the purity of the Moon must be checked for the woman as well. In travel and other such activities, the lunar strength of the husband is what matters. || 19 || In the sacraments beginning with <term>Garbhadhana</term><note>The rite of conception.</note> and in the first feeding of the child (<term>Annaprashana</term>), the faults of the combustion of Jupiter or Venus, or the intercalary month (<term>Malamasa</term>), do not apply. || 20 ||

**Now, the Worship of Vishnu:**
After the Simantonnayana, in the bright fortnight, under Rohini or Shravana, on the twelfth or seventh lunar days, worship [of Vishnu] should be performed for the satisfaction of the fetus. || 21 ||

<margin>Su. Ga. 78</margin>On an auspicious day, the mother, having completed the purification after childbirth, should enter the [main] house during the two Revati <note>The 27th lunar mansion or Nakshatra</note> constellations or during the pairs [of auspicious stars]. || 22-23 ||

### The Birth Rite (Jātakarma)

The father should perform the <term>Jātakarma</term><note>The birth rite, one of the personal sacraments (Samskaras) in Hinduism, involving the father touching and feeding the newborn honey and ghee</note> immediately upon the birth of the child. Or, he may perform it on the eleventh or twelfth day according to the prescribed rules. || 24 ||

The constellations of Revati (the two), Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, Shravana (and the following two), the three stars of Hasta, Rohini, and Mrigashira || 25 || and the three "Uttara" stars are considered suitable for the birth rite and other ceremonies, even if the primary time has passed. || 26 ||

### Determining Inauspicious Times at Birth

**Regarding the "Unenjoyable" Root (Abhukta Mula):**
The sage Narada has said: the last two <term>Ghatikas</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> of the constellation Jyeshtha and the first two Ghatikas of Mula constitute the "Abhukta Mula" <note>A highly inauspicious astrological period</note>. || 27 ||

However, Vashistha states it is the last and first single Ghatika of those two respectively. Angira says it is one Ghatika, while others say six or eight. If a child is born during this time, the father should abandon the child or not look upon them for twelve years. || 28 ||

**Regarding the Quarters of the Mula Constellation:**
If birth occurs in the first quarter of Mula, it affects the father; in the second, the mother; in the third, wealth is destroyed; but the fourth quarter is auspicious. || 29 ||

**The Residence of Mula:**
In the months of Magha, Ashadha, Ashvin, and Bhadrapada, the Mula constellation resides in the Heavens. In Kartika, Shravana, Chaitra, and Pausha, it resides on the Earth. || 30 ||

In Vaishakha, Phalguna, Jyeshtha, and Margashirsha, it resides in the Underworld. The negative affliction is only known to occur when it resides on the Earth; otherwise, there is no fault. || 31 ||

**Regarding Ashlesha, Jyeshtha, the Junction Points (Gandanta), and Twin Births:**
As it has been said:

In the [remaining] quarters of the lunar mansion, the result would be the opposite. ### Now, the results of the quarters of Jyeshtha

If born in the first quarter of *Jyeshtha*, it destroys the elder brother; in the second, it destroys the younger brother. Born in the third, it destroys the mother; and in the fourth quarter, the child destroys himself. || 34 ||

### Now, other previously mentioned and additional evil times for birth

These become auspicious through pacification rites; he explains them: *Gandanta*, *Parigha*, *Shula*, *Vyatipata*, and *Vaidhriti* <note>These are specific astrological yogas or configurations considered malefic</note>; *Mula*, *Ashlesha*, *Yama*, *Jyeshtha*, the hour of *Yamaghanta*, and the Sun's transit into a new sign. || 35 ||

The *Vajra* and *Ganda* yogas, the time of death, *Bhadra* <note>A specific inauspicious period in the lunar day</note>, *Vyaghata*, and "burnt" days; the fourteenth day of the dark <margin>5</margin> fortnight, an eclipse, and birth occurring under the same star as the father or a sibling. || 36 ||

Likewise, the "omitted" day <note>Avama: a day where a lunar day begins and ends within one sunrise</note> is censurable. If a "Triad" <term>Trikadosha: a specific sequence of gendered births</term> occurs at the time of the child's birth, one should perform the pacification rite according to the rules to remove that defect. || 37 ||

### Now, the defect of the Triad (Trikadosha)

If after three sons a fourth child is a girl, or if after three girls a son is born, then that is the defect called the "Triad." || 38 ||

### Now, the first breastfeeding

On a day prescribed for the birth ceremonies, or under the stars *Shravana* or *Punarvasu*, and avoiding *Svati*, the first breastfeeding is declared auspicious on a favorable day. || 39 ||

### Now, the decoction for the woman in confinement

Under a star and on a day prescribed for medicine, and free from bad astrological combinations, <margin>76</margin> a decoction <note>A medicinal herbal tea or tonic</note> should be given to the woman who has recently given birth and to her child for the sake of their health. || 40 ||

### Now, the diet for the woman in confinement

# Page 80

On the auspicious days and under the favorable <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>Lunar mansions or star signs used in Vedic astrology</note> mentioned previously, while the sun is shining, and avoiding the <term>Rikta</term> <note>Empty or hollow lunar days: the 4th, 9th, and 14th, considered unlucky for new ventures</note> days and the four specific yogas, the prescribed diet for the woman who has recently given birth is declared. || 41 ||

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati 80</margin> **Now, the Worship on the Fifth and Sixth Days.**
On the fifth and sixth days from birth, the worship of the goddess Jivanti should be performed at night. On the sixth day, the goddess Ambika <note>A form of the Mother Goddess</note> should be worshipped with songs, vigils, and other festivities. || 42 ||

**Now, the <term>Sutika Snanam</term> <note>The ritual purification bath for a mother after childbirth</note>.**
Under the stars of Hasta, Mrigashirsha, Anuradha, Rohini, the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>, the three Uttaras, or Svati; and on the days of Jupiter (Thursday), the Sun (Sunday), or Mars (Tuesday); || 43 || the ritual bath of the mother is considered excellent. One should avoid the three Ardras, Shravana, Vishakha, Bharani, Mula, Chitra, Krittika, and Magha. One should also avoid the <term>Rikta</term> days, Wednesday, Saturday, and the sixth, twelfth, and eighth lunar days. || 44 ||

**Now, regarding the Daily Bath for everyone in specific contexts.**
At the birth of a son, during a <term>Sankranti</term> <note>The sun's transit from one zodiac sign to another</note>, or on a day of spiritual attainment, when a daily bath must be performed, the faults usually associated with the lunar date (<term>Tithi</term>) do not apply. || 45 ||

**Now, by chance, the prohibition of bathing for women during <term>Shatabhisha</term>.**
**From the <term>Shripati Nibandha</term> <note>A classical treatise on astrology and law by the scholar Shripati</note>:**
Any woman who bathes when the Moon is in the Shatabhisha star will surely become barren, widowed, and unfortunate for seven births. || 46 || If a woman must bathe when the Moon is in Shatabhisha, she should first worship her husband with all due honors. || 47 ||

**Now, the results of the <term>Teething</term> <note>Dantotpatti: the timing of the first appearance of a child's teeth</note> of an infant.**
If teeth emerge in the first month, it destroys the self; in the second month, the younger brother; in the third, the sister; in the fourth, the mother; and in the fifth, the elder brothers. || 48 || An infant born with teeth already present destroys everyone. However, if teeth appear from the sixth month onwards, the child attains enjoyments; if they appear in the middle of the gums, it is always considered unfavorable. || 49 || <margin>80</margin>

# Auspicious Times for Childhood Rituals

<margin>Su. Ga. <note>Abbreviation for the title of the work, likely "Sushila Ganesha"</note></margin> **Now, the Worship of Water.** Under the two Punarvasus, Hasta, Mrigashira, Mula, and Anuradha <note>These are specific lunar mansions or Nakshatras</note>; on a Thursday, Wednesday, or Monday, and on an auspicious lunar day, the worship of water should be performed. || 50 ||

<margin>81</margin> Salutations. || 50 || When Jupiter or Venus have set <note>referring to planetary invisibility</note>, or during the months of Chaitra or Pausha, or during an intercalary month <note>an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year</note>, or on the completion of a month, or on an unfavorable day, one should not perform the water worship. || 51 ||

**Now, Placing the Child in the Cradle.** One should perform the placing of the child in the cradle under the same lunar mansions prescribed for the first breastfeeding. This should occur on the 18th, 12th, 16th, 20th, or 32nd day from the birth of the child. || 52 ||

Counting from the Sun's lunar mansion: the first 5 are auspicious, the next 15 are unfavorable, and the remaining 21 are auspicious for placing the child in the cradle. || 53 ||

**Now, Placing the Child on a Bed.** Under the lunar mansions mentioned for the cradle, either the mother or a woman whose husband is living <term>Suvasini: a woman considered auspicious because her husband is alive</term>, having meditated upon Lord Vishnu in his yogic sleep, should place the child down with the head facing East. || 54 ||

**Now, the Child’s Drinking of Milk.** On the thirty-first day, under the lunar mansions prescribed for the first feeding of solid food, in the morning and on an auspicious day of the week, the ritual drinking of milk for the child is remembered. || 55 ||

**Now, the First Outing.** The first outing from the house <term>Nishkramana: the ritual where the child is first taken out to see the sun or moon</term> is traditionally performed in the fourth month on a day prescribed by the sages. Or, it may be performed with auspicious rites on the twelfth day from birth. || 56 ||

**Now, the Tying of the Loincloth.**
<margin>Gara Kamara-udane <note>Vernacular gloss likely in an early form of Hindi/Marathi meaning "tying around the waist"</note></margin> The tying of the loincloth should be done during the bright fortnight on an auspicious day, under the five "Hand" stars <note>Hasta and related constellations</note>, the fixed stars, Punarvasu, Ashvini, Magha, Revati, or Mrigashira, and on a favorable weekday. || 57 ||

**Now, the Waist-thread and Sitting on the Ground.** Under Pushya, Hasta, Ashvini, Mula, the three "Uttaras," <margin>81</margin> Rohini, or Mrigashira; or under Jyeshtha or Anuradha, on an auspicious day in the fifth month. || 58 || When Mars is purified <note>astrologically well-placed</note>, having performed worship...

# Muhurta Ganapati
<page-num>82</page-num>
->**The Boar who holds the Earth**<- <note>original: "Baraham dharani bhuvi"; a reference to the Varaha avatar of Vishnu who rescued the earth, often invoked for stability.</note>

<margin>
Muhurta Ganapati
82
</margin>

One should tie a waist-thread <note>a protective cord made of silk or thread</note> and then seat the child. **(59)** A book, a pen, a weapon, silver, and gold should then be placed before them. Whatever the child grasps at that time shall indicate their future livelihood and temperament. **(60)**

### The First Feeding of Solids
<term>Annaprashana: the ritual of a child's first taste of solid food, typically rice.</term>
The auspicious stars for this are the two Revatis, Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, the three beginning with Shravana, the three beginning with Hasta, Rohini, and Mrigashirsha. **(61)** It should be performed under the "fixed" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> in a favorable zodiacal sign, excluding Pisces and Aries. For a boy, this is done in the sixth month or another even-numbered month; for a girl, it is done in the fifth month or another odd-numbered month. **(62)** This first feeding is agreed by all to be performed on an auspicious day. However, some authorities state it should not be done under the birth star, Swati, or Anuradha. **(63)**

### The Eating of Betel Nut
<term>Tambula Bhakshana: the ceremonial first tasting of betel leaf and nut, marking a stage in digestive development.</term>
The stars Anuradha (and its followers), the three Hastas, the two Revatis, the "fixed" stars, Rohini, the two Shravanas, Mrigashirsha, **(64)** Punarvasu, and Pushya are recommended. This should not be done on Saturdays or Tuesdays. The eating of betel should take place at two and a half months of age, or at the time of the first feeding. It should be performed when Mercury or Saturn is in the ascendant, specifically in the sign of Taurus. **(65)**

<margin>
82
</margin>

### The Piercing of the Ears
<term>Karnavedha: the traditional ear-piercing ceremony, believed to have health benefits and spiritual significance.</term>
The auspicious stars are the two Revatis, Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, the two Shravanas, Chitra, Mrigashirsha, and Hasta, on a favorable lunar day. **(66)** It should be done on a beneficial weekday, on the twelfth or sixteenth day from birth. Alternatively, ear piercing may be performed in the sixth or seventh month. **(67)** One should avoid even-numbered years <note>after the first year</note>, the birth month, the month of Pausha, and the period when Vishnu sleeps <note>Chaturmas</note>. One should also avoid the birth star, though some authorities allow the "fixed" stars. **(68)**

**Nose Piercing:** The rules prescribed for ear-piercing are also considered auspicious for the piercing of a girl's nose. <note>original: "Sikavedha" or "Nasikavedha"</note> This should be performed during the constellations of Uttara-Phalguni, Uttara-Ashadha, Uttara-Bhadrapada, Rohini, or Revati, specifically during the bright half of the lunar month. || 69 ||

**The Annual Birthday Ritual:** Every year, on the day of one's birth, one should perform a worship ceremony starting with the Earth and other elements. One must worship Lord Ganesha, the Mother Goddesses, and the "Long-Lived Deities" <note>The Seven Immortals or Chiranjivis, such as Ashwatthama and Hanuman, invoked for longevity</note>. || 70 ||

One should look at their reflection in ghee <note>A ritual called "Chhaya-patra-dana" to ward off evil</note>, perform the prescribed rites, and give various gifts. After tying the auspicious thread <note>Mangala-sutra</note>, one should eat the remaining food along with the twice-born <note>term: Dvija (members of the three upper castes, usually referring here to Brahmins)</note>. || 71 ||

If these rituals are not performed on the birthday or under the birth constellation, or if the day falls under the influence of Saturn or Mars, then great suffering and failure in one's work will occur at every step throughout that year. || 72 ||

**The Tonsure Ceremony:** The consecration of reservoirs, temples, and gardens; the thread ceremony <note>Upanayana</note>; the establishment of the sacred fire; marriage; the first haircut <note>term: Chaura or Chudakarman</note>; and the coronation of a king— || 73 ||

—as well as entering a new home and similar acts, should not be performed during the Southern Transit of the Sun <note>term: Dakshinayana (the period between the summer and winter solstices, considered less auspicious for certain rites)</note>. Nor should they be done when Jupiter or Venus are in their "combust" states, or during their periods of "infancy" or "old age" <note>Refers to the days immediately following the rising or preceding the setting of a planet when its astrological power is considered weak</note>. || 74 ||

For Venus, "infancy" lasts ten days in the West and "old age" lasts five days. In the East, "infancy" is said to be three days and "old age" is fifteen days <note>one fortnight</note>. || 75 ||

For Jupiter, a fortnight of "infancy" and "old age" should always be avoided for auspicious works. Others say that the period of infancy and old age for these planets lasts ten days, while some say seven days. || 76 ||

In cases of absolute necessity, some say the period for Venus and Jupiter is three days. Others know the "infancy" of both to be half a day and "old age" to be three days. || 77 ||

In the region of Avanti <note>Modern-day Ujjain</note> it is seven days; in Vanga <note>Bengal</note> it is ten; in the Vindhya region it is five; and in the Konkan it is two. In all other regions, it is three days. || 78 ||

**The Rising and Setting of Venus:** When it is visible to the eyes, it is considered "seen"—

Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> becomes visible. When it has traveled eighty-seven <note>original: "vasu-shaila," numerical code for 8 and 7</note> degrees, it is no longer seen as it sets. || 79 || Venus is seen in the west when it is below two hundred and fifty <note>original: "kha-vara-agni," numerical code for 0, 5, and 3—though the text glosses 250</note> degrees. In that same position, when gripped by the sun's rays, it is not seen for nine <gloss>9</gloss> days. || 80 ||

### Regarding the Rising and Setting of Jupiter
Generally, Jupiter <note>original: "Vachaspati"</note> remains invisible to the eyes for one month. It rises in the east for a month and then moves to the west after a year. || 81 || The tonsure ceremony <term>Chaula: the first ritual shaving of a child's head</term> should be performed after a full year, or according to the rules in the third year. It should be done during the northern solstice <note>original: "Saumyayane"</note>, when Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, or the Moon fall on their respective days <gloss>92</gloss> <note>The gloss 92 likely refers to a specific calculation or verse reference</note>, under the movable or swift lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term>, or under Chitra, Mrigashira, or Revati, on an auspicious day. Alternatively, for a Brahmin, it may be on the day of the Sun; for a Kshatriya, on the day of Mars. || 82 || For Vaishyas and Shudras, it may be on Saturday, following the prescribed lunar dates and constellations. The ceremony should occur when the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> is endowed with planetary strength and the Moon and stars are auspiciously aligned. || 84 || Some say the eldest child should not have the tonsure in the month of Jyeshtha, nor should it occur in the month of Margashirsha. The tonsure should not be performed if the child's mother is more than five months pregnant. || 85 || However, it is considered acceptable for a child over five years old, even if the mother is pregnant. || 86 ||

### Regarding the Beginning of Writing the Alphabet
In the northern solstice, in an auspicious month, on a day favorable for Vedic study, when Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus are well-placed and the Ascendant is strong: || 87 || Primarily under the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>, Punarvasu, or Anuradha; also under Ardra, Vishakha, Shravana, Hasta, Swati, Chitra, or Abhijit. || 88 || Having worshipped Ganesha <note>original: "Heramba"</note>, the Goddess of Knowledge <note>Saraswati</note>, and the family deities, <margin>84</margin> a wise man should always have the child begin the study of writing in the fifth year. || 89 ||

For one desiring sacred knowledge, the fifth year is considered pure. || 90 || For Kshatriyas <note>the warrior/ruling class</note>, the eighth or eleventh year is declared appropriate. For Vaishyas <note>the merchant/agricultural class</note>, it should be the twelfth year, or even the eighth from conception. || 91 || The secondary or "allowed" period <note>the limit before one loses the right to the ceremony</note> lasts until the sixteenth year for Brahmins, the twenty-second for kings <note>Kshatriyas</note>, and the twenty-fourth for Vaishyas. || 92 || When the lords of their respective classes, or the lords of the Sun, Jupiter, and Mercury are strong, the binding of the vow <term>Vratabandha: the sacred thread ceremony</term> brings auspicious results for the twice-born classes. || 93 ||

**Now, the Lords of the Classes**
Jupiter and Venus are the lords of the Brahmins; the Sun and Mars are the lords of the Kings <note>Kshatriyas</note>. Mercury is the lord of the Vaishyas, and also of the Shudras. Saturn is the master of the outcasts. || 94 ||

**Now, the Lords of the Vedas**
Jupiter is declared the lord of the Rigveda; Venus is the master of the Yajurveda. Mars is the lord of the Samaveda, and Mercury is the master of the Atharvaveda. || 95 ||

**Now, the Strength of Jupiter and Others**
The strength of Jupiter and the Sun must be understood here, just as it is examined for marriage. The strength of the Moon and the birth-star <note>Nakshatra</note> previously mentioned should also be accepted as auspicious for the boy. || 96 ||

**Now, the Months and Other Factors**
In the five months beginning with Magha <note>roughly January to June</note>, while Venus, Jupiter, Mercury, and the Sun are visible. Some authorities say the ceremony may occur in the dark fortnight <note>waning moon</note> if the three primary strengths are present. || 97 || The binding of the vow for Brahmins in their birth month, on their birth star, or in their birth ascendant grants great knowledge, even for those who are the first-born. || 98 ||

**Now, the Lunar Days**
On the 2nd, 3rd, 11th, 10th, 5th, and 12th lunar days <margin>85</margin> in the bright fortnight; and in the dark fortnight, the 5th, 2nd, and 3rd are auspicious. || 99 || Now, the best...

# The Lunar Mansions (Nakshatras)

The three stars of Hasta <note>Corvus</note>, Mrigashira <note>Orion</note>, Pushya <note>Cancer</note>, the two stars of Shravana <note>Aquila</note>, Ashwini <note>Aries</note>, and Revati <note>Pisces</note> are considered excellent for the **binding of the girdle** <term>Mekhala-bandhana: the ritual tying of a triple-stranded cord around the student's waist</term> for a young student. || 100 ||

**Now, the mediocre stars:**
The stars of Vishakha, Chitra, Arka <note>Hasta</note>, Indu <note>Mrigashira</note>, Kuja <note>Dhanishta</note>, Mula, the three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, and Shatabhisha are considered middling. Some authorities also accept Punarvasu, Anuradha, Ardra, and Rohini in certain cases. || 101 ||

**Now, the stars specific to each Veda:**
For those who study the **Rigveda** <note>the oldest of the four Vedas</note>, the ceremony is best performed when Jupiter is in Ardra, the three stars of Hasta, Mula, the three Purvas, or Ashwini. || 102 ||

For the students of the **Yajurveda**, the ceremony should be held when Venus is in the three Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Revati, Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, Mrigashira, Hasta, or Rohini. || 103 ||

For the singers of the **Samaveda**, the ceremony is best when Mars is in Ashwini, Shravana, Pushya, Dhanishta, the three Uttaras, or Hasta. || 104 ||

For the followers of the **Atharvaveda**, the ceremony is auspicious when Mercury is in Mrigashira, Hasta, Ashwini, Pushya, Dhanishta, Revati, Shravana, Punarvasu, or Anuradha. || 105 ||

**A special note on general stars:**
The star Punarvasu is considered excellent for the vow, and some say the same for Shatabhisha. || 106 ||

**Now, the prohibited times for the vow:**
One should not perform the **binding of the vow** <term>Vrata-bandhana: another name for the initiation ceremony</term> during the dark fortnight of the moon, on Saturdays, at night, during the evening twilight, or during **"throat-seizing" days** <term>Galagraha: specific lunar days considered inauspicious for starting Vedic study</term>. It is also forbidden on holidays or in the afternoon. || 107 ||

**Now, the holidays (Anadhyaya):**
The day the sun enters a new zodiac sign, the anniversaries of the world-ages <note>Yugadi</note>, the anniversaries of the Manus <note>Manvadi</note>, the second day of the bright half of Jyeshtha, the third day of the dark half of Chaitra, and the twelfth day of the bright half of Magha. || 108 ||

The eighth and fourteenth days of each lunar fortnight, and the three days surrounding the new moon. These, along with the "Empty" days <note>Rikta: the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</note> and the eight "throat-seizing" days, must be avoided for the initiation ceremony. || 109 ||

Now, here...

# The Strength of the Ascendant
<margin>Muhurta Ganapati 87</margin>
All malefic planets <note>planets like Mars, Saturn, and the Sun that generally have a harsh influence</note> are auspicious for the sacred thread ceremony when placed in the 3rd and 6th houses. The Moon is favorable in the 2nd, 7th, 10th, and 3rd houses. Benefic planets <note>gentle planets like Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury</note> are auspicious in the angles <note>1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> and trines <note>1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>. All planets are auspicious in the 11th house (the house of gains) for the ceremony. || 110 || Jupiter, the Moon, Venus, and the Lord of the Ascendant are not desired in the 6th or 8th houses for this rite. Venus and the Moon are afflicted in the 12th house, as are malefic planets in the 2nd, 8th, 5th, 10th, and 3rd houses. || 111 || Benefic planets are said to be auspicious for the rite except when in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses. When Venus is in its sign of exaltation <note>Pisces</note>, the Moon in the 1st house is best; sometimes the Sun is also acceptable there. || 112 || The Moon is considered excellent for the rite when it is in the <term>Navamsha</term> <note>a one-ninth divisional chart used for precision</note> of Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus. In other cases, the Moon in its own division or entering the lunar mansion of Punarvasu is auspicious. || 113 ||

# On the Results of Planets in the Angles
One becomes a servant of the king, a merchant, a statue-like figure <note>perhaps meaning stoic or handsome</note>, a soldier, a wealthy nobleman, a scholar, a rich man, or an outcast <note>original: "Mleccha"</note> respectively, based on the Sun and other planets being outside the angles. || 114 || Jupiter or Venus in the Ascendant, or the Moon in a division of Venus, is auspicious for the rite. Jupiter and the Moon together are good, but Venus is not desired if joined with the Sun, Mars, or Saturn. || 115 ||

# On the Excellence of the Month of Chaitra
In the month of Chaitra <note>March–April</note>, even if the Sun is in Pisces and Jupiter is weak, the sacred thread ceremony for a boy is highly recommended, because Chaitra and Pisces are considered auspicious for this. || 116 ||

# Special Rule Regarding the Mother’s Menstrual Cycle
The <term>Keshanta</term> <note>the first shaving of the beard</note> should be performed in the 16th year at the time prescribed for the <term>Chaula</term> <note>tonsure or first haircut</note>. The <term>Samavartana</term> <note>graduation ceremony returning home from the teacher</note> is said to occur at the time prescribed for the sacred thread ceremony. <margin>87</margin> || 117 ||

# On the First Shaving and Graduation
<margin>16</margin>
Regarding the flaw of the mother’s menstrual period <note>which usually halts auspicious rites</note>: if it occurs after the <term>Nandi Shraddha</term> <note>the preliminary ancestral invocation</note>, then the necessary sacred thread ceremony or tonsure may proceed.

### Now, the Binding of the Knife for Kings

In the months and lunar days prescribed for religious vows, excluding the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, and when Mars is strong, the binding of the knife <term>Churika Bandhanam: a formal ceremony where a warrior or king is invested with a ceremonial dagger</term> should be performed for kings prior to their marriage, provided it is not a Tuesday. || 119 ||

Thus ends the Chapter on Life-Cycle Rites <term>Samskara: transformative rituals that mark life stages</term> by the Great Astrologer Ravala Harishankara. || 14 ||

A wife is the witness and the means for the attainment of Virtue, Wealth, and Pleasure <note>Dharma, Artha, and Kama: three of the four goals of human life</note>. Her character is determined by the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth or inquiry</term>; therefore, I shall describe the auspicious Ascendant. || 1 || Having honored the Astrologer <term>Daivajña: one who knows the divine/fate</term> on an auspicious day with betel leaves, coconuts, and other gifts, and having informed him, I shall speak of the marriage of the groom and the bride. || 2 || If the Moon is situated in the 10th, 11th, 3rd, 5th, or 7th house from the Ascendant of the Query <term>Prashna Lagna: the chart cast for the moment a question is asked</term>, and is aspected by Jupiter, the marriage of the groom and bride will occur quickly. || 3 || Or, if the Ascendant is Taurus, Libra, or Cancer, and is joined or aspected by benefics; and if Venus and the Moon are in odd-numbered signs and aspect the Ascendant, || 4 || they grant a husband and a wife respectively. If they have reached even-numbered signs or divisions, and the Moon is in the 6th or 8th house during the dark fortnight and placed in an even sign, || 5 || and is aspected by a malefic planet, it is declared to cause the breaking of the marriage. If the Moon and Venus are in the 7th house, the woman becomes a widow; if Mars is there, she becomes unchaste; if Saturn is there, she is of low character. || 6 || If Jupiter or Mercury are in the 7th house from the Ascendant of the Query, she will be well-behaved and fortunate. || 7 || A wise man should speak of the woman's query regarding her children and her husband by observing what kind of offspring she has and how she enters the assembly. || 8 || If the sound of conches, kettledrums, or similar instruments is heard at the time of the query, it is auspicious; if the sound of crows or jackals is heard, one should not proceed with the auspicious event. || 9 || The couple—

Between the two, if there is no friendship <note>original: "maitri"; refers to planetary or psychological compatibility</note>, marriage will not bring auspicious results. When friendship exists, it is auspicious; therefore, I shall explain the various kinds of friendship. || 20 ||
Having first understood what is fitting and auspicious for the bride and groom, only then should the marriage be performed; for this reason, I speak of these matters first. || 21 ||
The <term>Yunji</term> <note>joining/union</note> (1), the <term>Varga</term> <note>alphabetical group</note> (2), the <term>Varna</term> <note>social-spiritual class</note> (3), <term>Vashya</term> <note>mutual attraction/control</note> (4), <term>Tara</term> <note>stellar compatibility</note> (5), <term>Yonija</term> <note>biological/instinctive compatibility</note> (6), <term>Graha-maitri</term> <note>friendship of the planetary lords</note> (7), <term>Gana</term> <note>temperament: divine, human, or demonic</note> (8), <term>Rashyornha</term> <note>zodiacal sign compatibility</note> (9), and <term>Nadi</term> <note>pulse or life-force constitution</note> (10)—these are the ten factors. || 12 || <note>The text numbers these as ten, though the verse structure suggests a complex counting system for the total points.</note> Furthermore, the strength of these must be understood mutually. When the qualities <note>Gunas</note> are abundant, the marriage between the bride and groom should be performed. || 13 ||

### On the Affection of the Yunji
The first part consists of the stars from Revati to Krittika, and from Ardra to Punarvasu. Among the twelve, Jyeshtha is in the middle, and the last nine are at the end. || 14 || If the birth occurs in the first part, the husband will be dear to the girl. If the birth occurs in the latter part, the girl will be dear to the husband. || 15 || If in the middle part, mutual affection is said to exist between the couple. || 16 ||

### On the Affection of the Varga
The 'A' group of letters is called the Eagle <note>Garuda</note>. The 'Ka' group is the Cat. The 'Ca' group is known as the Lion. The 'Ta' group is declared to be the Dog. || 17 || The 'Ta' group is known as the Serpent. The 'Pa' group is considered the Mouse. The 'Ya' group is named the Deer, and the 'Sha' group is called the Ram. || 18 || There is natural enmity between the Eagle and the Serpent, and between the Mouse and the Cat. There is enmity between the Tiger <note>Lion</note> and the Deer, as well as between the Ram and the Dog. || 19 || <margin>82</margin> If there is enmity between the groups <note>Vargas</note>, there will be great sorrow; if the groups are the same, the affection is excellent. This applies to the bride and groom, as well as to a master and servant. || 20 ||

# Now, the Compatibility of Social Class (Varna)

The zodiac signs of Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces are known as the Priestly class <note>original: "Brahmana"</note>. Next are the Warrior class <note>original: "Bahujas" or "Kshatriya"</note>, which are Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius. Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn are the Merchant class <note>original: "Visha" or "Vaishya"</note>. || 21 || Libra, Gemini, and Aquarius are the Laboring class <note>original: "Shudra"</note>. These are the classes arising from the zodiac signs. In marriage, it is best if the groom belongs to a higher class; the bride should not be of a higher class than the groom. || 22 ||

# Now, the Compatibility of Control (Vashya)

Gemini, Aquarius, Libra, Virgo, and the first half of Sagittarius are considered "Two-footed" <note>human signs</note>. The second half of Sagittarius and the first half of Capricorn, || 23 || along with Leo, Taurus, and Aries, are "Four-footed" <note>animal signs</note>. The second half of Capricorn, Pisces, and Cancer are designated as "Water-dwellers." || 24 || Scorpio is categorized as a "Reptile" <note>literally "creeping thing"</note>. All "Two-footed" signs are naturally subservient to others, except for Leo <note>the Lion</note>. || 25 ||
<margin>60</margin>
Water-dwellers are considered "food" <note>prey</note> for others, and Scorpio is a source of fear for them. One should understand the measurements of power from common worldly knowledge, such as how Scorpio is controlled by the Lion. || 26 ||

# Now, the Compatibility of the Birth Star (Tara)

Count from the bride’s birth star <note>Nakshatra</note> to the groom’s, and from the groom’s to the bride’s. Divide each by nine. The remainder is auspicious, except when the remainder is seven, five, or three <note>these represent "Death," "Obstacle," and "Danger" respectively</note>. || 27 ||

# Now, the Compatibility of Sexual Nature (Yoni)

The horse is the animal for Ashvini and Shatabhisha; the buffalo for Swati and Hasta. The lion is for Purva Bhadrapada and Dhanishta; the elephant for Bharani and Revati. || 28 || The ram is for Krittika and Pushya; the monkey for Shravana and Purva Ashadha. The mongoose is for Uttara Ashadha and Abhijit; the serpent for Rohini and Mrigashira. || 29 ||

The deer is for Jyeshtha and Anuradha; the dog for Mula and Ardra. The cat is for Punarvasu and Ashlesha; the rat for Purva Phalguni and Magha. || 30 || The tiger is for Vishakha and Chitra; the cow—

When the temperaments are the same, there is supreme affection. Between the Divine and the Human...

# Page 83

...is equal. There is natural enmity between the **Gods** <note>Deva</note> and the **Demons** <note>Rakshasa</note>, and between **Humans** <note>Manava</note> and **Demons** there is the threat of death. || 41 ||

### Exceptions to the Faults of the Temperament Groups
<note>Gana-dosha-apavada: Rules explaining when the mismatch of temperament groups can be ignored.</note>

If there is friendship between the lords of the zodiac signs, or if the lords of the bride's and groom's signs are the same, then the fault of the temperament groups <note>Gana-dosha</note> does not apply; such a marriage is considered to grant auspicious results. || 42 ||

If the bride belongs to the **Demon** group and the groom belongs to the **Human** group, the marriage may still be performed if there is a high number of other compatibility points <note>Gunas</note> and the union is deemed necessary. || 43 ||

If the woman belongs to the **Demon** group and the man to the **God** group, their marriage may be conducted if there is "Yoni" compatibility <note>biological/instinctive compatibility</note> and other favorable factors; this was stated by the sage Garga. || 54 || <note>Original numbering jumps from 43 to 54</note>

### The Compatibility of Zodiac Signs
<note>Rashi-kuta: The relative positions of the bride's and groom's moon signs.</note>

If the signs are in the 6th and 8th positions from each other, it signifies death. In the 5th and 9th positions, it concerns progeny. In the 2nd and 12th positions, it signifies poverty. In all other positions, happiness is known for both. || 45 ||

A "bad wife" is indicated in an uneven 2nd/12th position; in the 6th position, there is fear for the groom. In even signs, it is known to be auspicious, while the opposite is my considered opinion. || 46 ||

### Exceptions to Malignant Sign Placements
<note>Dusta-kuta-apavada: When traditionally "bad" sign alignments are actually acceptable.</note>

Even in the aforementioned "bad" placements, if the two signs have the same planetary ruler, the marriage is excellent, provided they do not share the same pulse-point <note>Nadi</note>. || 47 ||

The following 6th/8th placements actually result in affection:
-> **Libra (7) and Taurus (2)** <-
-> **Pisces (12) and Leo (5)** <-
-> **Aquarius (11) and Virgo (6)** <-
-> **Sagittarius (9) and Cancer (4)** <-
-> **Capricorn (10) and Gemini (2)** <-
-> **Scorpio (8) and Aries (1)** <- || 48 ||

These specific 6th/8th alignments bring love; others should indeed be abandoned. Even if there is enmity between the sign lords, if there is friendship between the lords of the divisional signs <note>Navamsha</note>, and the pulse-points and birth stars are pure, the marriage is auspicious. || 49 ||

### Purification of the Pulse-Points
<note>Nadi-shuddhi: The most critical compatibility factor regarding the health and life-force of the couple.</note>

Jyeshtha, Mula, Ashwini, the two Ardras <note>Ardra and Rahu's influence</note>, the two Shatabhishas <note>Shatabhisha and its pair</note>, and the two Uttara Phalgunis—these are declared to belong to the **First Pulse** <note>Adi Nadi</note>.

|| 50 || Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, Bharani, Dhanishta <note>original: "Vasu"</note>, and Pushya; these along with Purva Ashadha <note>original: "Jalabham"</note> are known as the **Middle Nadi** <note>The Nadi represents the physiological/energetic temperament of a person in Vedic astrology</note> in the context of the Yoni system. || 51 || Rohini, Krittika, Ashlesha, Magha, Swati, and the two Vishakhas; as well as Rohini <note>likely a scribal repetition or variant</note>, Uttara Ashadha, and Shravana are the **End Nadi**. || 52 || If the bride and groom belong to the same Nadi, the "grasping of hands" <term>Karagraha: marriage</term> is not desired. If both are in the Middle Nadi, it leads to death; therefore, one should avoid this match by all means. || 53 ||

### Regarding Exceptions to the Nadi Defect

|| If the Moon signs <term>Rashi</term> are the same but the birth stars <term>Nakshatra</term> are different, or if the birth stars are the same but the Moon signs are different, or if the birth stars are different but fall within the same sign, then the defects of Gana, Nadi, and Bhakuta <note>The three major compatibility factors</note> do not apply. || 54 ||

### Special Considerations for the Same Birth Star

|| Vishakha, Ardra, Shravana, Pushya, Rohini, Uttara Bhadrapada, Revati, and Magha are considered excellent even if both partners share the same star; other stars are not recommended for such a pairing. || 55 ||

### Regarding Charities to Remedy Nadi and Other Defects

|| One should offer gold, clarified butter <term>Ghee</term>, gems, and cows, and perform the "Conqueror of Death" <term>Mrityunjaya Japa: a powerful healing prayer</term> chant; these must be done if a marriage is necessary despite a Nadi defect, in order to remove the affliction. || 56 || One should give copper for the 2nd-12th house defect, gold for the 6th-8th house defect, a pair of cows for the 9th-5th house defect, and gold for defects in Varna <note>social/spiritual class</note>. To remove all defects, one should give gold, food, clothing, and a milch cow. || 57 ||

### Regarding the Points for Varna and Other Qualities

|| The points for Varna and the other categories should be known to increase by one in sequence. In marriage, a score exceeding ten points is considered auspicious. || 58 ||

### Another Method for Varna Points

|| One point is assigned if the groom's Varna is superior or equal to the bride's. If the groom's Varna is inferior, the points are zero. Some say half a point is given if they are similar. || 59 || In the Vashya <note>attraction/control</note> category, half a point is given if one is "eatable" by the other, but one point if they are compatible...

The points are remembered as follows: Two points are given if there is friendship between the two <term>Rashis</term> <note>Zodiac signs</note>; if there is enmity in the food-relationship <note>Bhakuta</note>, zero points are recorded. || 60 || If the <term>Tara</term> <note>Star/Asterism compatibility</note> is auspicious from one side, then one and a half points are given to the pair. If it is auspicious from both sides, then it is remembered as three points; if both are auspicious, it is good for both. || 61 || In the case of "Great Friendship," four points are given; in "Yoni-Friendship" <note>Biological/instinctive compatibility</note>, three points. In "Neutrality," two points; in "Enmity," one point; and in "Great Enmity," zero points. || 62 || In mutual enmity, it shall be zero; in equal enmity, eight points <note>This likely refers to specific exceptions in the Bhakuta calculation</note>. If one is an enemy and the other a friend, and there is equality between the two, three points are given. || 63 || In "Equal Friendship," four points; five points if they share a single base. Likewise, if there is friendship between the lords of the signs of the groom and the bride. || 64 || If the woman is of the "Human" <term>Gana</term> <note>Nature/Temperament category</note> and the groom is of the "Divine" category, even if equal, the points are six. Otherwise, if the woman is Divine and the groom is of the "Demon" category, it is one point. || 65 || When the bride is of the "Demon" category and the groom is "Divine," there is happiness. Between "Demon" and "Human" categories, the points shall be zero for the mutual pair. || 66 || If the two do not share the same quarter of the star, but there is friendship in the <term>Bhakuta</term> and <term>Yoni</term>, then even if the bride's sign is distant, four points are given, or even one if it is only on one side. || 67 || In a good <term>Kuta</term> <note>Compatibility metric</note>, even if there is Yoni-enmity, it is not considered "distant" <note>Inauspicious</note> as stated. If the signs are the same but the stars are different, five points are given in the <term>Bhakuta</term> combination. || 68 || If the groom's star is far from the bride's, it is beneficial and auspicious. If the bride's star is far from the groom's, that is fearful and not auspicious in marriage. || 69 || If the <term>Nadi</term> <note>Pulse/Physiological constitution</note> is different, there are eight points; even in the last case, there are one and a half. If the Nadis are the same, it is like a "nipple on a goat's neck" <note>A Sanskrit idiom meaning something useless or a wasted effort</note>, yet all points may remain in specific exceptions. || 70 ||

**Now, the various "Poison Maiden" combinations:**
If a girl is born on a Sunday, Tuesday, or Saturday, falling on a "Bhadra" lunar day <note>the 2nd, 7th, or 12th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, and under the lunar mansions of Shatabhisha, Ashlesha, or Krittika, she is known as a "Poison Maiden" (Visha Kanya). (73)
If a malefic planet is situated in the ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> in an enemy's sign, or if two such planets are equally placed, this "Poison Maiden" yoga is formed. (74)
She who has Saturn in the ascendant, the Sun in the fifth house, and Mars in the ninth house is also called a "Poison Maiden"; such a variety of Poison Maidens should not be accepted in marriage. (75)

**Now, the remedy for the "Poison Maiden" defect:**
After performing vows such as the Savitri Vow <note>a ritual dedicated to Savitri, who saved her husband from death</note> to prevent widowhood, she should be symbolically married to a Peepal tree <note>Ficus religiosa</note> or similar, and then given in marriage to a long-lived groom. (76)

**Now, the results of inauspicious lunar mansions at the time of birth:**
A daughter or son born under the Ashlesha mansion destroys the mother-in-law. One born under Mula destroys the father-in-law. One born under Jyeshtha destroys the husband's elder brother. (77)

**Now, the exceptions to this:**
The first quarter of Ashlesha and the final quarter of Mula are auspicious. Likewise, the first three quarters of Vishakha and Jyeshtha bring good fortune. (78)

**Thus ends the exception for the Mula mansion for the bride and groom.**

**Now, the betrothal and the selection of the maiden:**
In the lunar mansions prescribed for marriage, as well as Purva-Phalguni, Dhanishta, Krittika, and Ashwini, <margin>65</margin> the selection and betrothal of the maiden and the groom should be performed on an auspicious day. (79)

**Now, the forbidden times for marriage and related rituals:**
Reservoirs— <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword indicates the next page begins with "reservoirs" or "water bodies"</note>

### Now, another method regarding the two months to be avoided:
Those desiring auspicious results should avoid the two months when Jupiter is situated in the sign of the Crocodile <note>original: "Nakra" (Capricorn)</note>. || 87 ||

### Now, the specific rule regarding Jupiter’s retrograde and rapid motion:
When Jupiter is in retrograde or rapid motion, one should wait for twenty-eight days to pass; after that, one may perform auspicious acts like vows and weddings. || 88 ||

### Now, an exception to the fault of retrograde and rapid motion:
<margin>66</margin>
If Jupiter is in retrograde or rapid motion but is situated in the first, fifth, ninth, or eleventh house, no fault is known to exist there; one may proceed with weddings and other ceremonies. || 89 ||

### On the Fault of Jupiter in Leo
When Jupiter is in the sign of Leo <note>Simha</note> or in the Leo division <note>Simha-navamsha</note> of any sign, one should not perform marriages or other auspicious ceremonies. This restriction applies specifically to the region north of the Godavari River and south of the Bhagirathi <note>Ganges</note> River. || 60 ||

### On the Absence of the Leo-Jupiter Fault in Specific Regions
In the region south of the Godavari and north of the Bhagirathi, all ceremonies such as marriage may be performed even when Jupiter is situated in Leo. || 61 ||

### On the General Exception for Jupiter in Leo in All Regions
When Jupiter is in the sign of Leo, but the Sun is in Aries, there is no fault. It is taught that for necessary marriages and rites, this applies to all regions. || 62 ||

### On the Specific Rule for Jupiter in Capricorn
In the regions of Magadha <note>South Bihar</note>, Gauda <note>Bengal</note>, Sindhu <note>Indus region</note>, and Konkan, Jupiter in Capricorn <note>Makara</note> should be avoided for marriage and auspicious rites; however, this celestial position of Jupiter is not prohibited in other regions. || 63 ||

### On the Vanished Year
If, due to an accelerated motion <note>Atichara</note>, Jupiter crosses into a new sign in less than ten months—excluding the four signs of Aquarius, Pisces, Aries, and Taurus <note>signs 11, 12, 1, and 2</note>— || 64 ||

And if it does not reach the subsequent sign, that is called a "Vanished Year" <note>Lupta Samvatsara</note>. The region between the Ganges and the Narmada rivers is considered extremely forbidden for rites during this time. || 65 ||

### On the Primacy of Birth Stars in Specific Activities
In matters concerning countries, villages, houses, gambling, legal disputes, war, and fever; or in giving gifts, chanting mantras, service, and the calculation of the "Ka-ka-ri" <note>a specific astrological grid</note> or group-vowels: || 66 ||

Furthermore, in the matching of couples, the name-sign <note>the zodiac sign based on one's given name</note> is primary. However, in all other matters, especially marriage, the primacy belongs to the birth-star <note>Janma-nakshatra</note>. || 67 ||

For those whose birth details are unknown, one should consider only the name-sign. For the compatibility of husband and wife...

### On the Purity of the Year and Other Factors for Marriage

From the time of conception or from the day of birth, an odd-numbered year is considered best for the mother. A girl’s marriage should be performed within ten years. (200) An eight-year-old girl is called *Gauri*; at nine years old she is *Rohini*; at ten she is known as a *Kanyaka* <note>literally "maiden"</note>; beyond that, she is considered *Rajasvala* <note>pubescent/having reached menarche</note>. (201)

#### Specific Considerations
For a *Gauri* bride, the strength of Jupiter is paramount; for a *Rohini*, the strength of the Sun and Mars is sought. For a *Kanyaka*, the strength of the Moon should be accepted, and beyond that age, the strength of the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is most important. (202)

### On the Strength of the Sun, Jupiter, and Moon in Marriage
Wise men should ensure the strength of Jupiter for the bride and the strength of the Sun for the groom at the time of the wedding; the strength of the Moon is necessary for both. (203)

### On the Strength of Jupiter for the Bride and Groom
Jupiter is most auspicious when positioned in the 2nd, 5th, 7th, 9th, or 11th house from the birth sign <note>Janma Rashi</note> for the marriage of a girl or the initiation ceremony <note>Vrata/Upanayana</note> of a boy. (104)

Positions in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 1st houses are considered mediocre. The 4th, 8th, and 12th houses are undesirable. (105) A "mediocre" position can be remedied by a single ritual offering <note>Puja</note>; the 4th and 12th positions require a double offering to become acceptable. (105) Even a Jupiter in the 8th house, which is usually very ill-omened due to the passage of time, can be made favorable through a triple ritual offering, or if Jupiter is in its sign of exaltation, its own sign, its friend's sign, its own division <note>Navamsha</note>, or is in the best division of a sign <term>Vargottama</term>. <margin>68</margin> <margin>50</margin> (106)

Jupiter in the 4th, 8th, or 12th houses may be auspicious under these special conditions, but even a normally "good" position becomes "bad" if the planet is in a debilitated or enemy sign, or if it is in retrograde or rapid motion...

**Now, the results of the Sun for the groom:** The Sun is auspicious for the groom at the time of <term>marriage</term> <note>original: "ihane," likely a scribal error for "udvahane"</note> if it is in the third, sixth, tenth, or eleventh house. <margin>Muhūrta Mārtanda 100</margin> It is said: One should perform the previously mentioned auspicious ceremonies <note>original: "maṅgala"; refers here to weddings or initiations</note> even if the years differ. Furthermore: || 117 || In times of distress or necessity, these may be performed after an interval of four days, or even one day, provided they take place across a river, a mountain, or in different houses. || 118 || Likewise, if there are different pavilions <note>original: "maṇḍapa"; the temporary ritual hall erected for weddings</note> and different ascendants <note>original: "lagna"; the specific astrological moment of the ceremony</note>, it is appropriate. However, for twins, there is a special rule: all their rituals should be performed together. || 119 || Two daughters born of the same womb should not be given to the same man; nor should two brothers marry two sisters <insert>at the same time</insert>. || 120 || After a marriage has been fixed, if a death occurs in the family of the bride or the groom—such as a father or close relative—the marriage is not auspicious for them <insert>at that time</insert>. || 121 || However, after one year, or after six months, or even after the period of death-impurity <term>Sūtaka: a period of ritual impurity following a birth or death</term> has passed, the marriage <note>original: "karagraha"; literally 'taking the hand'</note> is praised if preceded by a peace-making ritual <term>Śānti: a propitiatory rite to remove obstacles or ill-fortune</term>. || 122 ||

### Auspicious Months for Marriage

When the Sun is in Aries, Scorpio, Aquarius, Capricorn, Gemini, or Taurus, the taking of the hand <note>marriage</note> is excellent. In Gemini, it is permitted until the time Lord Vishnu goes to sleep <note>refers to Shayani Ekadashi, the start of the four-month period when weddings usually cease</note>. || 123 || The months of Vaishakha, Phalguna, Magha, and Jyeshtha are the givers of auspiciousness. For weddings, the month of Margashirsha is considered middling, while others are considered inauspicious. || 124 || When the Sun is present in Scorpio, Capricorn, or Aries, then the months of Karttika, Pausha, and Chaitra are also beautiful for marriage. || 125 || When the Sun is in Sagittarius during Margashirsha, or in Pisces during Phalguna, it is auspicious. In all matters of vows and weddings, the solar month is generally praised. || 126 || <margin>100</margin> <margin>||</margin> However, the lunar month is the preferred standard in the regions south of the Vindhya Mountains. In rituals such as marriage, the alignment of both the solar and lunar months is considered most auspicious. || 127 || Now...

# Auspicious Lunar Days and Stars for Marriage

The sixth, the new moon <note>original: "darśa"</note>, the eighth, the "empty" days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, termed 'Riktā' or empty</note>, and the final five days of the dark fortnight are forbidden. The first day of the bright fortnight is also not desired; all other lunar days are considered beautiful and auspicious. || 128 ||

The days of the gentle planets <note>Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday</note> are auspicious; Sunday and Saturday are mediocre, while Tuesday is the lowest <note>considered inauspicious for marriage</note>. The lunar mansions <term>Nakṣatras: the 27 divisions of the sky through which the moon passes</term> of Rohiṇī, the three "Uttara" stars, Mūla, Maghā, Anurādhā, Revatī, and Mṛgaśīrṣa || 129 || 

as well as Hastā, Svātī, and Mṛgaśīrṣa are considered beautiful for "taking the hand" <note>original: "kara pīḍanam," a poetic term for the marriage ceremony</note>. Marriage in Pūrva Phālgunī is acceptable for a widow <note>original: "sītoḍhā"</note>, but otherwise, it is not considered good. || 130 ||

**Now, the Ten Great Flaws in Marriage:**
Obstruction <note>Vedha</note>, the fall <note>Pāta</note>, conjunction, declination, the "kick" <note>Latta</note>, the bolt <note>Argala</note>, the "burnt" day, the minor planets, the seventh-house flaw <note>Jāmitra</note>—these ten flaws must be abandoned. || 131 ||

**Now, the Flaw of Obstruction (Vedha):**
Five lines are drawn vertically and five horizontally, with two lines drawn at each of the corners. This diagram, known as the "Five-Spoke Chart" <term>Pañcaśalākā Cakra: a grid used to calculate planetary interference</term>, is the means for determining obstruction in marriage. || 132 ||

Starting from the second line, one should write the stars beginning with Abhijit and Kṛttikā in clockwise order. The planets should be placed there in their respective positions. || 133 ||

If a planet and the marriage star fall on the same line, an "obstruction" occurs. One must especially avoid a star pierced by malefic planets; however, a piercing by a benefic planet may be acceptable. || 134 ||

The last quarter of Uttarāṣāḍhā and the first part of Śravaṇa constitute the auspicious Abhijit. A planet placed there pierces Rohiṇī. || 135 ||

**Now, Examples of Obstruction for Marriage Stars:**
There is a mutual obstruction between Revatī and Uttarā Phālgunī; between Bharaṇī and Anurādhā; between Maghā and Śravaṇa; and between Svātī and Śatabhiṣā. || 136 || The obstruction...

Then the fault of the piercing does not apply to marriages and other ceremonies; this is the opinion of the wise.

### On the Flaw of Conjunction (Yuti Dosha)

When the Moon occupies the same zodiac sign as the lord of that sign or another planet, it is called the "Conjunction Flaw" <note>original: "sagraha-dosha"</note>. This should be avoided in auspicious ceremonies. However, some authorities say this flaw does not apply if the Moon is with Mercury or Jupiter. || 143 ||

### Exceptions to the Conjunction Flaw

If the Moon is in its own sign, in its sign of exaltation, or in the sign of a friendly planet; or if Jupiter is in its own divisional chart <note>varga: sub-divisions of a sign used to calculate planetary strength</note>; or if a strong Moon is positioned in a cardinal house <note>kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> || 144 ||
and they cast their gaze <note>aspect/drishti</note> upon the flaw, then this planetary collection destroys the evil effect.

### Another Variety of Conjunction Flaw

When the Moon's sign is occupied by a malefic planet <note>papa-graha: such as Mars or Saturn</note>, the Conjunction Flaw is said to arise. || 145 ||

### Exception to the Above

Some authorities state that if the planets are in different constellations <note>nakshatras</note>, even if in the same zodiac sign, one need not avoid the conjunction with a malefic. || 146 ||

### On the Flaw of Equal Declination (Krānti Sāmya)

By drawing three vertical lines and three horizontal lines, the wise should understand Equal Declination <note>Krānti Sāmya: when the Sun and Moon have the same declination, often considered highly inauspicious</note>; Pisces should be placed in the center. || 147 ||
When the Sun and Moon are situated respectively in: Aries and Leo, Libra and Aquarius, Taurus and Gemini, Cancer and Scorpio, Virgo and Pisces, or Sagittarius and Pisces—then the occurrence of Equal Declination is known to be possible. || 148 ||

### Another Way Equal Declination Occurs

In the fourth part of the *Vriddhi* Yoga, or at the beginning of the *Brahma* Yoga <note>yogas: specific angular relationships between the Sun and Moon</note>, the birth of Equal Declination generally occurs there as well. || 149 ||

### Only Mathematically Calculated Declination is to be Avoided

If the previously mentioned possibilities are confirmed by mathematical calculation <note>ganitāgatam</note>, then Equal Declination is undesirable; otherwise, the mere possibility is considered of middling effect. || 150 ||

### The Five-Fold Flaw Popular in the South
Now, the "Five-Fold Flaw" <term>Panchakam: a set of five inauspicious conditions</term> famous among the Southerners:
Take the lunar days <note>Tithis</note> starting from the first of the bright fortnight, add the number of the rising sign <note>Lagna</note>, and divide the total by the number of the planets <note>nine</note>. If the remainders are eight (8), two (2), four (4), six (6), or one (1), these are the five "Arrows" <note>Bana-Panchakam</note>. **161**

These represent, in order: **Sickness, Fire, the King, Thieves, and Death.** This system is well-known among the people of the South; one should avoid these during auspicious works. **162**

### Exceptions to the Five-Fold Flaw
Now, the rules for their exceptions:
Avoid the "Thief" flaw at night and the "King" flaw during the day. Avoid the "Death" flaw during both twilights <note>dawn and dusk</note>. The "Fire" flaw must be avoided at all times. **163**

On Sunday, avoid "Sickness"; on Saturday, avoid "the King"; on Wednesday, avoid "Death." On Tuesday, avoid "Fire" and "Thieves." However, if the rising sign <note>Lagna</note> is possessed of great strength, the flaw of the *Panchaka* does not apply. **164**

Specifically: avoid "Sickness" during illness, "Fire" when building a house, "Thieves" when traveling on a road, "Death" during a marriage, and avoid the general *Panchaka* when entering service. **165**

### The "Burned" Lunar Days
Now, the "Burned" Days <term>Dagdha Tithi: specific lunar days considered scorched or unlucky when the Sun is in certain zodiac signs</term>:
When the Sun is in Sagittarius or Pisces, the 2nd lunar day is burned. In Taurus or Aquarius, the 4th. In Cancer or Aries, the 6th. In Gemini or Virgo, the 8th. **166**

In Leo or Scorpio, the 10th is known as burned. In Libra or Capricorn, the 12th. These lunar days are "burned" and are not auspicious for marriages and other ceremonies. **167**

### The Flaw of Secondary Planets
<margin>205</margin>
Now, the flaw of the Secondary Planets <term>Upagraha Dosha</term>:
The 8th, 5th, 18th, or 14th; the 22nd, 19th, <margin>7</margin> and the 23rd. **168**

And the 24th—these are the lunar mansions <note>Nakshatras</note> counted from the position of the Sun... <insert>va-</insert>

<margin>Mu. Ga. 106</margin> The constellations known as *Kanti* and *Upagraha* <note>Upagraha refers to secondary or 'shadow' planets that exert a malefic influence</note> should be avoided in marriages and similar rites. || 169 || The 10th, 7th, 15th, 25th, and 21st stars <note>original: "Dik (10), Sapta (7), Tithi (15), Tattva (25), Svarga (21)"</note> from a specific point are also called *Upagraha* stars by some authorities. || 170 ||

### Now, the specific quarter of a star to be abandoned when afflicted by Latta, Upagraha, or Pata faults:
Whichever quarter <term>charana|one-fourth of a lunar mansion</term> the planet occupies, that specific quarter should be avoided. In the cases of *Latta* <note>the 'kick' or planetary transit influence</note>, *Upagraha*, and *Pata* <note>astrological nodes or falls</note>, the other quarters of the star need not be rejected. || 171 ||

### Now, the Jamitra fault:
If a planet is situated in the seventh house from either the Moon or the Ascendant <term>Lagna|the rising sign on the eastern horizon</term>, then the *Jamitra* fault occurs. If it is a cruel planet <note>a natural malefic like Mars or Saturn</note>, it is extremely condemned. || 172 || Some say the *Jamitra* fault occurs in the 14th constellation; others say it occurs in the 55th division <note>navamsha</note> of the zodiac. || 173 ||

### Now, the exceptions for Latta, Upagraha, Jamitra, Ekargala, and Kartari faults:
The faults of *Latta*, *Upagraha*, *Jamitra*, *Pata*, *Ekargala*, and *Kartari* <note>the 'scissors' formation where a house is hemmed in by malefics</note> are destroyed if the Sun is placed in the Ascendant, even if it is otherwise weak. || 174 ||

### Now, the regional application of these faults:
In the regions of Kuru and Bahlika <note>ancient regions in North/Northwest India and Bactria</note>, one should avoid the *Upagraha* star. In Saurashtra and Malwa, avoid *Latta*. In Vanga and Kalinga <note>modern Bengal and Odisha</note>, avoid *Pata*. || 175 || Avoid *Ekargala* in Kashmir, but a pierced star <term>viddhabha|a constellation obstructed by a planet</term> must be avoided in all countries. || 176 ||

### Now, the Twenty-One Great Faults:
First are the faults arising from the five limbs of the calendar <term>Panchanga|Tithi, Vara, Nakshatra, Yoga, and Karana</term>, then the Solar Transit <term>Sankranti</term>, and the 'Scissors' formation <term>Kartari</term>. Then <margin>106</margin> planetary conjunctions, the bad ninth-division <term>Kunavamsha</term>, the 'junction-end' <term>Gandanta</term>, and the 'bolt' <term>Ekargala</term>. || 177 || Also the faults of the 'bad moment' <term>Dukshana</term>, the day of the week, and the malefic star...

## Muhurta Ganapati

-> Poisonous Intervals <-

...Poisonous intervals <note>original: "Vishanadika"; specific periods of time considered inauspicious within a lunar mansion</note>, pierced stars <note>original: "Viddhaksha"; stars affected by the aspect of malefic planets</note>, the equalization of declination <note>original: "Kanti-samya"; a phenomenon where the sun and moon have equal declination, often called Mahapata</note>, and unseasonal rain. || 178 ||

The star in which an eclipse occurs, the fall of a meteor, a malefic planet occupying its own sixfold division <note>original: "Shadvarga"; a system of six types of divisional charts used to assess planetary strength</note>, as well as the mid-point of the ascendant <note>original: "Madhyasalagna"; the meridian or 10th house cusp</note> relative to the birth ascendant and the Moon's sign. || 179 ||

The Moon positioned at the end of the sixth division <note>original: "Shadashantya"</note>, Venus in the sixth house, Mars in the eighth house, and the presence of a malefic in the ascendant or the seventh house. These are declared to be the **Twenty-One Great Evils**. || 180 ||

Those desiring auspicious results should abandon these Great Evils in marriage and other ceremonies. However, for works other than marriage, one need not worry about the defect of the seventh house <note>original: "Jamitra"; the seventh house from the moon or ascendant</note>. || 181 ||

### The Scissor's Defect (Kartari Dosha)

Now, those defects remaining from the previous discussion are written. If malefic planets are situated in the second and twelfth houses from either the Ascendant or the Moon, it is known as **Kartari** <note>literally "the scissors," where the beneficial point is "cut" by malefics on either side</note>. One should avoid this. || 182 ||

### Exceptions to the Scissor's Defect

If the cruel planets in the second and twelfth houses from the Ascendant are in the same degree as the Ascendant, then the defect born of Kartari exists; otherwise, the result is different. || 183 ||

If a planet in the second house is moving forward and one in the twelfth is retrograde, or if both are moving forward or both are retrograde, or if a benefic planet is in the twelfth house from the Ascendant, then it is not considered Kartari. || 184 ||

If Venus, Jupiter, or Mercury are in the angles <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> or trines <note>1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, then Kartari does not cause suffering. If the malefic planets causing the Kartari are in an enemy's sign or their sign of debilitation, || 185 || or if they are combust <note>hidden by the sun's light</note>, then there is no defect arising from Kartari. || 186 ||

### Poisonous Intervals of the Lunar Mansions

The poisonous intervals <note>Visha Ghatika</note> for the stars are as follows: 50, 24, 30, 40, 14, 21, 30, 20, 32, 30, and 20 <note>these numbers represent the starting 'ghatikas' or units of time within specific Nakshatras where the poisonous effect begins</note>. || 187 ||

### Regarding the Lunar Days (Tithis)

### Regarding the Poisonous Periods of Weekdays (Vāra)

Twenty (20), two (2), twelve (12), ten (10), seven (7), five (5), and twenty-five (25) are the measures in sequence <note>corresponding to the seven days of the week starting with Sunday</note>. From these, a duration of four <term>nāḍīs</term> is considered poisonous, starting from the beginning of the day. || 193 ||

### Exceptions to the Defects of Poisonous Periods

If Jupiter is situated in the 5th, 8th, or 4th house, or if a friendly benefic planet or Venus is present; or if the Moon is in its own sign, its own division (<term>amsha</term>), or in the Ascendant (<term>lagna</term>), or in a cardinal (<term>kendra</term>) or trine (<term>trikoṇa</term>) house (1, 4, 7, 10, 5, 8). || 194 || This destroys all the defects arising from the poisonous periods. || 195 ||

### Regarding Planetary Aspects (Graha Dṛṣṭi)

A planet sees with a quarter-glance the 3rd and 10th houses; with a half-glance the 5th and 9th houses (the trines); with a three-quarter glance the 4th and 8th houses; and with a full glance the 7th house. <note>In Vedic astrology, "aspects" or dṛṣṭi refer to the influence a planet exerts on other houses from its current position.</note> || 196 ||

This verse describes the 'Drishti' or planetary aspects used in Vedic astrology to determine how planets influence different areas of a birth chart.</note>

### On the Purification of the Ascendant and the Seventh House
If the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the rising sign on the eastern horizon</term>, or the degree of the Ascendant, is occupied or aspected by Venus, or if the lord of the Ascendant is aspected by or associated with a natural benefic or a friend, it is auspicious for the master (the groom). || 188 || If the degree of the seventh house <term>Dyuna-amsha; the portion of the chart related to marriage and partners</term> is in the seventh house, or if that degree is occupied or aspected by its own lord, or if the lord of the seventh house is aspected by a friend, it is considered auspicious for the bride. || 189 || If the lord of the Ascendant aspects the Ascendant or its own division, or if they aspect each other, it should be known as auspicious for the person; otherwise, it is not good. || 200 || If the lord of the seventh house aspects the seventh house or the degree of the seventh house, or if they are in a friendly relationship, then it is auspicious for the bride; otherwise, it is not. || 201 ||

### On the Lame and Blind Ascendants to be Avoided
During the day, Aquarius is known as "lame"; Libra and Scorpio are "deaf"; Aries, Taurus, and Leo are "blind"; and the three signs starting with Capricorn are "hunchbacked." || 202 || At night, Taurus is "lame"; Virgo, Gemini, and Cancer are "blind"; Sagittarius and Capricorn are "deaf." At those specific times, one should avoid these signs for auspicious works. || 203 ||

### On the Eight Days Before Holi
In the regions of the Airavati, Vipasha, and Shutudri rivers, and at the three Pushkaras, one should avoid the eight days preceding the Holi festival <term>Holika-ashtaka</term> for marriages and other auspicious ceremonies. || 204 || <note>These rivers correspond to the modern Ravi, Beas, and Sutlej in the Punjab region.</note>

### Exceptions for Certain Regions
The faults of "void months" <term>Masa-shunya</term> and "lame" Ascendants should be avoided specifically in the regions of Malwa and Gauda <note>Malwa is in Central India; Gauda refers to Bengal</note>; elsewhere, they are not traditionally considered to bring inauspicious results. || 205 || A strong Jupiter situated in a quadrant <term>Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> destroys the defects arising from lunar days or "void" dates, just as water quenches fire. || 206 || <margin>206</margin>

### On the Transitional Periods
<note>The text breaks off here at the beginning of a discussion on Ganda-antara, the inauspicious "junction" points between certain zodiac signs and lunar mansions.</note>
