<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological remedies, specifically focusing on how strong planetary positions can neutralize various inauspicious timings or "doshas."</meta>

<margin>Su. Ga. 110</margin> **Exceptions** <note>original: "apavāda"; refers to rules that cancel out negative astrological influences</note> || A strong planet destroys the defect arising from the *Tithi Gandanta* <note>the "knot-end" or transition point between certain lunar days</note>. A strong Jupiter, worshipped by Indra himself, destroys the defect born of *Lagna Gandanta* <note>the transition point between zodiac signs at the horizon</note>. || Even the most difficult *Atigandanta* <note>an inauspicious astrological yoga</note> is rendered auspicious by the presence of the *Abhijit* <note>the "victorious" 28th intercalary lunar mansion</note> and favorable planets. || 207 ||

### Now, the Exception for Kulika and Yamaghanta

If the Lord of the Day <note>the planet ruling the specific day of the week</note> is naturally strong and situated in the Ascendant (*Lagna*), it destroys the defect arising from *Kulika* <note>a specific inauspicious period of the day</note>. || 208 ||
If a benefic planet is in a Cardinal house (*Kendra*) <note>houses 1, 4, 7, or 10</note> or a Trinal house (*Trikona*) <note>houses 1, 5, or 9</note> from the Ascendant, it destroys the defect born of *Yamaghanta* <note>literally "the bell of Death," a highly malefic time</note>, which is otherwise like poison. || 209 ||

### Now, the Exception for Defects of the Birth Ascendant

Taurus, Scorpio, Pisces, Virgo, Capricorn, and Cancer: if these are the Ascendant, the defects associated with the Birth Star (*Janma Tara*) and others are neutralized. || 210 ||

### Now, the Exception for "Blind" and "One-Eyed" Ascendants

Defects arising from the year, solstice, season, month, lunar mansion (*Nakshatra*), fortnight, or "burnt" days <note>dagdha tithi</note>, as well as those arising from "Blind" or "One-Eyed" Ascendants <note>Andha/Kana Lagna; specific signs considered "vision-impaired" at certain times</note>, are destroyed by the Moon's *Navamsha* <note>the ninth divisional chart</note>. These defects perish when Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus are placed in Cardinal or Trinal houses. || 211 ||

### Now, the Exception for Defects of the Bad Navamsha

The defects arising from a bad *Navamsha* <note>the 1/9th divisional chart</note> are removed by those skilled in the *Shadvarga* <note>the six divisional charts used to determine planetary strength</note>. If all planets are in the eleventh house from the Moon, all defects are certainly destroyed. || 212 || **Now, the Exception for All Defects** || If the Ascendant is in *Vargottama* <note>the best division; when a planet is in the same sign in both the main chart and the 9th divisional chart</note>, or if the Lord of the Day is in the eleventh house, or if Jupiter is in a Cardinal or Trinal house, all defects whatsoever perish. || 213 || Mercury in a Cardinal, Trinal, or the seventh house (*Jamitra*) destroys a hundred defects. Venus destroys two hundred, and Jupiter <margin>110</margin> <note>referred to here as "Lord of Speech"</note> destroys many more. || 214 || If the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Day is situated in the eleventh or a Cardinal house, he quickly burns away a heap of defects like fire burns a pile of cotton.

<summary>This page details the "Apavada" or exceptions that neutralize various inauspicious astrological timings (doshas), emphasizing that strong placements of Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury can overcome even the most dreaded omens like Yamaghanta.</summary>

### On the Auspicious Ninth-Divisions for Marriage
The ninth-divisions <term>Navamshas: a 1/9th sub-division of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuning predictions</term> of Virgo, Gemini, Sagittarius, and Libra are considered to grant auspicious results. Others have declared that the ninth-division of Pisces is also auspicious for marriage. || 216 || Excluding the <term>Vargottama: a planet that occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth-division chart, increasing its strength</term>, a marriage should not take place in the final ninth-division of a sign. A movable ninth-division is acceptable if the rising sign is also movable, provided the Moon is situated in Libra or Capricorn. || 217 ||

### On Planets that Cause the Ruin of the Marriage Ascendant
Saturn or the Sun in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ceremony</term>; the Moon in the Ascendant, the sixth house <note>the house of enemies</note>, or the eighth house <note>the house of death</note>; Mars in the Ascendant or the eighth house; or Mercury and Jupiter in the eighth house [all cause ruin]. || 218 || Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> or the Sun in the Ascendant; Venus located in the eighth or sixth houses; or any of the planets located in the seventh house <note>the house of partnership</note> are remembered as "ruin-causers" in a marriage. || 219 || Mars in the twelfth house; Saturn in the Ascendant; the Moon in the eighth or sixth houses; or Venus and the Moon in the third house—these are not beautiful <note>auspicious</note> when in the twelfth house. || 220 ||

### On Exceptions to the Ruining Planets
If Venus is in an enemy's sign or its sign of debilitation, it does not cause a defect even if placed in the sixth house. Similarly, Mars in the eighth house is not inauspicious if it is in an enemy's sign, its debilitation, or is combust <note>too close to the Sun</note>. || 221 || The Moon, even if in its sign of debilitation or a debilitated ninth-division, is not inauspicious if placed in the sixth or twelfth houses. If a strong Jupiter, Venus, or Moon is in the Ascendant or in an auspicious division, or if they are aspected by a benefic planet, they surely destroy the defect caused by the Moon being in the twelfth or sixth houses. || 222 ||

### On Planets that Bestow "Lines" of Merit in the Marriage Ascendant
The Sun, Saturn, Ketu <note>the South Node</note>, and Rahu bestow "lines" <term>Rekha: points of merit or auspiciousness in a chart</term> when placed in the eighth, sixth, third, or eleventh houses. Mars bestows merit in the eleventh, third, or sixth houses, and the Moon in the fourth, third, or eleventh houses. || 223 || Mercury bestows merit in any house except the Ascendant, the seventh, the sixth, or the twelfth. Jupiter is auspicious except in the seventh or the eighth. Venus bestows merit and is auspicious except in the eighth, the seventh, the sixth, or the twelfth houses. || 224 ||

### On Planets Giving Middling Results

<margin>Sū. Ga. <note>Abbreviation for Sūrya-Gaṇita or a similar astronomical treatise.</note>
112</margin> ...from that which is similar, the planets located elsewhere possess their mean results. (225)

### The Strength of the Planets (Viṃśopaka)

Now, the <term>Viṃśopaka</term><note>A unit of strength or value in Indian astrology, similar to a 20-point scale.</note> values for the planets:
The Sun is three and a half; Mars and Saturn are one and a half; the Ascending and Descending Nodes (Rāhu and Ketu) are also one and a half. Venus and Mercury are two; the Moon is five; and Jupiter is three. (226)
A marriage should take place during a favorable <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon.</note> when the strength exceeds the required Viṃśopaka points. (227)

### Knowledge of the Desired Time (Iṣṭakāla)

Now, the method for knowing the <term>Iṣṭakāla</term><note>The specific elapsed time from sunrise to a given event.</note>:
Multiply the day-length of the following day by nine (9) and divide by the lunar date (15). The resulting value in digits (<term>aṅgula</term>) and their fractions is the midday shadow. (228)
If that midday shadow is subtracted [from the current shadow], then multiply the remainder by seven and two <note>original: "sapta yugdi guṇā", likely referring to a multiplier of 14 or a specific geometric constant</note>. Divide the product by the sum [of the shadows]; the result obtained will be the time in <term>nāḍikās</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note>. (229)
If the Sun is in the west, these are the remaining hours of the day; if the Sun is in the east, they are known as the elapsed hours. The method for knowing the desired time at night has been explained previously in four ways. (230)

### Calculating the Ascendant from the Desired Time

Multiply the degrees of the Sun (including the <term>Ayanāṃśa</term><note>The longitudinal difference between the tropical and sidereal zodiacs.</note>) by the rising periods (<term>Udaya</term>) of its own sign in <term>vināḍikās</term><note>Units of 24 seconds.</note>. Divide by thirty (30); the result is the "remaining time" (<term>bhogya-kāla</term>) expressed in <term>palas</term>. (231)
Subtract this from the desired elapsed time (<term>ghaṭīs</term>), and then subtract the rising periods of the subsequent signs. Multiply the remainder by thirty (30) and divide by the rising period of the "un-subtracted" sign. (232)
The resulting degrees and minutes should be added to the sum of the signs that were fully subtracted (starting from Aries). Subtract the <term>Ayanāṃśa</term> from that; the result is the precise <term>Lagna</term> (Ascendant). (233)
<margin>112</margin> If the "remaining time" cannot be subtracted, then take the desired time in <term>palas</term>, multiply by thirty (30), and divide by its own rising period to get the degrees. (234)
<margin>||</margin> Add this to the previous calculation; the precise Ascendant is found by subtracting the <term>Ayanāṃśa</term>. This applies to the desired time at night as well.

The self-calculated sun and the previous values should be treated as before. || 235 || **Now, the calculation of the desired time from the clarified Ascendant <note>The 'Ascendant' or 'Lagna' is the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment.</note>:** The time remaining for the Sun to cross its current sign, added to the time already passed by the Ascendant in its sign, plus the rising times of the signs between them, equals the clarified time. || 236 || If the Sun and the Ascendant are in the same sign, the difference between their degrees should be multiplied by the rising time of that specific sign and divided by 30; this result is the clarified desired time. || 237 ||

**Now, the auspicious combinations for marriage:** When the benefic planets are situated in the Ascendant and the malefic planets are in the eighth house <note>The eighth house, or 'Nidhana,' is traditionally associated with longevity and obstacles.</note>, the young woman married under this "Banner" alignment becomes dearly beloved by her husband. || 238 || When Mars and the Sun are in the eleventh house, Saturn and the Moon are in the second house, and the other planets are in the ninth house <note>The text uses 'Dharma' for the ninth house, representing fortune and duty.</note>, this is the excellent "Shrivatsa" combination. || 239 || If the Ascendant is Virgo, with the Moon and Jupiter in the third house and Venus in the fifth, it is the "Ananda" <note>Ananda means 'Bliss.'</note> combination, which truly brings joy. || 240 ||

When the Sun is in the eleventh house, Mars is in the sixth house, and Saturn is in the third house, this is called the "Half-Moon" combination; a woman married then becomes auspicious and virtuous. || 241 || When Jupiter is in the ninth house, Mercury is in the first house, and Saturn is in the eleventh, it is called the "Janmadesha" combination; she who is married then is a witness to truth, chaste, and a provider of wealth and virtue. When the benefic planets are in the tenth, fourth, and ninth houses, this is the "Conch" combination, which always brings good fortune. || 242 ||

**Now, the undesirable combinations:** If the malefic planets are in the first half of the zodiac <note>The 'Chakra' or wheel of the zodiac.</note> and the benefics are in the second half, this is the "Wheel" combination for marriage; the woman married then may become unfaithful. || 243 || If all malefic planets are in the center houses <note>The 'Kendra' or angular houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th.</note>, it is the "Cistern" combination, which is also condemned. If benefics are in the first house and malefics are in the twelfth and second houses, it is the "Bow" combination, which is also [inauspicious]...

->**Drunkard**<- <note>original: "Matta"</note> || 244 || When Mars is in the 12th house, Venus in the 6th, and Saturn in the 4th, this is declared the **Axe Yoga** <note>original: "Kuthara Yoga"</note>; it acts as a cutter of the vine of auspiciousness. || 245 || When the 12th house and the centers <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> are empty, Venus is in the 6th, Saturn in the 8th, and the Moon is present, this is the **Action Yoga** <note>original: "Karma Yoga"</note>; a newlywed woman under this sign may become a beggar. || 246 || With the Sun in the 1st house, Saturn in the 12th, and Mars in the 8th, it is called the **Pestle Yoga** <note>original: "Musala Yoga"</note>. ### Regarding the Cow-Dust Ascendant
<note>Godhuli Lagna: A specific time at sunset when cows return from the fields, kicking up dust; considered a powerful "emergency" time for weddings.</note>

One should avoid the Moon in the 1st, 8th, or 6th houses during the Cow-Dust period, as well as times afflicted by the transition of the sun <note>original: "Kranti"</note> or conjunctions with malefic planets. || 248 || Other faults, such as the position of Mars and others, need not be considered during Cow-Dust, because the dust raised by the cows' hooves provides a universal remedy for all directions. || 249 || If the Moon is in the 2nd, 3rd, or 11th house, the Cow-Dust time is considered excellent. If the Moon is anywhere other than the 1st, 7th, or 6th houses, it is considered middling. || 250 || Faults arising from the five limbs of the calendar <note>Panchanga</note>, the birth sign, the planets, or the 7th house; or those arising from the "kick" of a planet <note>Latta</note>, the 8th house position, or the nine-fold division <note>Navamsha</note>— || 251 || all these and others are never to be feared during the Cow-Dust time. || 252 ||

### Regarding the Time of Cow-Dust

The period of "Cow-Dust" <note>Goraja</note> is half a Ghatika <note>approx. 12 minutes</note> before and half a Ghatika after the sun's center crosses the horizon. This time is auspicious and brings good fortune for marriages and other rites. Some say it lasts for two Ghatikas <note>approx. 48 minutes</note> after the sun's disk has set. || 253 || In the summer, it is when half the sun's disk is visible; in the winter, when the sun appears as a solid orb; and in the rainy season, it is when the sun has fully set—these are the auspicious Cow-Dust times. || 254 || When a specific auspicious hour <note>Ghatika Lagna</note> cannot be found...

### The Auspicious and Inauspicious Effects of the Ascendant on In-laws in Marriage

The Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> represents the body; Venus represents the mother-in-law; the Sun represents the father-in-law; and the Moon represents the mind. The husband is represented by the seventh house, and the wife by the same. From the strength of these planets, their respective happiness is determined. || 256 ||

One should judge the husband from the Sun, the wife from the Moon, wealth from Mars, and children from Mercury. Happiness is determined from Jupiter, religious duty from Venus, and the home from Saturn; these should be analyzed from their root positions. || 257 ||

Happiness is known through planets situated in their own or exalted signs, while sorrow is known through planets that are debilitated or combust <note>hidden by the Sun's light</note>. || 258 ||

### The Sword Marriage for Kings

Now, the necessary <term>Sword Marriage</term> <note>Khadga Vivaha; a proxy marriage where a bride is wed to the groom's sword, often used by royalty when the groom was away at war</note> for Kings: When the Moon and the five limbs of the calendar <note>Panchanga</note> are purified, during the northern transit of the sun <note>Uttarayana</note> and under a marriage star, the Sword Marriage is praised for a King during the <term>Godhuli</term> <note>the "cow-dust" twilight hour</note> ascendant. || 259 ||

### Special Rules for the Marriage of Lower Classes

For those of mixed or lower castes, even dates, months, and lunar mansions not usually mentioned can be auspicious. In their marriages, such timings can bring wealth, sons, long life, affection, and happiness. || 260 ||

### Remarriage for Shudras and Others

Now, regarding the <term>Remarriage</term> <note>Punah-vivaha</note> of women among Shudras and others: The remarriage of a woman should be performed at night under a marriage star. In such cases, the usual faults of the lunar date or month are not considered. || 261 ||

Counting from the Sun's nakshatra <note>star</note>: the 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th, 11th, 15th, 21st, 24th, 25th, and 28th stars are considered in marriages such as the Gandharva <note>love marriage</note> style. || 262 ||

<margin>7</margin> These stars are declared by the wise to be unfavorable or favorable in their respective order for women. || 263 ||

Now, the rituals that are limbs of the marriage: These subordinate marriage acts <margin>215</margin> should be performed using the stars prescribed for marriage. One should avoid a weak Moon and the third, eighth, or ninth lunar days. || 264 || Vishakha, Bharani...

Except for the constellations of Chitra, Jyeshtha, Ashvini, and Shatabhisha, and excluding the four quarters of Ardra, one should perform the marriage rites. <note>original: "चित्राज्येष्ठाख्यामश्विनीशतम्... आर्द्राचतुष्टयंहित्वा"</note> ॥ 265 ॥ Thus, the worship, the oil ceremony, the entire offering of sprouts <term>ankurarpanam: a ritual sowing of seeds to symbolize fertility</term>, the wearing of ornaments and the wedding bracelet <term>kankana</term>, and the preparation of the altar and pavilion should be done. ॥ 266 ॥

### Regarding the Wedding Altar

One should construct a beautiful, square altar <term>vedika</term> four cubits in size and one cubit in height. It should be placed on the left side of the house and adorned with pillars. ॥ 268 ॥ The first pillar should be placed in the Northeast when the Sun is in Leo and the following two signs; in the Northwest when in Scorpio and the following two; in the Southwest when in Aquarius and the following two; and in the Southeast when in Taurus and the following two. The digging for the pillars should follow this same order. ॥ 269 ॥

### Regarding the Removal of the Pavilion after Marriage

The removal of the pavilion <term>mandapa-udvasanam</term> should be performed on an even-numbered day, excluding the sixth day. It may be done on the fifth or seventh day, provided it is an auspicious lunar mansion and an auspicious day of the week. ॥ 270 ॥

### Regarding the Bride’s Entry into her New Home

The entry of the bride <term>vadhu pravesha</term> is auspicious on even-numbered days from the wedding day, or on the fifth or seventh day. After sixteen days, or after a month or a year has passed, ॥ 271 ॥ the entry of the bride is considered auspicious whenever desired. The entry is beneficial under the two Shravanas <note>Shravana and Dhanishta</note>, Mula, Anuradha, Rohini, Mrigashira, ॥ 272 ॥ the three Hastas <note>Hasta, Chitra, Svati</note>, Magha, Pushya, the three Uttaras, and the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>. It is auspicious on Monday, Friday, Thursday, and Saturday. ॥ 273 ॥ Wednesday is middling, while Tuesday and Sunday are not recommended, nor are the "Empty" <term>rikta: the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</term> dates. ## Regarding prohibitions in specific months during the first year after marriage

It is considered harmful if, in the first month of <term>Jyeshtha</term><note>May–June</note> after the wedding, the bride resides in her husband's house; it then destroys the husband's elder brother. Likewise, in the month of <term>Pausha</term><note>December–January</note>, it harms the father-in-law. || 275 ||
In the month of <term>Ashadha</term><note>June–July</note>, it harms the mother-in-law; in an <term>Adhikamasa</term><note>An intercalary or "extra" leap month in the Hindu calendar</note>, it harms her own husband. In a <term>Kshayamasa</term><note>A "lost" or omitted month in the lunar calendar</note>, it harms herself; and in the month of <term>Chaitra</term><note>March–April</note>, if she stays in her father's house, it harms her father. || 276 ||

## Regarding the Second Entry (Dviragamana)

In the odd-numbered years from the wedding <note>original: "vime varshe", referring to the 1st, 3rd, or 5th year</note>, when the Sun is in Aquarius, Aries, or Scorpio, and when the Sun, Moon, and Jupiter are strong, on an auspicious day under the <term>Nakshatras</term><note>Lunar mansions or star clusters</note> of Ashvini or Mrigashira; || 277 ||
Revati, Rohini, Pushya, the three "Uttaras" <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, the three "Hastas" <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Svati</note>, Punarvasu, Mula, and Anuradha; || 278 ||
When the <term>Lagna</term> is Virgo, Pisces, Libra, Gemini, or Taurus and is endowed with strength, the <term>Dviragamana</term><note>The ritual ceremony where the bride moves to her husband's home for the first time after the initial wedding</note> of the lotus-eyed women is desired. || 279 ||
One should never go when the planet Venus is directly in front or to the right; if a pregnant woman does so, she may lose the fetus, and a newlywed may become barren. || 280 ||
Even a child might perish if they depart from the home under such conditions. || 281 ||

## Regarding the exception to faults for the Festival of Lights (Deepavali) entry

Even if Jupiter is in Leo, or if Venus is in front or set <note>invisible in the sky</note>, the entry of the bride into the husband's house during the <term>Deepotsava</term><note>The Festival of Lights or Diwali</note> is considered auspicious. || 282 ||

## Regarding the exception to the fault of "Facing Venus" (Prati-Shukra)

In the same city, village, or country, or during a royal calamity, famine, or a pilgrimage to a holy site, or during the entry of a new bride; || 283 ||
The faults of "Facing Venus" or Mercury, which are usually considered harmful in marriage, are disregarded according to the lineages of Atri, Angiras, Vashistha, Vatsa, Kashyapa, and Bhrigu. <note>These are the names of the great sages or Gotras whose traditions provide these exemptions.</note> || 284 ||

<summary>This page outlines the astrological prohibitions for a bride's residence during her first year of marriage and details the auspicious timing and exceptions for the Dviragamana (second entry) ritual.</summary>

### The Establishment of the Sacred Fire
<note>Agnyadhana: The ritual of placing the sacrificial fires, marking a householder's commitment to daily Vedic rites.</note>

The establishment of the sacred fire is desired during the northern transit of the sun <note>Uttarayan: the period between the winter and summer solstices</note>, under the lunar mansions <note>Nakshatras</note> of Vishakha, Krittika, Rohini, Mrigashirsha, the three "Uttaras" <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Revati, Jyeshtha, and Pushya.

|| 1 ||

It should be performed on the days of Mars, the Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, provided these planets are not in their signs of debilitation, are not set <note>combust</note>, and are not in an enemy's sign. It should not be done on "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta Tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, nor when the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is Cancer, nor during the three signs of the Deer <note>referring to specific divisions of Capricorn</note>.

|| 2 ||

One should perform the fire-establishment when the Moon is not in the Ascendant. It is best when the Sun, Jupiter, Mars, and the Moon are in the Trines <note>1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, the Upachaya houses <note>3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th</note>, or the Quadrants <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note>.

|| 3 ||

With the remaining houses and the eighth house being pure <note>free of malefic planets</note>, the fire-establishment is excellent. One becomes initiated when Jupiter is in the Ascendant or in Sagittarius, or when the Sun is in the 7th, Mars is in the 10th, or when the Moon or Sun is in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses.

|| 5 ||

Thus ends the section on the Establishment of the Sacred Fire.

### The Royal Coronation
<note>Rajyabhisheka: The ritual anointing and crowning of a king.</note>

When Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus have risen, excluding the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, and during the Sun's northern transit; under the birth star of the king, or when the Sun and Mars are endowed with strength:

|| 1 ||

Under the two Rohinis <note>Rohini and Shravana</note>, Pushya, Jyeshtha, the three Uttaras, the two Revatis <note>Revati and Ashvini</note>, Hasta, Chitra, and also Shravana.

|| 2 ||

<margin>118</margin>

On an auspicious lunar day and weekday, and when the Sun is favorable, one should perform the Royal Coronation. This should occur in a "Fixed" Ascendant, an Upachaya house from the birth sign, and in a sign that rises by the head <note>Shirshodaya: signs considered more powerful and auspicious</note>.

|| 3 ||

**Now, the Consecration of the Crown Prince:**
The consecration of a Crown Prince <note>Yuvarajyabhisheka</note> should take place under the same lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> and conditions mentioned for the King's consecration. || 7 ||

**Now, Consecration of Another in the Absence of a Son:**
If a King of the same lineage dies and there is no son, another born of the same family should be consecrated in his place. In such a case, one should not worry about the lunar day <term>Tithi</term> or the weekday; he should be consecrated immediately. || 8 ||

The fly-whisk <note>Chamara</note>, the royal umbrella, the palanquin, and the throne should all be first used on an auspicious day, under the same stars prescribed for the royal coronation. || 9 ||

**Now, the Chapter on Expeditions:**
For kings whose birth details are known, the wise should plan a journey <note>Yatra; often referring to a military march or royal progress</note> after determining a period of auspicious results. For those whose birth details are unknown, the journey should be planned based on the grace of the moment <note>Prasada; likely referring to Prashna or horary astrology</note>. || 1 ||

**Now, the Auspicious and Inauspicious Results of Expeditions from the Query Ascendant:**
If the ascendant of the journey is the same as the birth sign, or if the lord of that sign is present, or if the journey's ascendant is in an 'Upachaya' house <note>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses, which signify growth</note> from those two, then victory for the King is certain. || 2 ||

<note>The following verse discusses the 'Kosha' or treasury/direction based on planetary positions</note>
Regarding the treasury: from Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and Venus—if the Moon is in the 4th from any of these, it is significant. If the Moon is in the treasury house, it is auspicious. || 11 || Whichever planet is stronger between the two determines the direction. In a query, the lord of the direction to be traveled and the Moon are the powerful planets to consider. || 12 ||

### The Results of Journeys during Solar Transits

When the Sun is in Aries <gloss>1</gloss>, Leo <gloss>5</gloss>, or Sagittarius <gloss>9</gloss>, a journey is said to be very auspicious. When in Aquarius <gloss>11</gloss>, Capricorn <gloss>10</gloss>, Virgo <gloss>6</gloss>, or Gemini <gloss>3</gloss>, the result is middling. || 13 || When the Sun is in Cancer <gloss>4</gloss>, Pisces <gloss>12</gloss>, or Scorpio <note>implied by context</note>, a journey results in a long, difficult exile. || 14 ||

<margin>Muhurtaganapati 121</margin> ->The three types of results for a journey based on planetary strength:<- A journey is considered excellent when the planetary periods <term>Dasha: A period of time in which a specific planet exerts its influence</term> are favorable. The second day leads to the accomplishment of tasks, and the third day brings safety and prosperity. || 17 || The fourth day is known to cause distress, while the fifth day brings gain. The sixth day causes pain from disease, and the seventh day provides enjoyments and good food. || 18 || The eighth day is known to bring illness, and the ninth day always brings the risk of death. The tenth day always brings gain, and the eleventh is remembered as being equal to the tenth. || 19 || The twelfth day is said to take away one's life-breath, while the thirteenth grants all success. The fourteenth of the bright fortnight is not desired, and it is especially avoided in the dark fortnight. || 20 || The Full Moon is said to be middling, but the New Moon must be avoided entirely. These are the results of the lunar days <term>Tithis</term> that should be known by the wise regarding travel. || 21 || **Now, the auspicious and inauspicious days of the week for travel:** Travel is excellent on the days of the Sun (Sunday) and the Moon (Monday), and on the day of the teacher (Thursday). On Wednesday, it is middling according to common folk, but this is not mentioned anywhere in the sacred scriptures. || 22 || **Now, travel at specific times of the day as mentioned by some:** <margin>/1</margin> One should go at sunrise on Sunday, and during the afternoon on Saturday. On Tuesday, one should always eat before departing; this is what has been said by some regarding the days of the week. <margin>121</margin> || 23 || **Now, the best, middling, and undesirable stars for travel:** Dhanishtha, Shravana, Hasta...

### Regarding the Directional Spike (Dik Shula)

The <term>Dik Shula</term> <note>literally "Directional Spike," a period when travel in a specific direction is considered harmful</note> is in the East on Saturdays and Mondays when the moon is in Jyeshtha. In the South, it occurs on Thursdays during Purva Bhadrapada. || 35 || On Sundays and Fridays, a wise man should avoid the West during Rohini. In the North, one should avoid travel on Tuesdays during Uttara Phalguni. || 36 ||

### Regarding the Spike in Intermediate Directions by Weekday

One should avoid traveling toward the Southeast on Thursdays and Mondays; toward the Southwest on Sundays and Fridays; toward the Northeast on Wednesdays; and toward the Northwest on Tuesdays. || 37 ||

### Rituals for Neutralizing the Fault of the Directional Spike

If travel is absolutely necessary, a person does not incur the fault of the <term>Dik Shula</term> if they obtain and consume the following: clarified butter <note>ghee</note> on Sunday, milk on Monday, jaggery on Tuesday, and sesame seeds on Wednesday. || 38 || Curd and barley on Thursday, honey on Friday, and similarly on Saturday. If one must go, they will not suffer the ill effects. Furthermore: betel leaf on Sunday and Monday; sandalwood is prescribed for Tuesday. || 39 || Flowers on Wednesday, curd on Thursday, clarified butter on Friday, and sesame seeds on Saturday. By giving, eating, or wearing these, the fault arising from the "Spike" yoga <note>astrological alignment</note> is removed. || 40 ||

### Stars to be Avoided at Specific Times

In the forenoon, the stars Rohini, Vishakha, Krittika, and the three "Uttara" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> should be avoided. At midday, Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, Ardra, and Mula are forbidden. || 41 || In the afternoon, Ashwini should always be avoided for travel. During the first part of the night, Chitra, Revati, and Anuradha are avoided. || 42 || In the middle part of the night, one should avoid the three "Purva" stars <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note> and Bharani...

The group of lunar days <note>Tithis</note> numbered eight, ten, and fifteen <note>original: "ashta-akashi-vimitah"</note> is known as the "Kula" <term>Kula: a category in divination meaning 'family' or 'stable group'</term> group. In battle, the attacker <note>the one who goes out</note> will face defeat, while the defender <note>the one who stays</note> will be victorious. || 54 ||

### Regarding the Kula-Akula Group
The lunar mansions <note>Nakshatras</note> of Abhijit, Mula, Ardra, and Shatabhisha, combined with Wednesday, and the <margin>148</margin> second, tenth, and sixth lunar days—this is the "Kula-Akula" <term>Kula-Akula: a 'mixed' category in divination</term> group. For a king setting out under this influence, there will be a peace treaty formed with his enemies. || 55 ||

### Natural Vowel-Sounds based on Name Initials
For those whose names begin with the letters Ka, Cha, Dha, Da, or Bha, the vowel-sound "A" is their natural birth-vowel. For the letters Kha, Ja, Ta, Na, Ma, or Sha, the vowel-sound is said to be "I". || 56 || The letters Ga, Ru, Ta, Pa, Dha, and Sha are assigned the vowel-sound "U". The letters Gha, Tha, Tha, Pha, Ra, and Sa are known by the vowel-sound "E". || 57 || The letters Ca, Da, Da, Sa, Ma, and Ha are declared to have the vowel-sound "O". If the initial letter is not present in these lists, one should determine the vowel-sound based on the primary phonetic quality of the name. || 58 ||

### Regarding the Vowel-Sound of Lunar Days
The five vowels starting with "A" <note>A, I, U, E, O</note> correspond to the five groups of lunar days starting with "Nanda" <note>the 1st, 6th, and 11th days</note> in sequence. One should recognize the strength of these vowels based on their corresponding lunar days. || 59 || The first stage is the Infant, the next is the Youth, then the Young Man, then the Old Man, and finally Death. The first <note>Infant</note> brings slight gain; the Youth brings half-success. || 60 || The Young Man achieves all success; the Old Man is mediocre, and the final stage <note>Death</note> is the lowest or worst. || 61 ||

<margin>128</margin>
### Regarding the Vowel-Sound of the Moment
From the horizontal movements of the planetary combinations <note>Yogas</note>, that which is the eleventh part is the <margin>125</margin> "Vowel of the Moment." This is remembered according to the sequence of its own state <note>as described in the stages of life above</note>. || 62 ||

### Regarding the Rahu-Cycle on the Path
In the mansions of Vishakha and Pushya—

The stars Anuradha, Ashvin <note>original: "shiva", referring to the Ashvins</note>, Shatabhisha, and Dhanishta belong to the category of **Dharma** <note>Righteousness or Duty</note>. These are auspicious for the path when the planet Venus is present. || 63 ||

The stars Bharani, Shravana, Jyeshtha, Magha, Swati, Punarvasu, and Purvabhadrapada—these stars are declared by the sages to belong to **Artha** <note>Material Prosperity or Wealth</note>. || 64 ||

Chitra, Ardra, Krittika, Mula, Purvaphalguni, Abhijit, and likewise Uttarabhadrapada—these seven are known to reside in the category of **Kama** <note>Desire or Pleasure</note>. || 65 ||

Uttaraphalguni, Hasta, Rohini, Revati, Mrigashira, and the two Ashadhas <note>Purvashadha and Uttarashadha</note>—the wise have declared these stars to belong to **Moksha** <note>Spiritual Liberation</note>. || 66 ||

When the Sun is situated in a Dharma-star, it is auspicious if the Moon is in a star of Moksha or Artha. When the Sun is in an Artha-star, the Moon is praised if it resides in Dharma or Moksha. || 67 ||

When the Sun has entered a Kama-star, the Moon is best in Moksha, Dharma, or Artha. When the Sun is situated in a Moksha-star, the Moon is auspicious in Dharma. || 68 ||

Otherwise, it should be known as inauspicious by those desiring success in travel <term>Yatra</term>. || 69 ||

### The Lunar Day Wheel according to the View of <term>Gorakshaka</term> <note>A famous medieval yogi and sage often associated with the Nath tradition and various esoteric sciences</note> and its Results in Travel.

In the months beginning with the bright fortnight: in the month of Pausha <note>December/January</note>, the lunar days <term>Tithis</term> begin with the 1st; in Magha <note>January/February</note>, they begin with the 2nd; in Phalguna <note>February/March</note>, they begin with the 3rd. || 70 ||

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati 127</margin> || 73 || Suffering, happiness, strife, death, and emptiness: these are the results in the **East**, in sequence. <note>These results correspond to specific mathematical remainders in astrological calculations.</note> Distress, happiness through one's own expenses, acquisition of wealth, and pain in gains; || 74 || happiness, profit, and gain through expenditure: these are the certain results in the **South**. || 75 || Fear, acquisition of beloved things, auspiciousness, wealth, and intellectual prosperity; gain through distress, success in purpose, wealth, profit, and death: these are the results in the **West**. || 76 || Wealth, friends, gain of money, happiness, profit, agony, and pleasure; suffering, possession of goods, emptiness, and hardship: these are the strengths of the **North**. <margin>127</margin> <margin>128</margin> || 76 ||

### The Results of the Sarvodaya Yoga
<note>Sarvodaya Yoga is a "universal rising" combination used to predict the success of an undertaking.</note>

Add the values of the lunar day <term>Tithi: the lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>, the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra: the star constellation the moon resides in</term>, and the day of the week. Multiply this sum by seven (7), eight (8), and three (3), then divide. If there is a zero <note>a remainder of zero</note> at the beginning, there will be great pain; if a zero is in the middle, there will be fear. || 77 || If there is a zero at the end, there will be disease of the body; if there are three zeros, death is certain. Victory, arrival of wealth, and happiness occur if there are remainders in these three positions. This should be considered before starting a journey, using the lunar days starting from the bright fortnight. || 78 ||

### The Adala and Bhra Yogas to be Avoided
<note>Adala and Bhra are specific inauspicious astrological configurations for travel.</note>

Count from the Sun's Nakshatra to the Moon's Nakshatra (including Abhijit). Divide by seven (7). If the remainder is two (2) or seven (7), it is <term>Adala</term>. If the remainder is three (3) or six (6), it is <term>Bhra</term>. Travel is not recommended during these times. || 79 ||

### The Commendable Haiva Application
<note>Haiva is a specific calculation used to determine a favorable time for arrival or return.</note>

Add the Moon's Nakshatra to the Sun's Nakshatra, then add the lunar day and the day of the week. Divide the total by nine (9). If the remainder is seven (7), it is called <term>Haiva</term>, which is auspicious for arrival. || 80 ||

### The Auspicious Yoga Called Ghamada

<margin>128</margin> Count from the Sun's Nakshatra to the Moon's Nakshatra, multiply by three (3), and add the lunar day (15). Divide by seven (7). This results in <term>Ghamada</term>, which is specifically mentioned as being favorable for travel. <margin>127</margin> || 81 ||

### The Damaging Moon (Ghatachandra)
<note>Ghatachandra refers to the Moon's position in a specific sign that proves harmful to an individual based on their birth sign.</note>

Now, the Damaging Moon: For one born in Aries, the Moon in the same sign is damaging. For Taurus, the Moon in the seventh or ninth positions is harmful. || 82 ||

### Now, the Detrimental Constellations

### Now, the Detrimental Days of the Week

### Now, the Detrimental Lunar Days

# Muhurta Martanda
<page-num>139</page-num>

The wise should avoid the specific time-measures <term>Nadi (a unit of time equal to 24 minutes)</term> on the sixth lunar day <note>original: "shat-ghata-tithau"</note>. Having discarded the initial portion of the time-measure, the remaining measures are not considered blameworthy. || 62 ||

### The Afflicted Quarters of Constellations in Relation to the Moon
Now, the quarters <term>Pada (one-fourth of a star sign)</term> of the constellations <term>Nakshatra</term> that are afflicted <term>Ghata (literally 'blow' or 'strike', referring to inauspicious times)</term> for the Moon must be abandoned:
In Aries, the first quarter of Krittika; in Taurus, the second quarter of Chitra; in Gemini, the third quarter of Shatabhisha; and in Cancer, the third quarter of Magha. || 63 ||
In Leo, the first quarter of Dhanishta; in Virgo, the third quarter of the same <note>Dhanishta</note>; in Libra, the second quarter of Mula; and in Scorpio, the third quarter of the same <note>Mula</note>. || 64 ||
In Sagittarius, the fourth quarter of Purva Bhadrapada; in Capricorn, the fourth quarter of Magha; in Aquarius, the fourth quarter of Mula; and in Pisces, the third quarter of Purva Bhadrapada. || 65 ||
The wise have stated that these specific quarters must be avoided during the performance of rituals when these "strikes" are active. || 66 ||

### The Afflicted Sun Positions
Now, the Sun and other planets as "strikers" <term>Ghata Grahas</term>. First, the Sun's strike positions:
Starting from Aries, the Sun is considered afflicted in the following numerical positions: 4, 8, 12, 9, 1, 6, 10, 7, 11, 2, and 3. <note>These numbers correspond to the signs of the zodiac relative to the Sun's position</note> || 67 ||

### The Destructive Positions of Jupiter

### The Destructive Moon for Women

### The Abode of the Moon and its Results
When the Moon resides in Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius, it is in the East. The Moon is always in the South when in Virgo, Taurus, or Capricorn. || 105 ||
From Libra, Gemini, and Aquarius, the Maker of Night <note>the Moon</note> is in the West. In Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces, it has gone to the North. || 106 ||
If the Moon is in front of the traveler, there will be gain of wealth; if to the right, happiness and prosperity. If it is behind <margin>131</margin> the traveler, it brings death; if to the left side, it causes the destruction of riches. || 107 ||
Thus the abode of the Moon is declared according to the directions of the zodiac signs. Lo-

What indeed is the root cause for avoiding certain actions? On the seventh and the full moon day, she is in the North-West corner. || 114 || The Yogini is in the Northern part on the second and tenth days. When the Yogini is in front of you, she brings death in gambling, travel, and war. She is inauspicious when on the left side. When she is behind or to the right, she grants victory. || 116 ||

### The Abode of Kaala (Time)
On the day of the Sun (Sunday), Time resides in the North; on the day of the Moon (Monday), it is in the North-West. || 119 || On the day of Mars (Tuesday), it is in the West; on the day of Mercury (Wednesday), it is in the South-West. || 120 || On the day of Jupiter (Thursday), it is in the direction of Yama (South); on the day of Venus (Friday), it is in the direction of Fire (South-East). On the day of Saturn (Saturday), the wise say Time resides in the East. || 120 || Facing Time leads to bondage <note>literally "the noose"</note>, while in the night, the opposite is true for the source. One should avoid both when they are directly in front; however, when they are to the left or right, they are auspicious. || 121 ||

### The Twofold Rahu of the Half-Watch
Now, Rahu <note>The shadow planet or ascending lunar node</note> is of two types based on the half-watch. During the night, starting from the East and moving through the directions every sixty minutes <note>original: "shasthi shasthi," referring to the division of time</note>, he wanders for half a watch. From the North-East, he moves every two Ghatis <note>A Ghati is 24 minutes; two Ghatis equal 48 minutes</note>. || 122 || This is now clarified.

### Rahu in the Half-Watches of the Day
Based on the measurement of half-watches from sunrise, he moves in sequence through the directions of Indra (East), the Wind (North-West), Yama (South), Shiva (North-East), Water (West), Fire (South-East), the Moon (North), and the Demon (South-West). || 123 ||

### Rahu in the Half-Watches of the Night
At night, his movement is said to occur in the directions of Varuna (West), Fire (South-East), the Moon (North), the Demon (South-West), Indra (East), the Wind (North-West), Yama (South), and Shiva (North-East), measured by the duration of half a watch. || 124 ||

### Rahu in the Muhurtas
In the directions of the North-East, Yama (South), the Wind (North-West), Indra (East), the Demon (South-West), the Moon (North), Fire (South-East), and Water (West)—
<margin>133 M.</margin>
<margin>133</margin>
—the planet Rahu wanders for a duration of two Nadis <note>synonymous with Ghati, 48 minutes</note> throughout the day and night. || 125 || Here, a Muhurta <note>a unit of time</note> should be known as the sixteenth part of the day or night. || 126 ||

### The Results of Rahu
Rahu is of two types: when positioned at the back, he grants auspicious results, and likewise when on the right. When on the left—

<keywords>Jyotisha, Rahu, Kaala, Yogini, Muhurta, Ghati, cardinal directions, Vedic astrology, auspicious timing, Yama</keywords>

If the Yogini is positioned behind the traveler, victory in battle is certain. || 127 || One who desires success in war or auspicious undertakings should avoid the Yogini when she is positioned to the left or directly in front. || 128 ||

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati Page 134</margin>

### Concerning the Iron Bolt (Parigha Danda)
Counting from the lunar mansion of Krittika <note>The Pleiades</note>, assign seven mansions to each of the four directions starting from the East. One must never cross the "Bolt" <note>Parigha: a symbolic barrier in the sky</note> when it resides in the Northwest or Southeast directions. || 129 ||

### Concerning the Movement of the Bolt in Intermediate Directions
Starting from the mansions assigned to the East, one should calculate the directions clockwise from the Southeast. By following this method in the intermediate directions, a King shall always be victorious. || 130 ||

### Concerning the Necessary Crossing of the Bolt
If a task is absolutely mandatory, a King may go forth even after crossing the Bolt, provided that the "Root" <note>The starting point of the obstacle</note> is avoided and the specific direction and the rising sign <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> are pure and auspicious. || 131 ||

### Concerning the Best Times for Travel in All Directions
When the lunar mansions of Pushya, Hasta, Anuradha, and others are present, travel in any direction is considered auspicious and free from doubt. || 132 ||

### Concerning the Avoidance of Retrograde Planets
If a retrograde planet <term>Vakra: a planet appearing to move backward</term> is in a cardinal house, or if the rising sign falls within the divisional chart of a retrograde planet, or if it is the weekday of a retrograde planet, the traveler will meet with destruction. || 133 ||

### Concerning Favorable Alignments
During the day, when the Sun is in its Northern Transit <note>Saumyayana: the period between the winter and summer solstices</note>, one should travel toward the North or East. During the night, if the Moon is in its Northern Transit, travel is advised in those same directions. || 134 ||

When the Sun is in its Southern Transit <note>Yamyayana: the period between the summer and winter solstices</note>, one should travel toward the West or South during the day. If the Moon is in its Southern Transit at night, travel is likewise favored in those two directions. || 135 ||

<margin>139</margin>

If both the Sun and Moon are in their Northern Transit, one should travel North or East. If both are in their Southern Transit, one should travel West or South, whether by day or night. || 136 || To act otherwise results in the ruin or even the death of men. <margin>134</margin>

It emphasizes avoiding retrograde planets and choosing directions based on the solar and lunar transits.</summary>

In such cases, there is no fault in travel even if Venus is facing the traveler or to their right. || 140 || Furthermore, for those born into the lineage <note>term: "gotra"</note> of Bhrigu and others, the fault arising from an opposing Venus does not apply. || 141 || **Now, the Fault of Venus Setting and Other Conditions during Travel** || If one sets out when planets are in the sign of Capricorn, in their debilitation, defeated in planetary war, or when Venus is combust <note>term: "asta" (hidden by the Sun's rays)</note>, even a powerful person will fall under the control of their enemies. || 142 || **Now, Mercury Facing the Traveler is also to be Avoided** || One should travel when Mercury is favorable; even if other conditions are good, if Mercury is facing the traveler, all other auspicious planets will cause distress. || 143 || **Now, the Absence of Faults like Opposing Venus in Ordinary Travel** || The faults of an opposing Venus and similar conditions apply only to a person's first journey or to a King seeking victory; they never apply to others in ordinary circumstances. || 144 || **Now, No Fault in Necessary Pilgrimages** || During rare astrological alignments like "Ardhoday" or during eclipses, a pilgrim seeking a holy site <note>term: "tīrtha"</note> may travel happily even if Venus is set or in an otherwise unfavorable state. || 145 || **Now, Staying Put if Venus Sets While on the Road** || If Venus sets or becomes "facing" while one is in the middle of a journey, <margin>135</margin> the King should remain in that place for that duration and proceed only when the time becomes auspicious again. || 146 || **Now, regarding Travel—**

# Considerations for the Ascendant of Travel

## First, the Ascendants to be Avoided

A King should never set out when the Ascendant <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of departure</note> is Aquarius or when the Navamsha <note>A ninth-part sub-division of a sign</note> is Aquarius. If one travels during Pisces or a Pisces sub-division, the traveler's path will be crooked and lead to loss of wealth. (147)

<margin>135</margin>
Some authorities state that Pisces, Scorpio, and Cancer are forbidden for travel. This applies whether these signs are in the main Ascendant or in the ninth-part sub-division for Kings seeking victory. (148)

If the Ascendant at the time of departure is the same as the King's birth sign or birth Ascendant, or if it falls in the eighth house <note>The house associated with death and endings</note> or its sub-division, such a journey leads to death. (149)

If the sign of one's enemy, or the sixth house from the birth sign, or the lord of that enemy sign occupies the Ascendant at the time of travel, that journey will result in destruction. (150)

## Now, the Auspicious Ascendants

If the lord of the birth sign, or the lord of the birth Ascendant, or a benefic planet <note>Such as Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury</note> occupies the Ascendant at the time of travel, or if the travel Ascendant is the same as the birth sign, it brings a successful outcome. (151)

Travel is said to achieve its purpose if the Moon is in the *Vargottama* <note>The best division; when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth-part sub-chart</note> position. If the Ascendant or its sub-division is a water sign <note>Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces</note>, travel by boat is highly recommended. (152)

If the sign is occupied by benefics, or is in a *Vesi* position <note>The sign immediately following the Sun</note>, or if that sign is the second or twelfth from one's own sign, it is auspicious when it becomes the travel Ascendant. (153)

<margin>135</margin>
Travel is most successful and grants all desires when the Ascendant is a "Head-first" rising sign <note>Signs like Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, and Gemini, considered more powerful</note>. For Kings, an Ascendant that forms a *Raja Yoga* <note>A "Royal Union" of planets indicating high status and success</note> is praised. (154)

## Now, the Directions of the Signs

The signs starting from Aries correspond to the four directions starting from the East. (155)

<margin>136</margin>
Known in groups of three starting from the East, these are called the "Gateways of the Directions." If the travel Ascendant is in the sign corresponding to the "Gateway" of the intended direction, the journey will bring victory. However, if the direction is contrary, it causes obstacles, pain, and defeat—

### Now, the Yoga and Adhiyoga Combinations

If even one of Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus is located in a Quadrant <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> or a Trine <term>Kona: the 5th or 9th houses</term>, then this combination provides safety for kings during a journey. || 195 || <margin>140</margin>
If two [of these planets] are so placed, it becomes an *Adhiyoga* <note>a "superior" or "extra" combination</note>, ensuring safety and victory. If all three are present, it is a *Yogadhiyoga*, bringing fame, safety, and the arrival of wealth.

|| 196 || **Now, the three types of travel: Limited, Moderate, and Great.**
Travel is called "Limited" <term>Mita: measured or limited</term> when it extends up to twelve <term>Yojana: a traditional unit of distance, roughly 8-9 miles</term>.
Beyond that, it is "Moderate" <term>Sama: even or mid-range</term> up to twenty-five Yojanas. || 197 || Beyond this, the wise declare it to be "Great" <term>Brihat: large or extensive</term>, continuing until the boundary of the region.
In Limited travel, one must consider the faults arising from the <term>Yogini: directional goddesses</term>, the <term>Shula: "spear" or inauspicious directions</term>, and the five limbs of the calendar <term>Panchanga</term>. || 198 || In Moderate travel, along with those mentioned, one should also avoid faults arising from the <term>Lagna: the ascendant or zodiac sign rising on the horizon</term>.
In Great travel, one must avoid all the faults previously described in the context of a "Yoga-Yatra" <note>a specialized astrological journey for success</note>. || 199 ||

**Now, Praise for the Tenth Day of Victory (Vijayadashami).**
The tenth day of the bright fortnight of the month of Ashvina <note>September/October</note> is called "Vijaya," the giver of all success. When combined with the Shravana star <note>the constellation Aquila</note>, it grants absolute victory to a king on his march. || 200 ||

**Now, the Excellence of Mental Enthusiasm and Other Factors.**
With a clear and joyful mind, favorable omens, and auspicious bird-signs <term>Shakuna: divination based on the flight or cries of birds</term>, and having secured the strength of the Ascendant, victory is certain for kings who set out. || 201 || A powerful purity of heart is even more significant than omens or bird-signs. If this clarity exists, one should go; without it, one should never depart. || 202 ||

**Now, the Excellence of Omens and Other Signs in Travel.**
Regarding travel, the sages Jiva, Usha, and Garga praise the observation of omens. One should also value the enthusiasm of the mind, the worship of Vishnu, and the blessings of the twice-born <term>Brahmin: the priestly class</term>. || 203 ||

**Now, Travel Should Not Be Undertaken Before Completing Festivals.**
<margin>|| 4</margin> One should not depart without completing a consecration ceremony, a wedding, a festival, a religious vow <term>Vrata</term>, a child's first haircut <term>Choula</term>, or the period of birth-impurity <term>Sutaka</term>. This also applies to the menstrual period of women. || 204 ||

**Now, the Fault of Untimely Rain.**
In the event of untimely rain, the sighting of lightning, or the sound of thunder, one should not travel for seven nights. The same applies during natural portents <term>Utpata: calamities or unusual natural phenomena</term>.

<margin>Su. Ga. 142</margin> ...by those desiring auspiciousness. || 205 || It is said that untimely rain occurs during the four months starting from the month of Pausha <note>December/January</note>. Great harm arises from heavy rain, while lesser harm comes from light rain. || 206 || No fault arises from rain as long as the ground is not marked by footprints <note>meaning the rain is too light to muddy the earth</note>. If untimely rain occurs, one should abandon necessary tasks. || 207 || The fault lasts for three days in Pausha, two days in Magha <note>January/February</note>, one day in Phalguna <note>February/March</note>, and two *ghatikas* <note>about 48 minutes</note> in Chaitra <note>March/April</note>. This fault does not apply to rain originating from "clouds in the womb" <note>term: *garbha-samudbhava*; refers to the natural formation of rain clouds according to seasonal cycles</note>. || 208 || **Now, the remedy for the fault of untimely rain:** Having fashioned images of the solar and lunar discs out of gold, the lord of the earth <note>the King</note> may then set out if the task is absolutely necessary. || 209 || **Now, the consideration of departure and entry on the same day:** If a King goes from one village to another on the same day, he should not consider the planetary days, constellations, or the "Face of Venus" <note>term: *prati-shukra*; an astrological position of the planet Venus that usually forbids travel in a certain direction</note>. || 210 || When both departure and entry occur on the same day, the auspicious timing for the entry should be considered, not the timing for the departure. || 211 || After an entry, a departure—or an entry after a departure—should never be performed by a man on the ninth lunar day or under "swift" constellations. || 212 || **Now, the procedure for travel:** Having offered oblations into the sacred fire and worshipped the Gods, the Brahmins, and the Lords of the Directions, and having had the prayer for success <note>term: *svastyayana*</note> performed by Brahmins, the King should give gifts. || 213 ||
<margin>142</margin> Meditating with a joyful mind upon the Guardian of the Direction toward which he is traveling—starting with Indra <note>Guardian of the East</note>—he should set out. || 214 || **Now, the gratification of the constellations:** <margin>142</margin> Horse-gram, rice mixed with sesame, black gram, cow's curd, ghee, milk, and the blood of a deer or a ram... || 215 || ...and milk-rice <note>pudding</note>...

...meat, young venison, and licorice; <note>original: "yashtika"</note> Long pepper, <note>original: "priyangu"</note> cakes, fish, and good fruits. || 216 || Tortoise, starling, <note>original: "sarika"</note> monitor lizard, porcupine meat, and sacrificial grains; <note>original: "havishyakam"</note> rice cooked with sesame and pulses, <note>original: "krusaranna"</note> rice with mung beans, and barley flour. || 217 || Fish with rice, variegated rice, and curd-rice—these in their proper order. **Now, the Ritual Diet for Lunar Days** <note>original: "Tithi-dohada"</note>
Starting from the first day of the lunar fortnight: swallow a leaf of the Sun-plant, <note>original: "arka-patra"</note> then rice-water, clarified butter, fermented gruel, <note>original: "aranala"</note> sacrificial grains, gold-infused water, and small cakes. || 219 || Citron fruit, pure water, cow urine, barley-meal, milk pudding, <note>original: "payasam"</note> jaggery, ginger, and rice with mung beans; having eaten these, a man may depart. || 220 ||

**Now, the Ritual Diet for Weekdays** <note>original: "Vara-dohada"</note>
One should eat mango juice, <note>original: "rasalam"</note> milk pudding, fermented gruel, mung beans, milk, and curd. On the final day, sesame seeds mixed with milk; this is the sequence of ritual diets for travel. || 221 ||

**Now, the Ritual Diet for Directions** <note>original: "Dik-dohada"</note>
One should go East after consuming clarified butter; South after eating sesame-rice; the ritual diet for the West is fish, and for the North, milk. || 222 ||

**Now, Consideration of Edible and Inedible Items in the Ritual Diet**
Regarding the substances mentioned for the ritual diet, one must consider what is permissible or forbidden to eat <note>based on the traveler's caste or specific religious vows</note>. If it is edible, one should taste it and depart; if it is forbidden, one should merely look upon it. || 223 ||

**Now, Meditation when the Diet is Unavailable or Untouchable**
If the required ritual food is unavailable, one should meditate upon that food and then depart. || 224 ||

-> Good Breath <-

...in the channels. || 225 || The left channel is the *Ida* <note>the lunar or cooling channel</note>, and the right is the *Pingala* <note>the solar or heating channel</note> belonging to the Sun. The *Sushumna* <note>the central channel</note> is known as the *Shambhavi* <note>associated with Shiva</note>; it is mixed and is the domain of the great masters of Yoga. || 226 ||

### Breath Flow According to the Lunar Day

During the bright fortnight <note>the waxing moon</note>, beginning with the first lunar day, the Moon <note>left nostril</note> should flow first for three lunar days. Then the Sun <note>right nostril</note> should flow for three days. This pattern should be known to repeat again and again. || 227 || In the dark fortnight <note>the waning moon</note>, the Sun flows first, then the Moon, and so on as previously stated. When the breath rises in its own proper channel, it is short; the rising of one's own breath lasts for five <insert>ghatikas</insert> <note>a unit of time, roughly 24 minutes</note>. || 228 || Then, for the lesser duration, there is a transition through the twelve signs of the zodiac. || 229 ||

### Rising of One's Own Breath According to the Day of the Week

The three solar breaths are known to belong to Sunday, Thursday, and Tuesday. Saturday, Friday, Monday, and Wednesday are declared to be the days of the lunar breath. || 230 || On one's own day, the flow begins in its corresponding channel; the Sun's breath rises for five <insert>ghatikas</insert>. One should perform actions dictated by their own breath when it rises on its corresponding day. || 231 || If the Moon's breath flows during the Sun's time, or the Sun's breath flows during the Moon's time, it causes anxiety, inauspiciousness, and loss. When each flows in its own time, everything is auspicious. || 232 ||

### Rising in the Morning with One's Own Breath

One should always rise in the morning with the breath corresponding to the day of the week. During the southern solstice, one should rise with the Moon's breath, and during the northern solstice, with the Sun's breath. || 233 || If one's own breath is not flowing as it should be at that time, then the wise person should make it flow through efforts such as <term>Pranayama</term> <note>breath control techniques</note>. || 234 ||

### Activities for the Lunar Breath

Entering a new home, marriage, starting a journey, wearing new clothes or ornaments, making treaties, and all auspicious works should be performed during the rise of the Moon's breath <note>left nostril</note>. || 235 || Also, acts for nourishment, peace-making, affection, happiness, and taking <term>Rasayana</term> <note>herbal tonics for longevity</note>...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions on Svarodaya (the science of breath), specifically detailing the auspicious activities for the lunar and solar nostrils and the tactical use of breath in conflict.</meta>

<margin><note>original: "Su. Ga. 245"</note> Reference to verse 245</margin>
Practices such as the discipline of Yoga <term>Yoga-abhyasa: the systematic practice of physical and mental exercises</term> and similar rituals are successful when the breath flows through the Moon <note>The left nostril, associated with cooling and receptive energy</note>. One should also ensure the Moon channel is flowing for calming someone, for the removal of grief, or when a person is unconscious or feverish, as well as for the awakening of a virtuous person. || 236-237 ||

### Now, the activities for the Sun Breath

One should engage in battle, business dealings, eating, sexual intercourse, disputes, gambling, bathing, breaking things, or facing fears while the Sun breath <note>The right nostril, associated with heat, digestion, and aggressive action</note> is active. || 238 ||

The killing of enemies, paralyzing them, enchanting, or driving them away, and gaining control over others—all such fierce and intense actions achieve success through the Sun channel. || 239 ||

A man should always make an effort to sleep on his side to activate the Sun channel after eating to aid weak digestion <term>Mandagni: slow or weak digestive fire</term>, and also for the purpose of attracting women. || 240 ||

During embracing or intimate encounters, a man desiring to win the affection of a woman while lying down should inhale through his Sun channel while she is in her Moon breath. || 241 ||

### Now, the activities for the Flowing Channel

When a questioner asks a practitioner, if the breath is in the "Full" <note>active</note> nostril, the work will be successful. If it is in the "Empty" <note>inactive</note> nostril, or during the transition between nostrils, it will not be successful. || 242 ||

A wise person should set out on a journey by first placing the foot corresponding to the "Full" nostril forward for three steps. Entering a place while that same nostril is active grants all success in travel. || 243 ||

In long-distance warfare, the Moon breath is auspicious; in close-quarters combat, the Sun breath is best. In sinful or aggressive acts, the one with the Moon breath wins, while the defender wins if their Sun breath is active. || 244 ||

<margin>245</margin>
By keeping the enemy on the side of the "Empty" <note>inactive</note> nostril and staying oneself on the side of the "Flowing" <note>active</note> nostril, one conquers. If the enemy is positioned in the "Direction of Death" <note>the empty side</note>, they are slain; of this there is no doubt. || 245 ||

**Now, the consideration of the Elements:**
The flow of the Earth element is in the middle; the Water element flows downward; the Fire element always flows upward; the Air element flows obliquely (sideways); and the Ether element flows in the transition between two [other elements]. || 247 ||

<margin>247</margin> **Now, the results of the Elements in the breath:**
Victory is achieved during the Fire element; success occurs during the Water element; in the Earth element, there is battle with wounds; in the Air element, there is defeat; and in the Ether element, there is death. || 248 ||

**Now, the procedure for departure at the time of a journey:**
With a healthy body, having spoken [auspiciously] or having heard pleasant words, the King, after consuming sacrificial food <note>Havisya: pure food suitable for offerings, such as rice or ghee.</note> and being blessed by the Brahmins, should set out. || 249 ||

As long as the auspicious signs are present, the Lord (King) shall go forth to be victorious. Having ensured the direction of travel is favorable, he should place the foot corresponding to the active nostril <note>The "Nadi" or channel through which the breath is currently flowing.</note> forward first. || 250 ||

After walking twenty-two steps in this manner, the Lord of the Earth (the King) should give a vessel of sesame and gold to the best of the astrologers and Brahmins. || 251 ||

Having given a vessel of clarified butter (ghee) along with wealth, he should mount his vehicle. Some say he should proceed after first placing his right foot forward. || 252 ||

This is prescribed if the breath is in accordance with the timing of the elements mentioned; if it is not, [one must wait].

**Now, the vehicles for travel in each direction:**
One should mount an elephant for the East, a chariot for the South, an excellent horse for the West, and a palanquin <note>Shivika: a covered litter or sedan chair carried by poles.</note> for the North; by proceeding thus, one conquers. || 253 ||

<margin>255</margin> **Now, meditation in the absence of the prescribed vehicle for a direction:**
If the vehicle prescribed for a specific direction is unavailable, one should meditate upon that vehicle and then proceed. || 254 ||

If there is a delay in the King's own departure for the sake of the work, then he himself... <note>The text breaks off here and continues on the next page.</note>

One should send the parasol, the fly-whisk <note>symbols of royal authority</note>, weapons, and other items toward the direction of the intended destination. (260) **Now, the rules for the pace of travel:** On the first day, the King should travel one *krosha* <note>approximately 2 miles</note>; on the second day, one *yojana* <note>approximately 8 miles</note>; on the third day, two *kroshas*; and after that, as he pleases. At the boundary, the limit is seven nights; for an ordinary person, it is five days. (262) **Now, the departure according to the directions:** Seven days are auspicious for staying after departure in the East; five in the South; three in the West; and in the North, two days. ...Lord || 286 || A barren woman, a person in chains, a thief, a eunuch, or seeing someone fleeing; riding upon a donkey, a camel, or a buffalo; or the hearing of wicked speech || 287 || <margin>150</margin> A black snake, a frog, a lizard, or a village pig; those who have fallen from their caste, the mutilated, the hunchbacked, the blind, the deaf, or the mentally disabled || 288 || One wearing wet clothes, a widow, a goldsmith, or a menstruating woman; shoes smeared with mud, feces, fat, or grass || 289 || Likewise, witnessing a fight involving a menstruating woman, a black cow, a buffalo, or a bull; the burning of one's own house, a battle, a cat, or a quarrel within the family || 290 || The death of a calf, the injury of living beings, or the trembling of the head or ears; a cat blocking the path, a stumble, or an empty water-pot || 291 || These are the bad omens <term>Shakuna (omens)</term> encountered when setting out; they forbid the success of all undertakings || 292 ||

### Now, the Great Omens of Death

A sneeze, or a cat or snake blocking the path; the mention of death; a fight between cats or buffaloes; the shaking of a dog's ears || 293 || And a fire accompanied by excessive smoke—these six are the givers of death || 294 ||

### Now, Bad Omens to be Avoided on the Left

If these bad omens occur on the left side, one should seek a better omen afterward. If they are not seen at all, or if these omens appear on the right, they are to be known as auspicious || 295 ||

### Now, for Some the Mention is Auspicious, for Others the Sight is Inauspicious

<margin>150</margin> For the rabbit, the mongoose, the monitor lizard, the snake, and the pig: the mention of their names is auspicious, but neither their cry nor the sight of them is good || <margin>150</margin> 296 || For the bear and the monkey, both the sight and the cry are auspicious when starting a journey; however, the mention of their names is not desired. The braying of a donkey on the right is also auspicious.

<page-num>251</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Ganapati 151</header>

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati 151</margin> || 297 || ### Now, Auspicious Omens on the Left Side ||
The sow, the cuckoo, the bird <note>likely referring to a specific small bird species</note>, the muskrat, the female owlet <note>original: "pingala"</note>, the *rala* <note>a type of bird</note>, the *potaki* <note>the female Wagtail</note>, and the female jackal—all these are well-known to be auspicious when appearing on the left side. || 298 || **After midday, the blue jay and the mongoose are best when appearing on the left.** <margin>4</margin> || 299 ||

### Now, Auspicious Sounds from the Left ||
The sounds of the donkey, the owl, and the jackal are auspicious when heard from the left or from behind. || 300 ||

### Now, Auspicious Omens on the Right Side ||
The wagtail <note>original: "shrikantha"</note>, the monkey, the bearded vulture, the *ritvakkara* <note>a specific bird of omen</note>, the Indian cuckoo, and the *koruta* <note>a water-fowl</note>; the dog, the bear, and the crow are best on the right, as are those animals with feminine names. || 301 || The cry of a monkey or a deer is auspicious in the evening when on the right, as is the peacock. || 302 ||

### Now, Auspiciousness of Moving to the Right ||
Deer and birds are auspicious when they move from the left to the right <term>Pradakshina: the act of circling to the right, considered sacred</term> during a journey. If an odd number of deer are seen walking, it is highly auspicious for a king. Deer, mongooses, and birds are best when moving from the left to the right. || 303 ||

### Now, Auspiciousness on the Left ||
The jackal and the dog are auspicious when they move from the right side to the left. || 304 ||

### Now, Special Rules for This Section ||
When traveling toward the East, the lizard and the cuckoo are "full" <note>meaning their omen is at full strength</note> when they call. A crow is auspicious on the left when departing, and on the right when arriving; otherwise, the result is different. || 305 || If a blue jay flies across the sky in front of a person, forming a "garland" or archway, it ensures victory; however, if a crow does the same, death is certain. || 306 ||

### Now, Specific Behaviors of Dogs ||
If a dog scratches its head, belly, jaw, nose, heart, throat, <margin>251</margin> shoulder, or back with its right hind leg while one is starting a journey or similar activity, || 307 || it brings profit, safety, and victory.

But showing teeth, sleeping, or acting lazy indicates a loss of intelligence or purpose. || 309 ||

### On the Reversal of Left and Right Omens during Travel

Now, regarding the reversal of left and right omens during entry and other actions: When entering a place, crossing low ground, or when there is fear upon seeing a man; also, when the breath flows through the *Ida* <term>Ida: the left nostril/lunar energy channel</term> or during medical treatments || 310 || and in battle—in these cases, the omens previously described should be understood in reverse. Whether an omen was said to be on the left or the right || 311 || wise men and kings should understand them by their opposites upon entry. The omens described for travel are the same as those for visiting a king. || 312 ||

### On the Wind as an Indicator of Good and Bad Fortune

A gentle, cool breeze blowing from the right or from behind grants success. || 313 || A fierce wind blowing from the front or the left causes failure in journeys and other undertakings. || 314 ||

### On the Consideration of Directions for Omens

When half a *yama* <term>Yama: a three-hour period; half a yama is 90 minutes</term> remains in the night, until the sun's power reaches its peak, the movement of the sun is recorded. || 315 || The direction joined with the sun is called "Blazing" (*Jvalanti*); the one it has just left is "Burnt" (*Dagdha*); and the one it is moving toward is "Smoky" (*Dhumavati*). These three are the "Illuminated" directions and are inauspicious. || 316 || <margin>102</margin> The remaining <margin>102</margin> directions are called "Peaceful" (*Shanta*); these five directions are the givers of auspiciousness. The two middle directions are considered mixed in their effects regarding omens. || 317 || <margin>152</margin>

### On What to Do When a Bad Omen Occurs

If a bad omen occurs while setting out, one should give gold along with clarified butter <note>Ghee</note> as charity; then one may proceed happily. If a bad omen occurs at the very start...

### A Compendium of Flaws to be Avoided During Travel

An ascendant that rises from the back <note>Prishthodaya: signs considered less powerful for new beginnings</note>, or when the planetary divisions are situated in the rear. || 325 ||

When Venus is in the seventh house, or when a retrograde planet is in a cardinal house <note>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>, or on the day of a retrograde planet. These, and the other flaws mentioned previously regarding marriage, are not desired for one starting a journey. || 326 ||

If the vowel is in the "Infant" state, victory comes through striking the enemy. If in the "Youthful" state, victory follows a strike; if "Old," there is defeat; and if in the "Dead" state, death occurs. || 333 || A King, established in a direction empowered by his own vowel, should place his enemy in a different direction; by doing so, he will conquer in both gambling and war. || 334 ||

### Now, the Entry into the House upon Returning from a Journey

The rising sign <term>Lagna</term> should be a "Movable" sign and strong. || 336 || Entry during *Pushya*, *Hasta*, *Dhanishta*, or *Shatabhisha* is considered auspicious by the authorities. || 337 ||

## || Now Begins the Chapter on Architecture (Vastu) ||

First, regarding the auspicious or inauspicious nature of a village for habitation: based on the zodiac sign of one's own name compared to the sign of the village, if the count falls on the 2nd, 9th, 5th, 10th, or 12th positions, it results in well-being; living there is considered excellent. || 1 ||
Disease is indicated if the village sign is in the 8th, 12th, or 4th position from one's name-sign. Enmity arises in the 1st and 7th positions. Loss occurs in the 6th and 3rd positions. || 2 ||

**Now, the Consideration of the Kakini** <note>A mathematical technique used to determine compatibility between a person and a place.</note>
Multiply the value of one's own letter-group <note>varga</note> by two, add the value of the other <note>the village's</note> letter-group, and divide by eight. The remainder is the *Kakini* value. This should be calculated for both parties. || 3 ||
The one whose *Kakini* value is higher becomes the provider of wealth to the other. A man should reside in a city only if his *Kakini* value is superior. || 4 ||

**Now, Consideration of the Direction of Residence in a Village**
The signs of Taurus, Leo, and Capricorn <note>original: "gohṛndhanaka siṃhārvya"</note> belong to the center of the village. Pisces, Scorpio, and Virgo (12, 8, 6) belong to the East. Cancer belongs to the South. || 5 ||
Sagittarius belongs to the West. Aries, Libra, and Aquarius (1, 7, 11) belong to the North. Those seeking happiness, wealth, profit, and children should not reside in directions [incompatible with their signs]. || 6 ||
The letter-groups starting with the 'Garuda' group <note>the vowels</note> are considered powerful...
