### On the Auspicious Income of the House

The eight types of *Aya* <note>mathematical "income" or proportions used to determine the energetic vibration of a building</note> are, in order: the Banner, the Smoke, the Lion, the Dog, the Bull, the Donkey, the Elephant, and the Crow. These should be known by the wise in the rituals of building houses and the like. (22)

Multiply the width of the area by the length and divide the total by eight (8). The remainder indicates the *Aya*, starting with the Banner. The odd-numbered remainders are considered excellent. (23)

Starting from the East, these are the powerful *Ayas* in their respective order. The Banner is auspicious in all directions; the Lion is auspicious in the East, South, and North. (24) The Bull is auspicious in the East, and the Elephant is auspicious in the South and East. In the Banner proportion, the house may face West; in the Lion, it may face North. (25)

<margin>108</margin> In the Elephant proportion, one should face the house South; in the Bull, face it East. Up to a measure of thirty-two cubits <note>hastas</note> plus eleven barley-corns <note>yavas</note>, (26) <margin>158</margin> one must consider these *Aya* calculations; beyond that size, they need not be considered. This applies to the portico, the storeys, or a four-faced temple. (27)

**Now, the method for bringing forth the Income <note>Ayadi: a set of six formulas used to determine the energetic suitability of a structure</note> from the area:**

In sequence, by the numbers 1, 8, 6, 8, 3, 1, 8, 4, 8 <note>These are numerical multipliers used in specific architectural calculations</note>. When the area <term>kshetraphala: the total floor space or plot size</term> is multiplied by these and divided by the numbers 8, 6, 8, 8, 12, 21 <margin>178</margin> respectively || 29 ||

And by the lunar days <note>tithi</note> and constellations <note>nakshatra</note> being 25, 5, 12, 20; the remainders found therein [determine the qualities]. In this order, the Income <note>Aya</note>, the Day <note>Vara</note>, the Constellation <note>Ansha</note>, and the Expenditure <note>Vyaya</note> are established || 30 ||

The sum of the lunar days is 8, and likewise the Lifespan; these are to be known in relation to the planets and other celestial bodies. || 31 ||

**Now, the knowledge of Zodiac Signs from the Constellations:**

The first three constellations starting with Ashwini belong to Aries (1). The three starting with Magha belong to Leo (5). The three starting with Mula belong to Sagittarius (9). In this way, the remaining signs are distributed in pairs. || 32 ||

**Now, regarding Compatibility <term>Ghatita: the structural or rhythmic alignment between the owner and the building</term>:**

In a region, a village, a city, or a house; between a lord and a friend, or a husband and wife: a single pulse <note>nadi</note> is praised as auspicious, while conflicting pulses are forbidden. || 33 ||

The friendship between the lord of the house and the house itself, based on their groupings <note>vargas</note>, should be calculated just as it is for a marriage. If there is an enmity between the lord and the house, that house is said to bring death. || 34 ||

**Now, the calculation of Expenditure:**

Take the number of the house's constellation, divide it by 8 <note>naga</note>; the remainder is the Expenditure <note>Vyaya</note>. If the Income is greater than the Expenditure, it bestows auspiciousness upon the house; otherwise, it does not. || 35 ||

**Now, the calculation of the Portions of Indra, the King, and Yama:**

Add the numerical value of the name-group <note>varga</note> starting with the fixed letter to the area. <margin>178</margin> Divide this by 3. If the remainder is 1, it is Indra; if 2, it is the King; if 0, it is Yama. || 36 || <margin>158</margin>

Indra and the King are auspicious, but Yama <note>the god of death</note> is not desired for houses and other buildings. || 37 ||

[Multiply] the desired income by one...

### On Determining Length and Width from the Area
Divide the calculated area by your desired width to find the length. Conversely, divide the area by your desired length to clearly determine the width of the house. || 40 ||

### On the Timing for Commencing House Construction
Beginning in the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note> brings sorrow; in Vaishakha <note>April-May</note>, it increases grain and wealth. In Jyeshtha <note>May-June</note>, it brings death to the builder; in Ashadha <note>June-July</note>, it causes suffering to livestock. || 41 ||
In Shravana <note>July-August</note>, there is an increase in wealth; in Bhadrapada <note>August-September</note>, there is loss. In Ashvina <note>September-October</note>, there will be conflict; in Kartika <note>October-November</note>, the loss of servants. || 42 ||
Prosperity <note>original: "Lakshmi"</note> comes to men in the months of Margashirsha <note>November-December</note>, Pausha <note>December-January</note>, and Phalguna <note>February-March</note>. In Magha <note>January-February</note>, one should know there is a danger of fire when starting a house; of this, there is no doubt. || 43 ||

Avoiding the fourteenth, sixth, fourth, eighth, and new moon days <note>lunar dates/tithis</note>, as well as the ninth, and avoiding Sundays and Tuesdays, the commencement of a house is ordained. || 44 ||
One should begin a house under the constellations of Pushya, Mrigashira, Anuradha, Dhanishtha, the two latter Utaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, or Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, the three "Hand" stars <note>Hasta, Chitra, Svati</note>, Rohini, or Revati. || 45 ||
A permanent residence should be made when the moon is in Punarvasu or Abhijit. In the cycle of the "House-Beginning Sun," if the sun is in the first three stars <note>at the head</note>, it is said to cause burning. If in the next four stars <note>at the forefeet</note>,
<margin>101</margin> it causes death according to the diagram. || 46 ||
Stability is found in the next four stars <note>at the hind feet</note>; wealth is found in the next three <note>on the back</note>.
Profit is found in the next four <note>in the chest and belly</note>; the next three <note>at the tail</note> bring the death of the master. || 47 ||
In the left belly, the next four stars bring constant happiness. <margin>159</margin>

### On the Face, Tail, and Hips of the Vastu Spirit according to Solar Months

In the construction of temples, for three months starting when the Sun is in Pisces (12), for two months in Leo (5), and for two months in [other signs], the face of the Vastu spirit is determined. In the case of water reservoirs, the face of the Vastu spirit begins from Capricorn (10) and moves counter-clockwise from the Northeast <note>original: "Isha"</note>. || 50 ||

Because of this counter-clockwise rotation, the tail is said to be in the direction already passed. It is taught that the tail is opposite the direction of the Sun’s face; therefore, digging for the house and other works should be done in the direction of the Vastu spirit's back. || 51 ||

### Clarification on Digging in the Home

One should perform the digging in the Southeast <note>original: "Agneyyam"</note> when the Sun is in the three signs starting from Leo (5). Similarly, it is said that digging should be in the Northeast <note>original: "Ishanyam"</note> when the Sun is in the three signs starting from Scorpio (8). || 52 ||

When the Sun is in the three signs starting from Aquarius (11), digging should be in the Northwest <note>original: "Vayu"</note>; and in the three signs starting from Taurus (2), it should be in the Southwest <note>original: "Nairitya"</note>. || 53 ||

### Determining the Door and Foundation based on Lunar Months

According to the bright fortnight <note>original: "site"</note> starting from the month of Bhadrapada, the head of the Vastu spirit moves through the directions every three months. In a house, the door is recommended to be placed in the direction where the head is located. || 54 ||

The head of the Vastu spirit should be known in its specific direction, while the tail is located in the direction facing it. || 55 ||

### Digging and Placing the Foundation Pillar on the Left Side

In the divisions of the site, avoiding the seventeen parts [of the body], the digging and the planting of the foundation pillar <note>term: "Shanku" (a ritual gnomon or pillar used to mark the center or orientation of a site)</term> should be done on the left side of the belly. Specifically, the digging is done eleven parts from the tail, in the middle of the left side. At the start of house construction, having offered worship <note>original: "vasham kum datva"</note> at the auspicious moment, one should fill the foundation. || 56 ||

### Determining the House Door according to Solar Transits

When the Sun enters Aquarius in the month of Phalguna, or during the transits into Cancer and Leo...

In the month of Pausha <note>December/January</note>, when the sun enters the sign of Capricorn, one should build a house facing either East or West. **(57)** When the sun is in Libra or Scorpio during the month of Margashirsha <note>November/December</note>, or in Taurus or Aries during the month of Vaishakha <note>April/May</note>,

This is what has been stated by some others. **(60)** The learned should understand that the months mentioned here are to be calculated starting from the dark fortnight <note>the waning moon phase</note>. **(61)** 

### On the Consideration of Lunar Days for Happiness in the Home
For an east-facing house, the nine lunar days starting from the Full Moon should be avoided. For a north-facing house, the first nine days of the dark fortnight should be avoided, as well as the six days following those. **(62)** For a west-facing house, the nine days starting from the tenth day of the lunar cycle should be abandoned. For a south-facing house, the nine days starting from the ninth day of the bright fortnight <note>the waxing moon phase</note> are to be avoided. **(63)**

### The Sixteen Types of Houses: Dhruva and Others
1. *Dhruva* (Constant), 2. *Dhanya* (Wealth), 3. *Jaya* (Victory), 4. *Nanda* (Joy), 5. *Khara* (Sharp/Hard), 6. *Kanta* (Beloved), 7. *Manorama* (Beautiful), 8. *Sumukha* (Fair-faced), 9. *Durmukha* (Ugly-faced), 10. *Ugra* (Fierce), 11. *Ripuda* (Enemy-giving), 12. *Vittada* (Wealth-giving), 13. *Nasha* (Destruction), 14. *Akrandam* (Wailing), 15. *Vipula* (Abundant), and 16. *Vijaya* (Triumph). These are the sixteen types. **(64)**

### Knowledge of the Dhruva-style Houses
<margin>5</margin>
In the directions starting from the East, the numbers are measured as one <note>Moon</note>, two <note>Eyes</note>, four <note>Oceans</note>, and eight <note>Elephants</note>. <note>These are traditional Sanskrit numerical symbols called 'bhutasankhya'</note>. Where the rooms <term>Shala: the wings or halls of a house</term> are located, those numbers are declared here: **(65)** The sum of the rooms plus one determines the house type, starting from *Dhruva*. **(66)**

<margin>Mu. Ga. 162</margin> **Syllable Counts:** The houses named *Ayasundara* and *Vishva* <note>These are specific architectural classifications</note> consist of 2, 3, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, and 13 syllables. The house named *Mandira* is known by two syllables. The remaining houses—8, 9, 11, 12, 14, 15, and 16—should have three syllables, while the 7th house consists of four syllables. || 67 ||

### Now, the Permutation Table for Understanding the Dhruva and Other House Types
First, place four *Guru* <term>Guru: a "heavy" or long syllable, represented in prosody as a long beat</term> marks. Then, place a *Laghu* <term>Laghu: a "light" or short syllable, represented as a short beat</term> below the first Guru. Moving forward, fill the remaining spaces exactly as they appear in the row above. || 68 || In this way, repeatedly place a Laghu behind and then fill the rest with Gurus. This process should be performed repeatedly until all positions are filled with Laghus. || 69 || Starting from the face of the house and moving clockwise is the area known as the *Alinda* <note>A veranda or lattice-covered gallery</note>. Based on the position of the Laghu, there are sixteen variations for the eastern side. || 70 ||

### Now, an Alternative Method for the Permutation Table
In the first column, write one Guru and one Laghu alternately. In the second column, write them in pairs (two Gurus, then two Laghus). In the third column, write them in groups of four. || 71 || In the fourth column, write them in groups of eight. This permutation table continues until sixteen variations are reached. || 72 ||

### Now, Consideration of the Strength of the Ascendant for Beginning a House
A wise person should begin the construction of a temple or house when the *Lagna* <term>Lagna: the Ascendant or the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is in a dual or fixed sign, with benefic planets situated in the 1st, 9th, or 12th houses, and malefic planets in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. || 73 ||

### Now, Auspicious Planetary Combinations for Beginning a House
If Jupiter is in the 1st house (Ascendant), the Sun is in the 6th, Mercury and Venus are in the 7th, Saturn is in the 4th, and the Moon is in the 3rd, the house shall stand for a hundred years. || 74 || If Venus is in the 1st house, the Sun is in the 3rd, Mars is in the 6th, and Jupiter is in the 5th, the house commenced under this alignment shall stand for two hundred years. || 75 || If the Sun is in the 11th house, Venus is in the 1st, and Mercury is in the 10th...

### Now, the Diagram of the Door (Dwara Chakra)

If five stars <note>original: "bhade-dabhaih"; referring to Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note> <gloss>5</gloss> starting from the Sun's position are situated in the "Head" of the diagram, there will be wealth and prosperity. However, if ten stars are situated in the "Corners," it leads to the abandonment of the house. || 87 || If eight stars <gloss>20</gloss> <note>The gloss '20' likely refers to the cumulative count in the calculation sequence</note> are in the "Door-posts," it brings endurance and happiness in the home. If three stars are in the "Threshold," it results in the death of the master of the house. || 88 || If four stars <gloss>24</gloss> are in the "Center," there will be gain of wealth and happiness. One should first consider this diagram before constructing the door in one's temple or house. || 89 ||

### Now, Determining the Direction of the Door According to Zodiac Signs

For those with the signs of Pisces <gloss>12</gloss>, Scorpio <gloss>8</gloss>, and Cancer <gloss>4</gloss>, an east-facing house is declared. For Virgo, Gemini <gloss>3</gloss>, and Capricorn, a south-facing door is considered auspicious. || 90 || For Libra, Taurus, and Aquarius, a west-facing door is prescribed. For those born under the signs of Sagittarius, Aries, and Leo, the door should face north. || 91 ||

### Now, the Determination of Windows (Gavaksha)

One should place the windows <term>Gavaksha (literally "cow's eye")</term> in the directions other than those previously mentioned as auspicious for the main door. || 92 ||

### Now, Determining the Horizontal Placement of the Door

Divide the length of the wall into nine parts; the door should measure one part. On the eastern side, the wise man should place the door in the fourth or third section from the corner. || 93 || On the southern side, in the sixth or fourth section; on the northern or western sides, in the fourth or fifth section, following the clockwise path <note>original: "savya margena"</note>. || 94 ||

### Now, the Height of the Door

The height of the door should be twice the measurement of one-fourth of the total height of the house. || 95 ||

### Now, the Height of the House

<margin>264</margin> Take the sixteenth part of the width and add four cubits <term>Kara (a unit of measurement, roughly 18 inches)</term>; this is known as the proper height of the house. Anything more or less is considered inauspicious. || 96 || Now, regarding the door—

# Page 165

<margin>Manuscript Page 165</margin> **Considerations on Door Obstructions**
Obstructions to a door occur when it is directly facing a corner, a path, a whirlpool, a pillar, a tree, a temple, or a well. <note>original: "Dvaravedha"; refers to "piercing" or blocking the energy flow of an entrance by placing it directly opposite another object.</note> || 97 ||

**Exceptions to Obstructions**
If the distance between the house and the object is more than twice the height of the house, or if there is a road separating them, then a corner or other such obstruction is no longer considered a defect. || 98 ||

**The Construction of the Sixteen Rooms**
Starting from the East and moving clockwise, the rooms are designated as follows:
1. Bathing room
2. Kitchen <note>original: "Paka"</note>
3. Bedroom
4. Armory <note>original: "Ayudha"; a room for weapons or tools</note>
5. Dining room
6. Granary <note>original: "Anna"; literally 'food' or 'grain' storage</note>
7. Treasury <note>original: "Bhandara"; storage for valuables</note>
8. Prayer room <note>original: "Devatana"; room for the deities</note>
These are the traditional eight rooms. || 99 ||

In the intermediate spaces, one should place the rooms for: waste disposal <note>original: "Purisha"; latrine</note>, study, mourning <note>original: "Sucha"</note>, intimacy, and medicine. These rooms are situated in the middle areas of the entire dwelling. || 100 ||

If the land is very small or resources are limited, a wise person should arrange the bathing and other rooms according to their capacity and personal preference. || 101 ||

The mortar and pestle, the water storage, the weighing scales, and the area for washing the feet are auspicious when placed to the South of the house. || 102 ||

**The Location for Digging a Well**
Starting from the Northeast <note>original: "Ishana"</note> and moving clockwise, the results of a well are:
1. Nourishment and prosperity
2. Increase in wealth
3. Loss of sons
4. Loss of women <note>or female health</note>
5. Death
6. Wealth <note>original: "Sampad"</note>
7. Fear of enemies
8. Happiness || 103 ||
If a well is dug in the very center of the house, it leads to the destruction of wealth. || 104 ||

**The Commencement of Brickwork and Plastering**
<margin>165</margin> One should begin the brickwork <note>original: "Ishtakarambha"</note> when the moon is in the constellations of Uttara, Shravana, Pushya, Jyeshtha, Rohini, or Hasta; on a Sunday, Thursday, or Saturday; and when the Ascendant is a fixed sign. <margin>165</margin> The same applies to the application of lime plaster <note>original: "Sudhalepa"</note> and the initial bricklaying of the house. || 105 ||

**Household Implements and Fire Rituals**

### Auspicious Trees for the House Courtyard
The following trees are beneficial: <term>Punnaga</term> <note>Alexandrian laurel</note>, <term>Champaka</term> <note>Magnolia</note>, <term>Ashoka</term>, <term>Pippali</term> <note>Long pepper</note>, <term>Dadimi</term> <note>Pomegranate</note>, <term>Shami</term>, <term>Bakula</term>, <term>Tilaka</term>, <term>Draksha</term> <note>Grapevine</note>, <term>Pichumanda</term> <note>Neem</note>, and <term>Amarojaya</term>. **109**

These trees, when planted in the house courtyard, bring auspiciousness. **110**

### Placement of Trees According to Direction
One should plant the <term>Nyagrodha</term> <note>Banyan</note> to the East of the house, the <term>Udumbara</term> <note>Cluster fig</note> to the Southeast, the <term>Karkandhu</term> <note>Jujube</note> to the North, and the <term>Kunajaranana</term> <note>Ficus religiosa/Peepal</note> to the West. **111**

### Inauspicious Trees for the House Courtyard
The <term>Matulunga</term> <note>Citron</note>, <term>Snuhi</term> <note>Cactus/Euphorbia</note>, <term>Rambha</term> <note>Plantain/Banana</note>, <term>Haridra</term> <note>Turmeric</note>, and <term>Munidruma</term> <note>Agati tree</note> bring sorrow to the home, whether they grow there naturally or are planted. **112**

### Alternative Opinions on Courtyard Trees
According to some authorities, all trees are inauspicious within the immediate courtyard. Some say that no tree at all should be planted within the courtyard of one's own residence; even a tree from heaven <note>original: "svargadrumo", referring to the mythical Kalpavriksha</note> is not auspicious when placed directly in the courtyard. **113** <margin>166</margin>

### || Now, the Chapter on Entering the Home ||

A leader should perform the entry into a new house during the Northern Transit of the sun <note>Uttarayana: the period between the winter and summer solstices, considered spiritually upward-moving</note>. Having first performed the worship of the Vastu <note>Vastu Purusha: the spirit of the dwelling</note>, the entry should occur in the months previously mentioned for beginning construction. || 1 || The months of Magha, Phalguna, Vaishakha, and Jyeshtha <note>roughly January through June</note> are best for a new home. ...on the branch, there is destruction. One should not enter a new temple or home under these conditions. Such are the results for kings regarding travel or returning home. || 8 ||

### The Results of the Weekdays
Entry on Thursday, Friday, or Wednesday brings happiness and wealth. Entry on a Saturday brings stability, though there may be some slight fear of thieves. || 9 ||

### Times to be Avoided for Entry
The month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, Tuesdays, Sundays, the "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta Tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th of the lunar fortnight</note>, and the New Moon should be avoided for entering a new house. One should also avoid times when the Moon is in an inauspicious position. || 10 ||

### The Strength of the Ascendant
Entry during a "Movable" Ascendant <note>Chara Lagna: Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn</note> or a movable division is not auspicious. However, it may be acceptable if it is joined with a beneficial division or if the planets are in an "Increasing" position <note>Upachaya: the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses</note>. || 11 || Benefic planets should be in the center or trine houses <note>1st, 4th, 7th, 10th or 5th, 9th</note>, while malefic planets should be in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. The 8th and 4th houses must be pure <note>empty of planets</note> for the entry to be successful. || 12 ||

When the astrological compatibility <note>Bhakuta</note> of the house and the owner is auspicious, one should enter the house accompanied by auspicious ceremonies, carrying a pot filled with water. || 13 || When the Sun is situated in the 8th, 5th, 2nd, or 11th houses, the Sun acts as the "protector" of the wife and sons in houses facing East and other directions respectively. || 14 ||

### Auspicious Lunar Days Based on the Door's Direction
For a house with an East-facing door, the "Full" <note>Purna: 5th, 10th, 15th</note> days are best. For a South-facing door, the "Joyful" <note>Nanda: 1st, 6th, 11th</note> days are auspicious. For a West-facing door, the "Prosperous" <note>Bhadra: 2nd, 7th, 12th</note> days are good, and for a North-facing door, the "Victorious" <note>Jaya: 3rd, 8th, 13th</note> days are recommended. || 15 ||

### Special Rules for Entry
Work on the house is best done during the day, but the first entry may sometimes occur at night. Travel is permitted when the Moon and Sun are strong, but one should not enter on the 12th lunar day or on a Friday night. || 16 ||

### The Pot Diagram for House Entry
In the diagram calculated from the Sun's lunar mansion, an entry at the "Fire" point leads to burning...

In the breath <note>referring to the 'Prana' or vital breath of the building</note>: If the calculation results in 5, there is 'shining' in the East; if 4, there is profit. In the West, if the count is 23, there is wealth; if 17, there is strife <note>original: "Kali," referring to the age of discord</note>. If the count is 21 in the center, there is destruction. In the lower part, at the "throat" of the diagram, if the count is 27, it is auspicious. || 28 || Now, he clarifies the same: From the position of the Sun, the 8th, 5th, and 22nd constellations <note>term: Nakshatras (lunar mansions used in Vedic astrology)</note>, and the six following them, are the most excellent in the "Pot Diagram" <note>Kumbha Chakra</note> for men entering a house. || 29 ||

### The Method of Entry

Now, the method of entry: At the stated auspicious time, the King <note>or householder</note>, accompanied by canopies and ceremonial arches, and amidst the auspicious sounds of Vedic chanting, should enter the beautiful home. || 30 || He should then honor the architect <note>term: Shilpin (the master builder/artisan)</note>, the astrologer, the ritual specialist, and the priest, treating them like gods with gifts of gold, gems, and fine garments. || 31 ||

### The Two Types of Entry

Now, the two types of entry: The sages have declared the "First Entry" <note>Apurva</note> to be for a brand-new house. The "Return Entry" <note>Sapurva</note> occurs after a journey, and the "Re-entry" <note>Dvandva</note> occurs after renovations. || 32 || The First Entry should be performed by kings under the constellations of Purva-Phalguni, Purva-Ashadha, Purva-Bhadrapada, Ashvini, Mula, and Shravana, while the moon is in a "hand" <note>Hasta</note> position. || 33 || The Return Entry is recommended by the wise under Uttara-Phalguni, Chitra, Anuradha, Revati, Mrigashira, and Rohini. These are the three types of entry. || 34 ||

One should never enter a house without performing the Vastu worship <note>term: Vastu-pujana (sanctifying the ground and structure)</note>. A wise man should never enter a new house that is uncovered <note>without a roof</note>, lacks doors, or has not had the Vastu rituals performed. || 35 ||

### The Worship of the Vastu

Now, the Vastu worship: Under the three "Ashva" constellations, Mrigashira, Mula, Anuradha, Revati, the three "Hasta" constellations, Punarvasu, the three "Uttara" constellations, Pushya, Rohini, and Ashvini... || 36 || ...one should worship the Vastu.

**Now begins the chapter on the Consecration of Temples and Related Structures.** || The consecration of temples and divine images should be performed during the Northern Transit of the Sun <note>Uttarayana; the period between the winter and summer solstices</note>. Likewise, the dedication of water reservoirs and gardens is considered auspicious, except during the month of Chaitra <note>March/April</note>. || 1 || Some authorities state that the month of Chaitra is only mediocre for consecration. During the bright fortnight, under the stars Anuradha, Revati, Punarvasu, Rohini, or Mrigashira; || 2 || or under Shravana, the three "Uttara" stars, the three "Hand" stars, or Pushya, when the Moon is auspicious—on such days, constellations, and lunar dates, the installation of divine images is prescribed. || 3 || For the principal deities such as Vishnu and others, the installation should occur during an auspicious worldly ascendant <note>Lagna</note>. || 4 || **Now, specific constellations for specific deities:** || For Vishnu, the previously mentioned stars are good; for the Sun, Anuradha and Chitra are cited. For others, Rohini, Shravana, Jyeshtha, Pushya, and Abhijit are recommended. || 5 || For Brahma and Indra, <margin>10</margin> the installation is performed well by the Aryas under those same stars. For the Sun, Kubera <note>God of Wealth</note>, and Skanda <note>God of War</note>, the stars Hasta and Anuradha are used. || 6 || Under Mula, the installation of Durga and other goddesses is done; Shravana is for the Buddha <note>original: "Sugata"</note>. Under Revati, one should install Dharma, Heramba <note>Ganesha</note>, the Serpent deities, and the chief of the Rakshasas. || 7 || For Yakshas, spirits, and demons...

The installation of the Earth and the Goddess of Speech <note>Saraswati</note> is prescribed [under the Rohini nakshatra]. The installation of the houses of Vyasa, Agastya, and Valmiki should take place under the Pushya star. || 8 ||
Wherever the Seven Sages <note>The Big Dipper constellation</note> travel, in those stations, and for all of them, installation is declared in Rohini and the three later stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>. For the Lords of the Directions, the installation is prescribed in Dhanishtha. || 9 ||

### On Specific Ascendants for Specific Deities
The Sun should be installed when the ascendant is Leo; Shiva should be installed in a dual-natured sign <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces</note>; Vishnu in a feminine sign; Brahma in Aquarius; the minor deities in a movable sign; and all Goddesses in a fixed sign. || 10 ||

### On the Results of the Days of the Week
Consecration performed on the days starting from Sunday [through Saturday] results in: protection, fire <note>or destruction</note>, the granting of boons, joy, and a duration lasting an eon. || 11 ||

### On Specific Months for Specific Deities
Even during the Southern Transit of the Sun <note>Dakshinayana, usually considered less auspicious</note>, a wise man may install Varaha, the Mothers, Bhairava, Vamana, the Slayer of the Buffalo Demon <note>Durga</note>, and Narasimha. || 12 || One should install the Linga <note>Shiva</note> in the month of Shravana, the Mother of the World in Ashvina, Vishnu in Margashirsha, and some say all deities may be installed in Pausha. || 13 ||

### On the Strength of the Ascendant in Consecration
With the benefic planets situated in the centers <note>houses 1, 4, 7, 10</note> or trines <note>5, 9</note> and the second and eleventh houses, and the malefic planets along with the Moon in the eleventh, sixth, or third houses, the deities installed in a palace <note>temple</note> grant prosperity. || 14 ||

1. A twitch in the **head** signifies the gain of land; in the **forehead**, it signifies the gain of a position or status.
2. In the **space between the eyebrows**, it signifies obtaining a beloved; in **both eyebrows**, it brings great happiness.
3. In the **ears**, it means hearing good news; in the **eyes**, it means seeing something pleasant.
4. In the **pupils of the eyes**, it signifies fortune; in the **lower eyelid**, it signifies victory.
5. In the **cheek** area, it brings happiness to a woman; in the **nose**, it brings pleasures derived from fragrances.
6. In the **upper lip**, it signifies a verbal debate; in the **lower lip**, it signifies a kiss for a woman.
7. In the **chin** area, fear is to be known; in the **mouth**, it signifies a sweet meal.
8. In the <term>throat</term> <note>Sanskrit: Kantha; the front of the neck</note> area, there is the acquisition of land; in the **back of the neck**, there is fear arising from enemies.
9. On the **back**, defeat is known; on the **shoulder**, a meeting with a friend.
10. In the **heart** area, there is the attainment of a beloved; in the **middle of the arms**, the arrival of wealth.
11. In the **hand**, the acquisition of goods; in the **chest**, knowledge and certain victory.
12. In the **waist**, joy and strength; in the **sides**, unsurpassed affection.
13. In the **navel**, a movement from one's place; in the **intestines**, an increase in the treasury.
14. In the **stomach**, the acquisition of a treasury for a woman; in the **hips**, union with a husband.
15. In the **buttocks or anus**, the acquisition of a vehicle; in the **penis**, union with a woman.
16. In the **testicles**, the gain of a son; in the **bladder** area, prosperity.
17. In the **thighs**, the attainment of a good residence; in the **knees**, a treaty with an enemy.
18. In the **shanks**, there is sometimes a loss; on the **top of the feet**, the attainment of a position.
19. Under the **feet**, it is known to grant profit. This is the fruit of <term>limb twitching</term> <note>Sanskrit: Anga-sphurti; involuntary pulsations or spasms used in divination</note>.
20. For men, these results apply to the left side; the wise should know the opposite for women.
21. For women, the twitching of the limbs occurs on the right side in the same manner.

### Now, the Results of a Lizard Falling

<margin>183</margin>
Regarding this, the auspicious and inauspicious lunar days, days of the week, and lunar mansions are as follows: The 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 10th, 13th, 12th, and 11th <note>original: "bhunetra, agni, shara, anga, dasha, vishve, aditya, sammitah" using numerical code words</note> are considered auspicious lunar days <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> for a lizard to fall; others are considered inauspicious. || 14 || Gentle weekdays <note>Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday</note> are declared auspicious; the harsh days <note>Sunday, Tuesday, Saturday</note> grant wicked results. The lunar mansions <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 sectors of the sky along the moon's path</term> of Pushya, Ashwini, Rohini, the two <note>likely referring to the Phalgunis</note>, Punarvasu, the three beginning with Arudra, || 15 || Dhanishta, Revati, Anuradha, and Shatabhisha are auspicious. Anything other than these is forbidden <margin>233</margin> when a lizard falls upon men. || 16 ||

For men, a fall on any part of the right side is auspicious; for women, it is the same for the left side. On the navel, the heart, the stomach, and the head (excluding the jaw), || 17 || the falling of a lizard is known to be auspicious; elsewhere, it is not good. Whatever result occurs from a lizard falling, the same applies to a chameleon <term>Sharata: a garden lizard or chameleon</term> climbing up. || 18 || When a chameleon moves away or a lizard climbs up, the result is the same. One must bathe if touched by a lizard; for a chameleon, the same applies but with more gravity. || 19 ||

If this occurs during one's birth star, a "death-yoga" <note>an inauspicious astrological alignment</note>, a "burned" day, or during the corruption of the <term>Vishti: an inauspicious period or 'Karana'</term>, or if the Moon is in the eighth house or joined with malefic planets, a great misfortune arises. || 20 || In that case, one should perform a <term>Shanti: a propitiatory or pacification rite</term> consisting of prayers, fire offerings, and the "Conquering Death" <note>original: "Mrityunjaya"</note> hymn to Shiva. One should bathe with the five products of the cow <term>Panchagavya: a mixture of milk, curd, ghee, urine, and dung used for purification</term> and perform the ritual of gazing at one's reflection in clarified butter. || 21 || Now, for the well-being—

# Knowledge of Fatal Omens
<note>original: "Arishta-jnanam"; refers to the diagnostic signs that predict the timing of death.</note>

<margin>Su. Ga. 274</margin>If the Sun-breath <note>The flow of breath through the right nostril, associated with heat and solar energy.</note> remains dominant and powerful for sixteen days continuously, then death is imminent. If it lasts for a shorter period than that, death is said to occur within a year. **22** Likewise, if the Moon-breath <note>The flow of breath through the left nostril, associated with coolness and lunar energy.</note> flows constantly, it brings happiness; however, if both breaths cease their natural rhythm, death occurs in an instant. If the Moon-breath begins to act like the Sun, and the Sun-breath acts like the Moon constantly— **23** —for two or three days, then that person will die within six months. If one sees the bright sun as lacking in luster, they will die within ten days. **24** One who sees dreams while wide awake will not live for more than a year. He who cannot hear the internal ringing in his ears, or cannot see the tip of his nose, his tongue, or his eyebrows— **25** —or cannot see his left <term>medhra</term> <note>The male organ; used here as a physical landmark for self-examination.</note>, will not live beyond six months. If one cannot see the subtle lines on the wrist or the forehead— **26** —or if one emits a foul odor without cause, or becomes suddenly lusterless or excessively radiant, or if a thin person suddenly becomes stout, or if one's hair and nails turn the color of heated blood— **27** —or if one's eyes become sunken or agitated in an instant, they will not live for more than a month. If one cannot see the Pole Star <note>original: "Dhruva"</note>, the steps of Vishnu <note>A celestial formation or constellation.</note>, the star Arundhati, or the Circle of Mothers <note>A group of stars or a mystical vision.</note>— **28** —or the orb of the earth, the markings on the moon, then they shall certainly not live. If one's phlegm sinks in water, or if one's footprint appears broken in mud or dust— **29** —or if, after bathing, the chest dries before the rest of the body, or if a line of smoke appears to rise from the head, or if one finds no satisfaction even after a good meal, or if a stout body suddenly wastes away— **30** —these are reversals of one's natural state. If one cannot see three fingers held before the eyes, death is near. **31**

### Inauspicious Signs in Battle
Now, the unlucky omens for combat: dryness of the tongue, trembling of the body, and the loss of color in the <term>tambula</term> <note>The red juice produced by chewing betel leaf and areca nut.</note> within the mouth. If one's voice becomes choked or stammering—

## The Signs of Victory in Battle
One whose arms are flourishing <note>meaning robust or full of vigor</note>, whose hair stands on end with excitement, whose gaze is steady, who is endowed with courage and full of enthusiasm—such a warrior shall be victorious in the coming conflict. || 33 ||

## The Method of Observing the Shadow-Man and Its Results
In the morning, with the sun at one's back, a man should bathe and face toward the West. If he sees his shadow-form <term>Chhaya Purusha: a mystical practice of gazing at one's own shadow to predict the future</term> appearing complete in all its limbs and white in front of him, || 34 || then for a full year he shall obtain happiness, security, and victory. If that shadow is seen without a head, he shall live for only one month. || 35 || If it is seen without ears, he has one year; if without shoulders, seven months; if with a hole in the heart, seventeen months; and if without hands, || 36 || he has but two months of life. If the shadow appears without a torso, he has two months; if without a face, he lives for one month. If the shadow is seen without a body at all, death is immediate—of this there is no doubt. || 37 ||

## Results According to Other Variations
If the shadow appears distorted, there will be the loss of a friend and great sorrow; if it appears during a marriage, it signifies the loss of kinsmen. If the shadow appears shattered, it means the destruction of the self; in all such states, it signifies the wasting away of one's time. || 38 || A shadow appearing like a dog signifies famine in the land; if it appears turned away, it brings camphor-like <note>perhaps meaning fleeting or cold</note> results. If the shadow appears with a hole, or smoky, or rough, or broken, or severed, it indicates a fatal blow. || 39 || If it appears yellow or red, 
<margin>275</margin> it signifies disease and loss; if it appears dark, death occurs. If seen in the second watch of the night, the result comes in two months; if in the third, death comes in three. || 40 ||

<margin>18</margin> ## The Observation of Dreams
A dream seen during the first watch of the night will bear fruit within a year. || 41 || A dream seen at dawn yields immediate results, provided the man does not fall back asleep. Dreams born of disease or anxiety are useless, as are those seen after a very long period of digestion. || 42 || Now...

### Now, Inauspicious Dreams

One who, in a dream, is smeared with oil, is naked, or is riding upon a buffalo, a donkey, a camel, or a black bull, and travels toward the south—such a person will not live long. || 61 || If a person dreams of entering a kitchen, a forest filled with red flowers, or a birthing room while being physically deformed, they will be separated from their life-breath <note>i.e., they will die</note>. || 62 || If lac, saffron, or mustard seeds fall from a tree onto one's house, there is a danger of the house burning or a fear of thieves. || 63 || The growth of grass, flowers, or trees on any part of the body other than the navel; or having sexual union with donkeys, camels, monkeys, or snakes; or the consumption of oil... || 64 || ...with muddy water.

In a dream, activities such as anointing oneself with oil, singing, playing, or trembling; laughing, being scolded, or falling into a downward-flowing stream. <note>Verse 67</note>
The falling of the sun, moon, planets, or stars; or seeing oneself on a funeral pyre. The snapping of a rope at the head, or the wearing of a bronze-colored mask. <note>Verse 68</note>
Entering the mother's womb—this is an evil deed <insert>in a dream</insert> and indicates a fatal sign. Being bound by a noose of Oleander or Ashoka vines. <note>Verse 69</note>
Being embraced by a woman dressed in black garments is a cause of death. One who climbs flowering trees and, while contemplating their own body, <note>Verse 70</note>
decorates it with red flowers—that person is also released from their life breaths. A person who is covered in ghee or red cloth meets with death. <note>Verse 71</note>
When one sees themselves enveloped in smoke; or sees ashes, ghee, iron, or lac—they are surely released from life. <note>Verse 72</note>
Jackals, dogs, cats, iguanas, mongooses, and snakes; flies, scorpions, and gadflies—seeing these <margin>18</margin>
<margin>18</margin> in a dream is not auspicious. <note>Verse 73</note>
The theft of gems, wealth, weapons, shoes, beds, women's ornaments, clothes, or other dear objects signifies the loss of wealth. <note>Verse 74</note>
Sorrow during marriage festivals; the falling out of teeth or hair; or the <margin>278</margin>
severing of hands or other limbs—these signify one's own destruction. <note>Verse 75</note>
The shaving of the beard or hair; the falling out or pain of the eyes; <insert>falling into</insert> a well, a pit, or a cave. <margin>18</margin>

### The Determination of Dream Results

Dreams that are seen regarding oneself give their results to that person alone. Those seen regarding another give results to that person, and only slightly to oneself. || 86 ||

For as many days as the portent appears in the vault of heaven, 128, for that many months it bears fruit; if it stays for months, one should predict the effects for years. 129.

If [the omen] occurs in the solar or lunar disk, there will be disease, fear, and the loss of vital fluids. If it occurs when no houses are visible <note>likely referring to a clear sky or lack of lunar mansions</note>, the result is unfavorable; however, if it occurs during the life-breath of the limbs <note>an astrological calculation of vitality</note>, it is auspicious. || 132 || In the region where the earth splits, there will be the destruction of the king and fear of enemies. In whatever year a fortnight consists of only thirteen days <note>a rare and inauspicious lunar calendar occurrence</note> || 133 || then there will be the destruction of the subjects, famine, and the ruin of the kings. || 134 ||

**Now, the nature of thunderclaps and their results.**
When a thunderclap <term>nirghāta - a sudden atmospheric crash or earthquake-like sound</term> produced by violent winds falls upon the earth, or if birds cry out while facing the sun, it is not favorable; it signifies the death of the king at sunrise. || 135 || The sound of the thunderclap <unclear>angada-</unclear> destroys whichever direction it travels toward. During the day, it kills Brahmins, Kshatriyas, Vaishyas, and Shudras <note>the four traditional social classes</note> in the villages respectively, according to the watch of the day. || 136 || At sunset, it destroys the outcasts and others; and during the watches of the night, it destroys grain, spirits <term>paishāchān - demonic entities</term>, and horses respectively. || 137 ||

**Now, the characteristics of meteors and their results.**
The earth is filled with the movement of living beings and inhabited by fierce creatures. [A meteor is] a flaming, long, crooked fall, accompanied by a crackling sound like lightning. || 138 || Those that have small tails, look like fire, have two shadows, are two cubits long, and fall nearby [are specific types]. A star that moves horizontally, is white, red, or thin [is also noted]. || 139 || Meteors take various forms; those that are black and have tails
<margin>184</margin>
are extremely dreadful. || 140 ||

**Now, the results of the glowing of the horizons.**
If the glow of the horizons <term>digdāha - an atmospheric phenomenon where the sky appears to be on fire</term> is yellow in color, it kills the protector of the earth (the king). If it is the color of fire, it destroys one's own country, moving from the north through the various directions in order. || 141 || A smoky glow causes the loss of crops; a black glow makes the country desolate. In the East, it kills the king; in the Northeast and other intermediate directions, it kills in sequence || 142 || the Vaishyas, artisans, thieves, and horses...

### On the Effects of Earthquakes

An earthquake destroys the social classes <note>original: "varṇā", referring to the four castes</note> in order, depending on the watch of the day or night in which it occurs. An earthquake arising from the Wind Circle <term>Vāyu Maṇḍala: one of the four elemental divisions of the horizon in Sanskrit omenology</term> destroys kings, crops, water, and the lower classes. || 144 ||
One arising from the Fire Circle destroys those who eat well and those near water. One arising from Indra’s Circle <note>The East/Celestial circle</note> destroys kings and the people of the Gurjara country <note>Modern-day Gujarat</note>. One arising from Varuna’s Circle <note>The Water circle</note> destroys three kings and their countries. || 145 ||

### On the Omen of Dust Storms and Rain

When dust covers the villages and leaders, then the kings are affected. If the directions appear smoky or dim, there is a painful fear of diseases. || 146 || If dust covers the sky at sunrise for two or three days, then many diseases will arise; if it happens at night, it brings suffering to the king. || 147 || If it lasts for two nights, it brings poverty; if for three or four days, it is worse; if for five days, it is a terrible calamity, except during the winter season. || 148 ||

### On the "City of Gandharvas" and its Effects

The appearance of a "City of Gandharvas" <term>Gandharva Nagara: a complex mirage or Fata Morgana in the sky, often looking like celestial castles</term> destroys various things according to its color, starting with white. Starting from the East, it destroys kings, ministers, armies, and priests in succession. || 149 || If it appears with dust, it causes poverty; if it looks like weapons or fire, it brings fear.

### On the Timing of the Results of these Portents

The results of an earthquake occur within six months, as stated. In the case of a "burning of the directions" <note>an atmospheric glow</note>, unnatural phenomena, or the appearance of comets and thunderbolts, the result comes in three months. || 150 || In the case of a rain of dust or fire, the result is immediate; for other portents, it takes a year. || 151 ||

### On Unnatural Rain and its Effects

Rain out of season brings disease and pain; if the clouds persist for a week or if there is a rain of blood, it leads to poverty within a month.

### Now, the Exceptions to the Faults of Portents

Through their respective propitiatory rites <term>Shanti: rituals performed to avert evil or calm the gods</term> of great power, all faults are entirely destroyed. If a favorable omen is seen within seven days of a portent, it indicates that the sorrow will not be given. || 163 ||

### Now, the Summary of Portents

The need for propitiation arises from comets <note>original: "dhumaketu," literally "smoke-bannered"</note>, the burning of the quarters <note>an atmospheric phenomenon where the sky appears to be on fire</note>, earthquakes, thunderclaps from a cloudless sky, and the falling of meteors, among others. || 164 || In whatever country these great, terrifying portents arise, there will be suffering for the subjects and the destruction of the king. || 165 || Therefore, a propitiatory rite must be performed by everyone in the country, village, and city. Through such rites, the faults are destroyed; otherwise, a wonderful <note>here meaning "terrible" or "extraordinary"</note> calamity occurs. || 166 ||

### Now, the Result of Being Touched by a Crow

If a crow touches the head, it foretells poverty, death, or strife. If it touches the waist or the shoulder, it is proclaimed as a great fear, yet also as something desired <note>The text suggests a complex omen where fear is followed by a desired outcome</note>. || 167 || For women, a crow touching the head signifies the destruction of the husband or the son. || 168 ||

### Now, Occasions where Crow-Touch has No Fault

If one is under a tree, or if the crow is eating consecrated food, or due to the conjunction of certain diseases, the touch of a crow results in no fault. Why? Because in these cases, it is certainly peaceful. || 169 || If one witnesses crows mating, death occurs within six months. To pacify that fault, a wise person should perform the Crow-Propitiation rite. || 170 || If a pigeon, a crow, a house-lizard, or a chameleon should fall upon the head—

<margin>188</margin>
—then within six months, the death of that person or their master occurs. || 171 || Thus ends the description of the results of portents.

In the following seven situations, sneezing is proclaimed by the wise to be auspicious: taking medicine, studying, debating, mounting a vehicle, lying down to sleep, eating, and sowing seeds—

### Now, the Chapter on Prices (Argha Kanda)

First, the consideration of the seven lords of the year, such as the King of the Year. In the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, in the bright fortnight, on whichever day of the week the first lunar day <term>Pratipat</term> falls, that day's ruling planet becomes the King of that year; the results of the year follow from this. || 175 || The day on which the Sun enters Aries <term>Mesha</term> is called the Minister. When the Sun enters Sagittarius, that lord is known as the Lord of Grain; when in the constellation Ardra, he is the Lord of Clouds (Rain). || 176 || The Lord of Juices <note>Liquids/Sap</note> is determined when the Sun is in Libra, and the Lord of Crops when in Cancer. When the Sun enters Capricorn, the wise remember the Lord of Gold, Gems, and Wealth. || 177 ||

### Now, the Calculation of the Twenty Units (Vimshopaka)

Multiply the Shaka era year by 3, divide by 7, and place the quotient in separate places. The remainder, combined with 2 parts and 5 parts, applies to the year. || 178 || Taking the quotient as the Shaka year, the process should be performed as stated. Then these results arise in sequence: 2. Grain, 3. Grass, 4. Cold, 5. Hunger, 6. Wind, 7. Fire, 8. Destruction, and 9. War. || 179 ||

### Now, the Total Production, Virtue, and Truth

The sum of the nine factors of the year <note>the lords mentioned above</note> multiplied by ten and added together: the resulting value indicates "Excellent Production." || 180 || Half of that represents Virtue and Truth. || 180 ||

### Now, the Calculation of Sudha (Nectar/Abundance)

One who knows the principles should multiply the Shaka year by seven and divide by nine; the quotient...

# || Determining the Shalabha and Others ||

Divide the Shaka year by seven 7, nine 9, and eight 8 <note>The original text uses "Shaila" (mountain/7), "Dyo" (sky/9), and "Hrit" (8) as numerical code words</note>. Multiply the remainder by two 2 and divide by three 3. The results, in order as before, indicate: 1. Shalabha <note>Locusts/Grasshoppers</note>, 2. Mrichaka <note>Small insects or earth-dwellers</note>, 3. Daiva <note>Divine/Providential events</note>, 4. Pita <note>Yellow/Jaundice or bile</note>, 5. Tamra <note>Copper/Heat</note>, 6. Chanchaka <note>Unsteady/Fickle events</note>, and 7. The arrival of an enemy army. These should be known in sequence. || 181 ||

# Now, Determining the Birth of Living Beings ||

Take the Shaka year and multiply it separately by 517, 12, and 11 <note>original: vārinigaurakaikīśe</note>. Divide the results equally; if the remainder is multiplied by 2 and increased by 5, the sequence is as follows: || 182 || 1. Udvijah <note>Plants/Sprouting from the earth</note>, 2. Svedaja <note>Born from sweat/moisture, like insects</note>, 3. Jarayu <note>Born from a womb/placenta, like mammals</note>, and 4. Andaja <note>Born from an egg, like birds or reptiles</note>. || 183 ||

# Now, the Measurement of the Yugas (Ages) ||

Four hundred thousand joined with twenty-two thousand <note>432,000</note> is the duration of the years of the Kali Yuga, as declared by the ancient great seers. || 184 || The measurements of the Ages, beginning with Krita, are produced in sequence: the measurement of the Kali Age is multiplied respectively by four 4, three 3, and two 2. || 185 ||

->Measurement of Krita: 1,728,000 | Measurement of Treta: 1,296,000<-
->Measurement of Dvapara: 864,000 | Measurement of Kali: 432,000 | Total Measurement of a Maha-Yuga: 4,320,000 ||<-

# Now, the Elapsed Years of the Kali Age ||

When the Shaka year is added to 3,172 <note>original: khaga-shailendu-rama</note>, those are the years of the Kali Age that have passed. Subtracting that from the total measurement of the Kali Age gives the remaining years of the Kali Age. || 186 ||

# Now, Determining the State of Dharma ||

Add the remaining years of the Kali Age to 490,000 <note>original: khābhrakhābhraṃ kavēdai</note>. Divide this sum by the total measurement of the Kali Age; the quotient represents the units of Dharma <note>Righteousness/Virtue</note>. || 187 ||

# Now, Another Method for Knowing Dharma ||

<margin>189</margin>
Take half of the Shaka year and add the number of Jupiter's signs <note>Zodiac position of Jupiter</note>; divide this by the Shaka year. The result is the Merit (Punya); that which is less than twenty is said to be the Sin (Papa). || 188 ||

### Now, the Constant Numbers for Calculating Income and Expenditure

> The constants for the planets starting from the Sun are to be known in order as: Flavors (6), Lunar days (15), Elephants (8), Mountains and Moon (13), Nine and Moon (19), and similarly (14), Heaven (21), and the Directions (10). || 188 ||
<note>In Sanskrit numerical notation (Bhuta-sankhya), words represent numbers: Flavors = 6, Elephants = 8, Mountains = 7, etc.</note>

### Now, the Income and Expenditure for Signs starting with Aries

> Take the constant of the lord of the year and the constant of your own zodiac sign's lord; add them together. Multiply by three (3), add the number of the day of the week (5), and divide by the lunar days (15/30). The remainder represents the Income (Aya). || 200 ||
> Multiply that result by three (3), add five (5), and divide by fifteen (15). The remainders are the Expenditures (Vyaya). Thus, the wise have described them for the zodiac signs in sequence. || 201 ||

### Now, Knowledge of Abundance and Famine from the Shaka Era

> Multiply the Shaka year <note>The Shaka era is a historical Hindu calendar era beginning in 78 CE.</note> by three (3), add the day of the week (5), and divide by seven (7). Based on the remainder:
> (1) Little, (2) Abundance, (3) Famine, (4) Great Abundance. || 202 ||
> (5) High prices/Dearness, (6) Equality/Stability, (0/7) Anxiety and disease are to be known. || 203 ||

### Now, Knowledge of Abundance from the Samvatsara

> Multiply the Samvatsara <note>The specific year name in the 60-year Jovian cycle.</note> by three (3), add five (5), and divide by seven (7).
> If the remainder is four (4), two (2), or three (3), there will be vast abundance. || 204 ||
> If the remainder is three (3), five (5), four (4), six (6), or one (1), the result is middling. If the remainder is zero, a "Raurava" <note>Literally 'dreadful' or 'hellish'; implies extreme drought or catastrophe.</note> is to be known by the wise as the fruit of the year. || 205 ||

### Now, Another Method from the Samvatsara

> Multiply the Samvatsara by two (2), subtract three (3), and divide by seven (7).
> If the remainder is five (5) or two (2), there will be abundance in the year. || 206 ||
> If it is four (4) or one (1), famine is to be known. If zero, then "Raurava." If the remainder is six (6) or three (3), the result is middling or half-favorable, according to the wise. || 207 ||

### The Results of the Festival of Lights <note>original: "Deepa Malika"; refers to Diwali</note>
If the festival of Diwali falls on a day ruled by a malefic planet, or during the *Ayushman* yoga or the *Swati* nakshatra in the month of Kartik, there will be suffering for the subjects and disturbances such as wars between kings. || 212 ||

### The Omen of the First Day of Jyeshtha
On the first day of the bright half of the month of Jyeshtha <note>May-June</note>, one should observe if it falls on a Monday, Thursday, or Friday. || 213 ||

### The Omen of the Second and Ninth Days of Ashadha
In the bright half of the month of Ashadha <note>June-July</note>, if the second and ninth days fall on a Monday, Thursday, or Friday, there will be excellent rain and prosperity. || 214 || If these days fall on a Wednesday, conditions will be average; on a Sunday, there will be heat; on a Tuesday, rain will not occur. If they fall on a Saturday, there will be suffering for the people, famine, and disease. || 215 ||

### The Results of the Transit of Saturn
When Saturn is in Gemini, there is suffering in Gujarat <note>original: "Gurjara"</note> and stability in Prabhasa. When in the sign of the Twins <note>Gemini</note>, it affects the desert regions; when in Cancer, it affects the region of Kashmir. || 216 || When in Leo, it affects Indraprastha <note>modern-day Delhi</note>; when in Virgo, it affects Malwa. When Saturn is in the three signs starting with Libra <note>Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius</note>, there is famine and suffering for the people on earth.
<margin>192</margin> There is plenty when Saturn is in Capricorn or Aquarius, but when it enters Pisces, there is a destruction of all subjects. || 217 ||

## Now, the Diagram of Saturn according to its Transit
One should arrange the constellations of Saturn starting from the first: three in the face, which brings victory. Then six in the right arm, and thirteen in the feet, which causes wandering. || 222 || Four in the left arm brings disease; five situated in the heart brings wealth <note>original: "Shri"</note>. With three in the head, one gains a kingdom; with two in the eyes, there is happiness. || 223 || With the remaining in the private parts, death occurs. Such is the sequential fruit of the Diagram of Saturn <note>Shani Chakra</note>. || 223 ||

### Now, the Knowledge of Moonrise to Determine Monthly Price Increases
In the upper section, the cells for the calculation of Rahu <note>the north node of the moon, often associated with eclipses</note> are to be known. In the horizontal section, the cells for the zodiac signs of the Sun are to be known. || 229 || In that chart, the *ghatikas* <term>ghatika: a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> of the Sun and Rahu are proclaimed. From the ten *ghatikas* mentioned, sixty remain, and the remaining *ghatikas* are added accordingly. || 230 || By those *ghatikas* of the day's duration, if the *ghatikas* of Rahu and the Sun are greater, then the visibility of the Moon is achieved. || 231 ||

### Now, the Elevation of the Moon's Horns and its Results
When the Moon rises in Pisces or Aries with its southern horn elevated, it is considered auspicious. It has equal horns in Taurus and Aquarius, and in the remaining signs, the northern horn is elevated. || 232 || If the southern horn is elevated, there will be distress for four months; if the horns are equal, there is abundance; and if the northern horn is elevated, there will be prosperity and good harvests. || 233 || If the Moon appears in the shape of a spear <note>a sharp, pointed crescent</note>, there is great fear and loud lamentation. || 234 ||

### Now, the Results of the Moon Rising to the Left or Right of the Sun
If, on the second day of the bright fortnight, the Moon rises to the left of the Sun, then the entire month will be auspicious. If it rises to the right, it is not auspicious. || 235 || If the Moonrise occurs in a "Great" constellation, grain will be affordable; in a "Low" constellation, grain will be expensive; and in an "Even" constellation, prices will be moderate. || 236 ||

### Now, the Results of the New Moon in Each Month
If the New Moon falls on an auspicious day <note>Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday</note>, there will be abundance and happiness for the people. If it falls on an inauspicious day <note>Sunday, Tuesday, or Saturday</note>, there will be suffering and high prices for grain. || 237 ||

### Now, the Result of Five Occurrences of a Day in One Month
If, in a month starting from the bright fortnight, there are five occurrences of a day ruled by a malefic planet <note>such as Mars or Saturn</note>, there will be oppression of the people and great dearness of grain.

If an inauspicious planet is in the 7th house, the <insert>already</insert> grown crop will be destroyed. || 244 || If an inauspicious planet is in the 7th or 6th house, the production will be very meager. In this same way, the summer grain should be judged during the Scorpio Ingress.

### Knowledge of Grain Production and Rainfall through Fruits on Trees and Other Signs

A great rain is produced when there are many flowers on the trees. Where there is very little rain, famine and sugarcane will increase. || 251 || From the *Madhuka* <note>Butter tree (Madhuca longifolia)</note> flowers, there will be many goats; from the fruits of the *Vata* <note>Banyan tree</note>, there will be barley. From the *Naga-Nimba* <note>Ironwood or a variety of Neem</note>, there will be prosperity; from the *Sala* trees, there will be rice. || 252 || From the *Ashvattha* <note>Sacred Fig</note>, there will be abundant grain; from the *Arjuna* tree, there will be abundant water. From Mangoes, there will be safety; from *Palasha* <note>Flame of the Forest</note>, there will be *Kodrava* <note>Kodo millet</note>, goats, and sesame. || 253 || From *Khadira* <note>Catechu tree</note>, there will be famine; from *Kutaja* <note>Conessi tree</note>, diseases are proclaimed. || 254 ||

### Examination of the Wind on the Full Moon Day of the Month of Ashadha

On the Full Moon of the month of *Ashadha* <note>June-July</note>, whether at twilight, day, or night: if the wind blows from the East, there will be rain and excellent grain production. || 255 || If from the Southeast, there will be little rain, fear of fire, and high prices. If from the South, there will be a lack of rain, suffering for the subjects, and high prices. If from the Southwest, there will be a terrible, heavy famine and no rain. || 256 || If from the West, there will be <margin>266</margin> great rain, but the grain production will be average. If from the Northwest, the wind will be fierce, and the rain and grain will be average. || 257 || Abundant grain—

...[if the wind blows from] the north, there will be inauspicious rain <note>original: "ashubha-vrishti"; likely meaning irregular or poorly timed rain</note>, yet the people will remain happy. If it blows from the northeast, there will be an abundance of crops, and the people will be wealthy and content. 258

<margin>Muhurtaganapati 187</margin> **Now, the weighing of grain and other goods on the Full Moon of Ashadha <note>Ashadha is a lunar month falling in June–July, marking the onset of the monsoon in India.</note>:** On the evening of the Ashadha Full Moon, one should separately weigh all types of grain, as well as water and other liquids, using the standard measure of a *Drona* <term>Drona: an ancient Indian unit of volume or weight used for grain</term>. 259 After consecrating these items with the appropriate sacred chants <note>mantras</note>, the priest should let them sit undisturbed through the night. In the morning, the astrologer <note>original: "daivavit," one who knows the divine will through stars</note> should weigh them again using the same measure. 260 If the weight has increased, there will be an increase in prosperity; if it remains the same, conditions will be average; and if the weight has decreased, there will be a loss of that particular commodity. 261

**Now, the examination of the winds during Holika <note>The Holi festival, occurring at the spring equinox.</note>:** If the wind blows from the East during the time of Holi, there will be happiness for the King and his subjects. If it blows from the South or Southwest, there will be famine and the displacement of people. 262 If it blows from the West or the North, there will be plenty of food and the subjects will be happy. If it blows from the Southeast, there is a fear of fire; if from the Northwest, there will be high winds and storms. 263

**Now, the knowledge of the "Cloud-Embryo" <term>Megha-garbha: the theory that clouds "conceive" rain months before they actually release it</term> for the purpose of knowing future rainfall:** The formation of the cloud-embryo should be observed starting from the bright half of the month of Margashirsha <note>November–December</note>, indicated by frost, halos around the sun or moon, or the colors of the twilight sky. 264 In the month of Pausha <note>December–January</note>, it is known by extreme cold; in Magha <note>January–February</note>, by the appearance of evening clouds and wind; in Phalguna <note>February–March</note>, by a copper-colored sun, glossy clouds, and harsh winds. 265 In Chaitra <note>March–April</note>, it is known by halos, wind, or streaks of clouds. 266

**Now, differing opinions of some authorities:**

<margin>185</margin> Some teachers describe the signs of the cloud-embryo in the months starting from Margashirsha based on the sound of thunder, the movement of the wind, the falling of rain, or the appearance of lightning. <margin>186</margin> 267

**Now, the signs of a favorable cloud-embryo:** Those clouds that are white or blue like the eggs of a bird, and which do not leak water prematurely... <note>The text breaks off here describing the visual characteristics of healthy rain-bearing clouds.</note>

...and those clouds, born from the North-East <note>original: "udagīśāna"</note>, are the first-born. **(268)** Those "conceptions" <note>the embryonic state of rain clouds</note> reach full maturity through lightning, wind, and the thundering of clouds. Those clouds arising from the *Ashadha* month <note>June-July</note> and those originating in the *Vishakha* constellation <note>Alpha Librae</note> result in abundant water. **(269)**

### On the Knowledge of Rain through the Conception of Clouds
From the start of the cloud's conception, rain will occur after twenty fortnights <gloss>13</gloss> <note>The gloss "13" may refer to a specific calculation or variant reading of the lunar cycles</note>. If the conception occurs during the night, the rain is remembered to fall during the day; if the conception occurs during the day, the rain falls at night. **(270)**

### Another Method for Knowing Rain from the Conception
Starting from the eleventh day of the dark fortnight of the month of *Magha* <note>January-February</note>, and continuing for twelve days, if there is lightning, wind, and cloud-thunder, **(271)** then rain will occur sequentially when the Sun enters the constellations starting with *Rohini* <note>Aldebaran</note>.

### Yet Another Method for Knowing Cloud Conception and Rainfall
When the Sun enters the *Mula* constellation <note>Lambda Scorpii</note>, and for ten days from that point onward, wherever the "conception" occurs through lightning and clouds on a particular day, rain will surely be produced on the corresponding day when the Sun enters the *Ardra* constellation <note>Betelgeuse</note> and those following it. **(272)**

### Another Method
From the constellation *Mula* to *Bharani*—during these eleven specific days—if a conception occurs through clouds and other signs during the month of *Pausha* <note>December-January</note>, **(273)** then rain will produced in sequence when the Sun reaches *Ardra*. **(274)**

### Another Method
If during the ten constellations starting from *Ashwini* <note>Beta Arietis</note> in the month of *Chaitra* <note>March-April</note> there are clouds and such, then the conception will result in rain starting from the *Ardra* period in sequence. **(275)** Or it should be known similarly starting from the day the Sun enters Aries <note>original: "Mesha-arka"</note>. **(276)**

### The Results of Winds during the Four Days Starting from the Eighth of the Bright Fortnight of Jyeshtha

On the eighth day of the bright fortnight of Jyeshtha, and during the following four days: if there are pleasant winds, there will be rain in Shravana and the following months respectively. || 281 ||

### On the Lack of Rain in Shravana if it Rains during Swati and other Constellations

If it rains during the four constellations starting from Swati <term>Swati: the star Arcturus</term> in the month of Jyeshtha, then there will be no rain in Shravana and the subsequent months respectively in those specific areas. || 282 ||

### The Results of the Second Day of Ashadha and the Following Three Days

If it rains on the second day of the bright fortnight of the month of Ashadha; or if on the third day there is a wind from the front <note>the East</note> or a cloud situated in the eastern quarter; || 283 || or if on the fourth day there is a cloud in the East and the wind is from the South; or if on the fifth day there is a cloud in the eastern direction: then from the month of Shravana onwards, || 284 || a good and certain rain should be known to occur for the four months in succession. If it rains continuously during these four specific days, || 285 || then there will be excessive rain <term>ativrishti: floods or damaging over-abundance of rain</term> and the destruction of grain will occur. If the wind comes from the South or the West, a famine is said to be coming. || 286 || If on the third and fifth days there is water <insert>rain</insert> and the wind is from the East,
<margin>199</margin> then there will be a great production of grain and the best kind of rainfall. || 287 ||

### Now, the results of rain when the Sun is in Revati and other Nakshatras <note>Lunar mansions or star clusters</note>:

If it rains while the Sun is in **Revati**, there will be heat and distress; if in **Ashvini**, the grain will be destroyed. In **Bharani**, there is a dual effect <note>likely meaning both rain and heat</note>, and if the Sun is in **Krittika**, there may be no rain at all. || 289 ||

If there is thunder in **Rohini**, rain should be expected after two months. When there is no rain in **Mrigashirsha**, there will later be a great abundance of water. || 290 ||

### Now, the results of rain on the day of Sankranti <note>The Sun's transit into a new zodiac sign</note>:

If it rains on the day of Sankranti in the months of **Margashirsha** <note>Nov-Dec</note> or **Kartika** <note>Oct-Nov</note>, the year's harvest is considered mediocre. However, rain in the month of **Pausha** <note>Dec-Jan</note> is excellent. || 291 ||

There will be abundance and good rain if it rains in the four months starting from **Phalguna** <note>Feb-March</note>. Disease occurs if it rains in **Ashadha** <note>June-July</note>, but rain in **Shravana** <note>July-August</note> is the best. In **Ardra** <note>a specific solar period</note>, rain brings disease, while in **Ashvina** <note>Sept-Oct</note>, it brings happiness. || 292 ||

### Now, the results of the Crow’s Nest:

If a crow’s nest is built to the **West** of one's house, there will be excellent rain. If to the **North**, there is fear; in the **Middle**, it is average; and in the **Southwest**, it is spread by the wind. || 293 ||

There is little rain if the nest is in the **Southeast**. If built in the **Northwest** <note>original: "Shive-nile", the direction of Vayu/Shiva</note>, it is average. If built at the very **top of a tree**, heavy rain is foretold. || 294 ||

If nests are found in **all directions**, it indicates a great and terrible fear. If the nest is made of **mixed scraps** <note>original: "Sankarātake", likely meaning rags or thorny materials</note>, a loss of wealth will certainly occur. || 295 ||

Some say that if the nest is situated in the **East, North, or West**, there will be good rain. <margin>200</margin> || 296 ||

If the eggs of the Lapwing are placed on **high ground**, then excellent rain will occur...

### The Results of Planets Residing in These Nadis

Now, the results when planets are situated in these Nadis are described: When two or more planets—whether they be benefics <note>gentle planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> or malefic <note>harsh planets like Mars or Saturn</note>—occupy the same Nadi, the wise astrologer should predict the results, whether good or bad, based on the nature of that specific Nadi. || 309 ||

Planets in the **Fierce Nadi** <note>Chandanadi</note> cause great gales; in the **Wind Nadi** <note>Vatanadi</note>, they cause breezy winds. Situated in the **Fire Nadi** <note>Vahninadi</note>, they cause heat and burning; in the **Mild Nadi** <note>Saumyanadi</note>, they produce great clouds. || 310 || In the **Water-Stream Nadi** <note>Neeranadi</note>, there is a shower of water; in the **Water Nadi** <note>Jalanadi</note>, there is rain. In the **Nectar Nadi** <note>Amritanadi</note>, the celestial wanderers <note>planets</note> produce heavy, torrential rain. || 311 ||

Even a single planet staying in its own Nadi bestows the specific fruit of that Nadi. However, Mars, when situated in any of the Nadis, produces the specific effects born of that Nadi <note>Mars acts as an intensifier here</note>. || 312 ||

When the Sun enters the constellation Ardra and subsequent stars, the results are told based on the unity of the Nadis. On the day when malefic and benefic planets are situated in the same Nadi, || 313 || and are joined by the Moon in a mixed state, there will be excellent rain on that day. If they are in the divisions of watery signs, they produce rain for as long as || 314 || the Moon remains in those watery divisions; if the Moon is alone, it gives only scanty rain. ### Now, the knowledge of rain based on the gendered union of constellations during the Sun's entry into Ardra and others.

### Now, the Signs of Immediate Rain During the Monsoon Season

The sky takes on the color of a crow's egg or possesses the brilliance of a parrot's wing. Clouds appear at the edges of the horizon, resembling mountains or collyrium <note>dark eye-salve or mascara</note>. [342] They take the shapes of aquatic animals or move toward the left. Mountains appear like heaps of black pigment, and peacocks begin their dances with misty breath. [343] Clouds that look like the eyes of a parrot or the color of honey are full of water. Halos appearing close around the Moon bring swift rain. [344] Children build dams in the dirt as if playing with water; fish jump out of the water; and frogs croak together in the pools. [345] Serpents mate, and they climb trees or give birth. Flies appear in great numbers, and ants carry their eggs. [346]

### Concerning the Omens of Divine Listening

First, having worshipped the Lord of the Ganas <note>Ganesha, the elephant-headed deity who removes obstacles</note> and having informed him of the task at hand, one should set out carrying a vessel of water along with unbroken rice and fruit. [352]

"O Divine Listening! O Great Lakshmi! You who dwell even in the house of the outcaste <note>original: "Chandala"; this refers to the goddess's omnipresence, even in socially marginalized spaces</note>! Tell me the truth regarding the matter I am contemplating, O Goddess of the Gods! [353] Everything is known by you, especially the works of Indra." Having spoken thus, one should listen for a clear voice consisting of three sentences. [354] After pouring the water there, one should return to their own home. The result should be understood truly according to the meaning of those spoken words. [355]

**Concerning the Specific Days of the Week:**
On Sunday, one should go to the house of an outcaste woman; on Monday, to the house of a barber; on Tuesday, to the house of a washerwoman; on Wednesday, to the house of a merchant; [356] on Thursday, to the house of a Brahmin woman; on Friday, to the house of another's wife; and on Saturday, to the door of a servant girl to listen for the auspicious divine voice. [357]

**Concerning the Raghuvamsha and Other Texts:**
Regarding the omens found in the *Raghuvamsha* <note>The famous Sanskrit epic poem by Kalidasa</note> or the *Durga-yami-gama*, one should worship the book with flowers, unbroken rice, and fruits. [358] Having placed it on a Saturday, one should look at it early on Sunday morning, having a young girl open the pages at random...
<margin>206</margin>

It also mentions using classical literary texts like the Raghuvamsha as bibliomancy tools.</summary>

### The Examination of Physical Traits <note>original: "Samudrika Pariksha," the traditional Indian study of physiognomy and palmistry</note>

A man is considered fortunate if he possesses a broad forehead, curved eyes, and a wide chest, arms, hands, and feet. His head should be well-rounded, his navel deep, and his nose prominent. || 361 || His teeth and skin should be glossy; his hair should be dark, lustrous, and curled. The corners of his eyes should be reddish, and the eyes themselves should be large with dark pupils. || 362 || The hair on his body should turn to the right <note>clockwise whorls are traditionally considered lucky</note>, and his voice should be smooth and deep. One whose urine is frothy and who is not of very small build is blessed. || 363 || A man who resembles his mother is fortunate, as is a woman who resembles her father. Reddish lips, nails, and soles of the hands and feet are auspicious signs. || 364 || He who is generous, courageous, profound, ambitious, and fond of fame <unclear>..</unclear>; who has a pleasant face, takes pride in his lineage, and speaks the pleasant truth; || 365 || who is ethical, follows the words of his teacher, is pure, capable, and fears committing sin—a man endowed with such qualities becomes truly lucky. || 366 || The line that goes toward the index finger <term>Tarjani: the forefinger</term> horizontally is known as the line of Longevity. The horizontal line between the index finger and the thumb is known as the line of Prosperity. || 367 || The line that joins at the base of the Prosperity line from the wrist <term>Mani Bandha: the bracelet lines on the wrist</term> is called the Line of the Paternal Lineage; if it is unbroken, it grants happiness in the home.
