# Page 212

## The Jewel of this Work

...shines forth. || 9 || From this [source], this jewel of a book was composed, which brings joy to all people. It is illuminated by the light of astrology <note>original: Jyotir-diptam</note> and organized through various chapters and terrestrial divisions. This "Essence of the Best" <note>original: Saroddharam</note> was beautifully strung together with excellent syllables <note>The author uses a play on words: 'suvarnaih' means both 'beautiful letters' and 'gold'</note> by the learned Ganapati. May the virtuous and the entirety of the learned ones find fulfillment through the one who created this. || 10 ||

### Description of the Author’s Family Lineage

Now begins the description of the author's family lineage. Among those of the Gurjara <note>referring to the Gujarat region</note> land, accompanied by thousands of divine twice-born <note>term: twice-born (dvija); a member of the three upper castes of Hindu society who has undergone the sacred thread ceremony</note> families, there was one of virtuous character named Ravala. He was honored by all kings and possessed praiseworthy fortune. Belonging to the lineage <note>term: gotra; a patrilineal clan descending from a common rishi or sage</note> of Bharadvaja, he achieved stainless fame. Devoted to the Supreme Reality, he was the most eminent and celebrated Sage among Sages <note>original: Brahmarshi</note>, appearing like the venerable Lord Vashistha himself. || 11 ||

In the ocean of the famous, overflowing light of the autumn's clear moon, the ten directions became brilliant through the weight of his glory. To him was born a son, Yashodhara, the upholder of the family; wise men, out of respect for his auspiciousness, always joyfully spoke of him as a vision of the protector. || 12 ||

His son was Ramadasa, an abode for those who have seen the furthest reaches of the Vedas. By his words of prophetic instruction <note>original: prashnadesha-vagbhih; likely referring to horary astrology or divination</note>, he brought the entire circle of kings under his influence. His feet were eternally illuminated by the lamps of the crest-jewels of the earthly rulers <note>A poetic way of saying kings bowed at his feet, their jeweled crowns reflecting near his toes</note>. Shining with knowledge, when his body reached its full term, he went to Kashi <note>modern-day Varanasi, the sacred city where dying is believed to grant liberation</note> and attained liberation. || 13 ||

His son was like the moon rising from the ocean: a listener to the sacred texts, one who maintained the sacrificial fires <note>original: ahitagni</note>, ever peaceful, self-controlled, and of noble intellect. He was a leader in power attained through great penance. Having reached Kashi, he indeed attained liberation; he was Harishankara, a knower of the principles of astrology <note>original: daivajnata-tattva-vit</note>, a knower of the Self, a great sage whose name truly reflected the non-dual reality. || 14 ||

His son, proficient in all the arts of the scriptures, an astrologer <note>original: Jyotirvid</note>...

Through the grace of my Guru, a knower of the Vedas, <note>The Vedas are the oldest sacred scriptures of Hinduism; "knower of the Vedas" implies a teacher of the highest spiritual and intellectual caliber.</note> and having composed this book titled *Muhūrta Gaṇapati* <note>The title translates to "The Lord of Auspicious Moments."</note> with devotion, I, who am known as Gaṇapati, offer it at the feet of Lord Shiva. — 15

<margin>213</margin>
Because it contains a vast multitude of auspicious moments <term>Muhūrta: a traditional unit of time, roughly 48 minutes, used here to mean "auspicious timing" for rituals</term> and because it removes obstacles in all undertakings, this work is equal to the god Gaṇeśa himself. Since it was created by a twice-born <note>Sanskrit: Dvija; referring to the Brahmin or priestly class</note> named Gaṇapati, this other "Muhūrta Gaṇapati" shines forth as a creation for the world. — 16

By considering the months, lunar days <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>, the Yoga, and the nectar-like movements of the Karana <note>Yoga and Karana are two of the five elements of the Panchāṅga or almanac</note>, this work shines as something to be honored during the sacred thread ceremony <term>Mauñjī: the Upanayana or initiation ritual</term>, marriages, house-warmings, and other auspicious decorations. — 17

Having thoroughly reflected upon the opinions of the learned and the works of great scholars—such as the *Jyotirvidābharaṇa*, the auspicious *Vasantarāja*, the *Jyotir-nibandha*, the *Gana-maṇḍana*, and the *Ratnamālā*; as well as the books beginning with the word 'Muhūrta' such as *Muhūrta Tattva*, *Muhūrta Dīpa*, *Muhūrta Mārtanda*, *Muhūrta Cintāmaṇi*, and the *Kalpavṛkṣa* <note>These are all major historical treatises on Sanskrit astronomy and astrology</note> — 18

<margin>213</margin>
—this book titled *Muhūrta Gaṇapati* has been composed in verses with well-known and easily understood meanings. It should be cherished with love in the temple of their own hearts by those who recognize true merit. — 19

Whatever secrets from other sacred traditions <term>Āgama: traditional doctrine or scripture</term> are contained herein have been revealed out of compassion for good students. This knowledge should never be given to one who is envious, crooked, atheistic, or devoid of the desire to serve and learn. — 20

<margin>213</margin>
With the years as its crest-ornament, the Sun and Moon as its eyes, the months as its limbs, and the conjunctions and transits <term>Saṅkrānti: the passage of the Sun from one zodiac sign to another</term> as its garland; adorned with the jewels of rituals like the sacred thread and marriage, and with the chapters on travel and miscellaneous matters as its flowers and sacred grass—this work stands complete. — 21

Again and again...

<meta>This page serves as the colophon (concluding section) of the Sanmuhurtavali, a treatise on elective astrology. It follows the technical instructions for determining auspicious times for rituals and construction.</meta>

# The Garland of Auspicious Moments

->**The Conclusion of the Work**<-

He who has achieved success in the practices of hospitality, victory, and bliss; who is honored for the rituals of establishing sacrificial fires, consecrations, and birth ceremonies; who is the beloved of the sacraments <note>The sixteen Hindu rites of passage known as Sanskaras</note>; who is famous for his expertise in the beauty of architecture and temples, the movement of rays, and the debates regarding rain and wind—this Ganapati triumphs through his work, the *Garland of Auspicious Moments*, which begins with the coronation of kings. **(22)**

In the year 1742 of the era of King Vikramaditya <note>equivalent to 1685-1686 CE</note>, on the fifth day of the bright half of the month of Magha <note>known as Vasant Panchami, the festival of spring</note>, when the moon was in the eighth mansion, Ganapati—the son of the learned scholar Sri Harishankar, a maintainer of the sacred Vedic fires—joyfully completed this book after worshipping the Lord Shiva. **(23)**

May this final portion, endowed with the splendor of Shiva and providing the desired auspicious moments, spread throughout the worlds like the rising sun in the sky. **(24)**

I, Ganapati, born of the noble Brahmin Harishankar Raval, a maintainer of the sacred fires, having completed this excellent book, **(25)** offer it to Lord Shiva, the embodiment of pure consciousness, and pray for a long time: "May this auspicious work, the granter of boons, spread everywhere for the benefit of all." **(26)**

Now, he describes the further purpose for composing this book:
By his generosity, he equals the kings Vikramaditya, Bhoja, and Karna; by his valor, he equals Prithu and Arjuna; by his depth, he equals the lord of rivers <note>the Ocean</note>; and by his policy, he equals the preceptor of the gods <note>Brihaspati</note>. In his devotion to the Lord of Gauri <note>Shiva</note>, he reaches a weight equal to the splendor of the sun, this Lord Lakshmirama, the protector of a vast army. **(27)**

He who is highly esteemed through the affection of the illustrious King Manohar of Jodhpur; who is skilled in the art of poetry and the path of righteousness; and who is a knower of the meaning of all the scriptures...

214
</margin>

While she <note>referring to the Goddess of Speech or Wisdom</note> sits there with curiosity, for the pleasure of the learned and by the inspiration of those before me, let this easily understood book be composed for the investigation of the <term>Jyotish Shastra</term> <note>The traditional Indian science of astronomy and astrology</note>. || 28 || Thus, having understood the prince's mind, for his immediate amusement, this excellent composition on the various <term>Muhurtas</term> <note>Auspicious time-measurements or moments for starting activities</note> was composed by Ganapati Ravala Sharma. || 29 || Just as the speech of the god Ganapati is the cause of union with Shiva, so too is the speech of this Ganapati, the son of Harishankara, in the city of Kashi <note>Modern-day Varanasi</note>. May this composition, named 'Muhurta Ganapati', manifest on this earth for the benefit of the people. || 30 || May the one who understands the essence of this book obtain long life, wisdom, fame, happiness, good fortune, imperishable rewards, their heart's desires, and even cattle and sons. || 31 || Thus ends the Chapter on the Ornamentation of the Book in the 'Muhurta Ganapati', composed by Ganapati, the son of the wise Ravala Harishankara—who was a performer of the <term>Chaturmasya</term> <note>A four-month seasonal sacrifice</note> and <term>Agnihotra</term> <note>A sacred fire ritual</note> sacrifices, and who attained the essence of the goals of human life.
